- **Making room** (`BridgeInventory.offer(id, n, { title, onTake, onDecline })`): puts the thing in if it fits (onTake at once, returns true); if the pack's full the Backpack opens with it on top — pick what makes room and Drop it here, Send it to the ship or Throw it away and the new thing takes its place (onTake), or Leave it (onDecline; Esc too). One at a time, the next waits; one that arrives during a dialog, a climb, the fishing panel or the sea chart waits till it's done. `BridgeInventory.give(id, n)` is the same, for rewards. Loot you walk over with a full pack stays on the ground and is offered (it used to vanish); the climbing routes' crystals and gifts, the old camp's crystals, Ilsa's maps and the whistle go through `give`. Anything else that adds to the pack should use one of them rather than `addItem` when losing the thing would hurt — a fish you've landed, a reward, a purchase.
- **Map** (`js/map.js`, BridgeMap): **Here** is the place you're in drawn small from its own scene render (the composite already in memory, layers and season grade and all, at 16 px a tile; anywhere else `full.png` fetched and shrunk, the last six kept), cropped to the world's `view`, night worlds lifted a little to read (Sylvara's dusk only a touch; Verdance and Hoarfell not at all — `mapLift`), with you on it and a marker for each thing worth finding: people (named once met, *Someone* before), shops and traders, the ways in and out (where they lead once you've been — a grey **?** before), your ship, shuttle and sloop, fishing spots, things to gather (ready or growing back), games, what's yours (chests, homes you own, the wardrobe), useful things (wells, notice boards, the tool shed) and the quest clues you've found, and where a quest you haven't begun starts. Markers come from the world's interactions (`classify`): the live ones where you stand, the world JSON plus what the JS adds as it loads (fishing, forage, outfitters, traders, chests) anywhere else; a door three tiles wide is one marker (two people you haven't met stay two *Someone*s). Point at or tap one for its name; the legend counts each kind and picks one to show alone (Esc puts them back). **All places** is the map of everything: a card per system — the Wayfarer, Lumar, Arcadia, Enigma, the Singularity, Verdance, Hoarfell, Sylvara (map.js `KINDS` / `PLANET` / `SYS_RX` and atlas.py `SYSTEMS`: a new system goes in all four) — (its planet drawn live by `js/orbit.js`, the Wayfarer a little galleon) with its places as a tree from where the shuttle sets down — which door leads where — Lumar's by island (those on your sea chart named, the rest counted). Places you've been are named and open their own map; a way on from one of them you haven't taken is "?" with what the door's called; anything further stays hidden. On Lumar there's a Sea chart button (C). The bridge's **chart desk** opens it.
- **The atlas** (`assets/atlas.json`, written by `python3 tools/bridge-art/atlas.py`): every world's system, island and ways out — `enter_world` / `leave_world` / `password_gate` from the world JSON plus the ones the JS makes (`EXTRA`: home doors, the club, the palace gate, the mine's lift, the chairlift, the climbing routes, the pistes and the Park, the Last Ridge, the ice door). The mine's three floor layouts are one place. **Re-run it after adding a world or a door.** Where you've been is the journal's first-arrival record (`BridgeQuests.visited()`, localStorage `bridge_log_seen`), plus the sea chart's islands and the nav chart's systems for older saves; the bridge always counts.
- **Journal** (`js/quests.js`): **Next up** first — the quests under way with a lead for each (a clue's `hint`) (the places their missing clues are, by name where you've been, *somewhere on Crimson Reach you haven't been* where not; a Map button opens that place), takings waiting, crops ready, a full pack, how many people here you haven't met, the ways on from here you haven't taken, the worlds you've never set foot on, and a tip for a new pilot. Then the quests (under way in full with their leads and a *Track* button, the rumours you've heard, finished folded up), the log, and the rest of what's yours (crew, businesses, the farm, the fleet, the curios, the Last Ridge, Hoarfell). Fish and the Collection have tabs of their own.
- **Fish** (`js/codex.js` `renderFish`): every fish in the codex's fish and sea-legend sets, the ones you've caught drawn by BridgeFishArt (crabs and pearls by their icon) with how many, the biggest and what it's worth, rarity in its colour; the rest as shadows with where to look (`SETS` hints) — legends included, so you know they're out there. The messages from bottles fold up under them. **Collection** is the codex's page (`BridgeCodex.render`).

- **Ledger** (`js/ledger.js`, BridgeLedger; L; its Today, Treasury and Standing orders sections are under *Running it from the office*): Beck asked for a business ledger — "all of the different businesses and things available, maybe even people to hire and have some of them blocked out… the status of things, what they're making, and where they are. If it needs you." It only reads the modules that keep the state. **Needs you**: a business with takings piled up (red when it's at its cap and has stopped earning), the holm's strongbox once your ships are home (`BridgeHolm.returns`), a battered hull, fish in a hold, ripe crops, dried-out rows when Tam isn't hired, eggs and milk waiting, a contract due today or tomorrow. **Businesses** (`BridgeBusiness.all()`): yours with what each earns an hour, its improvements, who runs it and what's waiting; ones in places you've been, for sale with the price; the rest *? ? ?* with the region they're in. **People you can hire**: the crew (people.json `crew` blocks) — hired (at their post on the bridge, and what they do for you), met (their wage, or *not ready to leave yet*), or a shadow with a region hint; the farm's hands by the day; the hands aboard your ships and where to find more. **Staff & payroll** (`staff()`; Beck: "good ways to manage the workers and see… how much you are paying and how much they are bringing in"): a head line — wages a day against what your businesses made today, and anything owed with a *Pay it* button (`BridgeBusiness.payAll`) — then everyone who works for you with their face: at each business (role, where, open or shut, what it took today, their wage a day, owed or missed paydays), the crew on the bridge and the hands aboard each ship (role, grade, what their grade does, wage a day). Tap anyone for their card (`act`): a business's person view (move or let go), a hand's card (move to another ship, pay off). **The captain's office** (below) is where the book lives aboard: its desk opens it, its staff board opens it on this page (`open({ focus: 'staff' })`) — the ledger works anywhere, phone included. `roster()` (everyone working for you, with how to draw their face and how they are) and `payroll()` (wages a day, made today, what's owed) are what this page and the office both read. **Your fleet** (where each lies or how long till she's home, hull, hold, fish, hands), **Your places** (homes, the farm, the holm and what's built), **Contracts**. Every row in a place you've been has a Map button.

### Storage, selling and packs (`js/storage.js`, BridgeStorage)
Beck wanted places to keep things, somewhere to sell more often, and bigger packs he could find.
- **Chests**: aboard the Wayfarer from the start, the **sea chest** on the bridge and the **storage locker** in your quarters (24 slots each; the locker's Decor locker button opens the old decor locker), kept in localStorage `bridge_storage`. More are furniture (`CHESTS`): the **crate** (9), the **old trunk** (18) and the **captain's strongbox** (36, on the trunk's sprite — `SHARED` in `tools/bridge-art/furniture_sprites.py` keeps it in `furniture.json`), bought from the Ship's Chandler or any home's furnishing shop (H) and put down in any place of yours — the bridge and quarters, your houses, the farmhouse, the holm's cottage. A furniture chest keeps its things on its own layout entry (`items`, saved with `bridge_homes`, so a partner visiting sees the same chest; js/home.js carries them when you move it and won't put away a chest with anything in it). Opening a chest (an interaction of type `chest`, registered with `BridgeInteractions.register`) shows its slots above your pack in the Backpack: click a stack to move it across (shift-click or right-click: one), Store your pack (not the hotbar), Add to stacks, Take all, or drag.
- **Selling**: specialists pay full price (people.json `sell` topics: Oro, Maud, Hob, Marlo…). **General traders** take anything with a value (items.js `value`) for less — the Ship's Chandler's quartermaster's hatch aboard (6/10), Joro's pawn counter (6/10), the fishmonger in Lumar's square, Pennyworth's in Brambleford and the guide's hut in Fellhaven (7/10), the Crownhaven market (3/4) — interactions given type `trader` as their worlds load (`TRADERS`; they keep what they sold and offer Buy and Sell your goods). Each opens the game's own sell sheet (`BridgeStorage.sell` → an interaction of type `sell`); the Backpack's quick sell works at any of them. Keys, maps and things with no value don't sell.
- **The Ship's Chandler** on the bridge (type `chandler`): deck fittings (js/catalog.js), chests, fish tanks (`BridgeAquarium.shop`, see *Homes and furniture*), packs (the Courier's Satchel and the Expedition Pack — `BridgeGear.SHOPS.wayfarer`), and selling. The Backpack names the next pack up and every outfitter that sells it.

### Interactions
`js/interactions.js` picks the target by proximity: anything within 1.5 tiles (the eight tiles round you, or the one you stand on), the one you face first, then the nearest; something two tiles ahead counts when a solid tile (a counter) stands between. No exact tile to stand on. The prompt is a small glass pill floating over the target — key cap, verb, name — built from the interaction's `prompt` (`"PRESS E TO TALK"` → *Talk*) and `label`; tap or click it to act. A soft pool of light marks the target's foot. Games get a small gold ▶ badge and exits a teal chevron; the one you're next to gives way to the pill. Clicking an object's body (up to two tiles above its tile) also uses it. A module can bring its own interaction type without a case in interactions.js: `BridgeInteractions.register(type, fn(inter), verb)` (chests, traders and the Chandler do). An interaction with an `action` goes to js/actions.js first (sit, sleep, view); the bridge's `deck` stations pass on through it to BridgeDeck.

### The two ships
Both are fantasy sci-fi, not NASA: the **Wayfarer** is a sky-galleon (indigo hull, gold wales, three masts of banded light-sails, a glowing keel fin, portholes and a stern castle) and the **skiff** an ivory-and-gold courier (bubble canopy, crescent wings, aether-ring nacelles with violet cores) — `assets_ship.mothership` / `assets_ship.shuttle`.
The **mothership** (bridge, quarters, hangar) jumps between systems from the nav chart (`js/starmap.js`: a pixel chart drawn in a native buffer, live planets, routes, ←/→ to pick, hold Enter/J/Space to charge and jump; the jump itself is `BridgeCine.jump` — the drive spooling with aether rings tightening round the hull, the leap (the ship stretching away into a white-out), the aether tunnel seen from the bridge through the gallery's arched windows (a per-pixel log-polar shader at half resolution, hoops and spiral light, dithered, its colour drifting violet → cyan → gold), dropping out over the destination, then the shuttle peeling off the bay for the planet; cuts are flashes (`dip: false` shots), each shot can fire a sound (`warp`, `leap`, `dropout`), Space/Esc/a click skips; jumping together, `opts.wing` puts the other galleon alongside — see *Playing together as a party*) and parks in orbit. **After a jump you go straight down**: `BridgeShip.descend` lands the shuttle on the world's pad — no stop on the bridge. The first time at a world the cutscene pauses on it with its name (`.cine-title`). The star map's *Stay aboard after the jump* (`bridge_stay_aboard`) keeps you on the bridge instead (the old *In orbit* card), to look out of the windows; every window on board draws the place below through `js/orbit.js` (`BridgeOrbit.draw` — per-pixel planets with a dithered terminator: Lumar's cloudy ocean, Arcadia's city-lit night side, Enigma's ringed giant, the Singularity, Verdance's green quilt of fields under two little moons, Hoarfell's ice). The **shuttle** flies you down: `js/ship.js` runs the flow (arrival card after a jump → lift off the hangar pad, seen from above in the world → a short fade → touch down on the world's pad, from above; "Return to ship" anywhere off the ship reverses it — no cutscene in between, by Beck's call: the top-down lift-off and landing are the good angles). A world with a landing pad has `"shuttle": {"spawn": [x, y]}` in its JSON and the parked shuttle as a scene decor layer (`with k.variant('shuttle', 'parked')`, default-on via the world JSON's `scene.layers`); while it flies the layer is switched off and its image drawn lifted with thrust, shadow and dust. The cutscenes (`js/cinematic.js`: the jump and the galleon) draw Blender sprites rendered side-on by `tools/bridge-art/cine_sprites.py` (`cinekit.CineKit`: the scene kit with a free camera, pitch and a sun; anchors such as engine exhausts land in `assets/cine/cine.json`) into a ~480 px native buffer scaled by a whole number. Between Lumar's docks you now sail the sloop yourself (see *Sailing the sloop yourself*); the galleon's passage on the open sea of `js/voyage.js` on autopilot (`BridgeVoyage.start(from, {auto: to})`) is the fallback: a route planned round the islands over the chart (A* on a coarse grid, coasts cost more, pulled straight and rounded), the ship casting off beside the island's moored galleon, running across the seas (each seam named as it's crossed) and gliding in to lie off the destination's berth, the camera leading the bow and settling to frame the island; a tap or Esc goes straight ashore. (`BridgeCine.sail`, the old side-on shot, is only a fallback now.)

### The title page (js/intro.js)
The site's root `/` serves the Bridge, so its start screen is Becknology's title. Beck: "the mothership in orbit over the planet it's parked at, with day and night following the real clock … but still keeping some level of that technology aspect".
- **The scene**: the Wayfarer (the cutscenes' `mothership` sprite, `BridgeCine.get`) drifting over the world she's parked at (`bridge_ship_location`; a ship that's never jumped shows Lumar). The planet is `BridgeOrbit.globe` — the windows' planets seen close: only what's on screen worked out per pixel, the axis tilted so the ground below sits at the place's latitude (`PLACES[*].lat`), the texture sampled through a 4×4 dither so magnified coastlines break into pixel clusters, the surface turning once in ~18 minutes, the air past the limb glowing (dithered) where the sun reaches it, a night side never quite black (`ambient`). **The sun follows the planet's clock**: `sun()` places it from `BridgeClock.sky` — the hour angle from the planet's day, a declination from its daylight share (the sunrise equation, `cos πD = −tan φ tan δ`) — so the terminator crosses the ground under the ship at that planet's sunrise and sunset, morning light from the east (right), a sunset band along it, the lights (`glow` in the planet's texture) coming on after dark. Wet weather (`BridgeWeather.at`) thickens the cloud; a storm flickers lightning on the night side. A little after sunset she's in the planet's shadow: her night copy (`prepShip`: the hull darkened and blued, lamps kept — portholes, the gallery, the keel and engine rings). Her aether rings idle, masthead strobes, red astern and green at the bowsprit. Parallax stars in three layers, the place's nebula (`BridgeCine.nebula`), a shooting star every 7–18 s, the skiff dropping out of the bay and diving for the surface every 40–70 s (its re-entry streak). Enigma is the ringed giant with the station, the Singularity its disc (no clock: ship time), Arcadia the night side with a crescent of day.
- **The technology**: BECKNOLOGY is the old ANSI-shadow ASCII drawn as pixels (`buildMark`: █ the face in four bands, the box-drawing shadow as 1-px lines from each cell's middle), in its own canvas at a whole number of device pixels per pixel; printed in row by row with a block cursor, then a breathing glow, scanlines (the last device row of each pixel darkened), a light sweep every ~8 s and a rare 120 ms glitch (a slice shifted, split cyan and magenta; both off for `prefers-reduced-motion`). A thin HUD frame with readouts (where she is — the longitude turning with the planet — and the planet's clock, season, weather and Eastern time); the ship's systems typed in as boot lines over the menu (a fault line if the records can't be reached).
- **The menu**: a pilot saved on this device gets **Continue as <name>** — their face (`BridgeLook.sheet` in what they wear, cropped like `BridgeLook.face`), where the ship is, coins (their `pilot_coins` row by id — not `BridgeProgression.getBalance`, which keeps the first pilot it resolves for the page's life), the planet's time and season — then *Switch pilot* and the sound (`BridgeAudio.cycle`); someone new gets **New pilot** and **Sign in** (js/identity.js opens that form straight away, `show({ form })`; its BACK, and the choice screen's, return to the title). Enter / Space take the chosen one (the first), ↑/↓ choose; a click or tap on the sky continues (someone new: the identity choice, as the old CLICK TO BEGIN did). **On the wire**: with a pilot saved, every 45 s while the title's up and in front, a look at the lobby's presence on `BridgeProgression.client()` — its own socket, never BridgeNet's channel — joined for a few seconds and left (so a title left open costs almost nothing in Realtime messages).
- **Continuing**: the pilot is looked up as the title opens (a failure that isn't *no such pilot* is asked again); Continue pushes in through the stern gallery (the bridge's windows; the composite scaled up round them, light rushing past, `warp`) to a flash held until the bridge has loaded, which then fades off it (`.ti-flash`). No such pilot → cleared and the identity screen, as before; the records out of reach → back on the title with a fault line, the pilot remembered. js/identity.js boards the same way after a new pilot or a sign-in (`BridgeIntro.board(then)`).
- **Drawing**: a native buffer ~640 px across (~560 on a phone), scaled by a whole number; main.js leaves its starfield out while the title covers (`BridgeIntro.covers()`), and the scene stays (dimmed) behind the identity forms. Light on CPU: after the first full draw the globe works out a band of rows a frame, the whole of it every 420 ms (the texture moves a texel every ~2 s at that spin); the rest is sprites and points (~2 ms a frame on desktop). Things that take a moment (the nebula, the planet's texture) are made a frame or two after the stars are up. `BridgeIntro.hide()` (every test skips the title with it) tears it all down. Layout: desktop — the wordmark top centre, readouts in the frame's corners, the menu low on the left, the planet's limb across the bottom third; a phone (≤ 760 px, or portrait) — the wordmark and one readout line up top, the ship mid-screen over a bigger world, the menu across the bottom over a scrim.
- Test: `world/title.js` (desktop and phone, with a pilot and without, by day and night, every place; Enter, a click, a tap, the records out of reach, New pilot / Sign in and back; screenshots `wide-*.png` and `phone-*.png` in `tools/tests/shots/title`).

### Landing sites
Every world has a logical place for the skiff, with its own touchdown in the world (`BridgeShip.drawWorld` styles by tileset); the Approach column below describes the old approach cutscenes, since removed:

| World | Site (world id) | Approach | Touchdown |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lumar | the east beach under Diggen's Point (`lumar`) — sea-glass ring, lantern posts, a beached rowboat | low over the night sea past the lighthouse | lift jets kicking up sand |
| Arcadia | Skyport 7, a rooftop pad (`arcadia-skypad`) — marshal's booth, fuel line, lift down to the Skywalk | dropping between towers through traffic lanes and rain | the pad's ring pulsing |
| Enigma | Docking Bay 3 (`enigma-dock`) — a hangar open to space behind a containment field, clamp cradle, the crew's gutted tug, hatch to the relay deck | in toward the dark station and the ringed giant, through the lit bay mouth | gliding in through the field, clamps reaching for the hull with tethers |
| Singularity | the old landing ring at the Brink (`singularity`) | the horizon swallowing the sky | trailing echoes of itself |
| Verdance | a gravel pad with a painted ring in the farmstead's meadow (`verdance`) | — | green dust off the grass |
| Hoarfell | a ploughed pad ringed with lamps below Fellhaven (`hoarfell`) | — | a burst of snow |
| Sylvara | the landing ring of mossy stone and glowing runes in Elderglade (`sylvara`) | — | green leaves stirred up |

A landing world has `"shuttle": {"spawn": [x, y]}`, the parked skiff as a default-on `shuttle` scene layer (`with k.variant('shuttle', 'parked')`, solid tiles marked outside the variant) and a `shuttle` interaction at the hatch; `LANDING` in `js/ship.js` maps each location to its site. Put the spawn where you can be seen — beside or in front of the parked skiff, never behind her (Diggen's Point's was behind her nose; it's beside her now). The dock's live parts (field, guide-light chase, things adrift, clamp positions, the giant beyond the mouth) come from its world JSON `dock` block, drawn by `js/worlds/enigma.js`.

### Big islands (Lumar)
Each Lumar island is a whole place to walk (≈56 × 40 tiles), built with `tools/bridge-art/assets_isle.py`: `mask()` unions noisy lobes into the island's shape (a C round a cove for Rose, a crescent town for Sapphire), `ground()` lays one continuous surface over it banded by a material function (paths, moor, meadow, ash, flagstones via `assets_town.island_ground` for towns) and can be sculpted (`height`: pools dug below the waterline fill with the sea's own water), `ridge()` raises cliffs with strata where the land climbs out of reach, and `assets_coast.coast()` takes the shore into the sea (`sheer` rects keep deep water right up to a quay wall; give one a fifth number, `soft`, and its seaward sides shelve into the shallows over that many tiles along a wandering line — a berth on a natural shore rather than a box cut into the sea, as on Starling Holm — and a sixth, `'all'`, for a pier off a beach with no wall at all, as on Tern Holm). Pass the island's own `(material, albedo)` as `ground=` to both `coast()` and `ridge()` (with `foot=`, how far the cliff's foot wanders in): the land then ends at the coast's wandering outline and the cliffs rise off a wandering line, never along the tile grid. Shape bays by carving the mask (Rose's `cove()`), not by leaving gaps between lobes — gaps come out as straight-sided rectangles. Mark small props (headstones) solid on their own tile only, or rows of them wall the player out. Each island has its own purpose and story: **Crimson Reach** (pirate camp, the Teeth, a ruined watchtower, the Ashen Ridge and the dragon's cave, a wreck on the reef, a rope bridge to a sea stack), **Rose Cove** (crystal terraces, a glasshouse, a chiming windmill, hot springs, an old mine), **Sapphire Port** (quay, market square, houses, the warehouse, a lighthouse whose beam sweeps the water, the potter's kiln) and **Midnight Isle** (the processional way of empty plinths, roaming stone sentinels, the graveyard of the drowned, the moon pool, abbey ruins, rolling mist). Scene extras the Lumar hooks animate: `smoke` (chimneys, kilns), `steam` (springs), `beacon` (a lighthouse lamp) and `mist`. Scripts: `scenes/lumar_crimson.py`, `lumar_rose.py`, `lumar_sapphire.py`, `lumar_midnight.py` (each rewrites its world JSON, keeping the DOCK sailing menu and the old labels so quests still match). Rose Cove's **glasshouse** opens (`lumar-rose-glasshouse`, `scenes/lumar_rose_glasshouse.py`): iron-framed glass, the night pink through it, raised grit beds of roseite growing in clusters — seedlings by the door, half-grown in the middle (ripe for picking: forage spots that grow back), the big ones humming against the glass — the cistern, the potting bench with its seed-crystals, and Liesl Marrow who keeps it (`liesl`, `npc-liesl`).

### The Crimson and the Midnight seas, grown (Workstream B2)
The denser-worlds plan's Lumar workstream B2 (`_claude_docs/spec-denser-worlds.md`): the dangerous sea and the haunted
one built out round what was there (the dragon's lair on Crimson Reach, Midnight Isle and Morwen's tower). Midnight's
own code is `js/midnight.js` (BridgeMidnight).
- **Morwen's tower, grown** (`scenes/lumar_midnight_tower.py`, `_2.py`, `_3.py`): her room's second bookshelf is now
  an iron spiral stair (STAIRS UP) to **the stillroom** (`lumar-midnight-tower-2`) — where she lives: her bed under a
  canopy of hanging stars (the cat has the pillow), the black mirror, the copper still dripping moonwater over a blue
  flame, herbs drying on a rack, the raven that says "Saltmere", a chained grimoire on its lectern, books hanging in the
  air — and in a ward circle of cold blue light in the middle of the floor, **Idris Thorne** (`idris`,
  `npc-ghost-scholar`), her old master, who drowned with Saltmere and whom she bound there rather than let go. On up
  the stair, **the star chamber** (`lumar-midnight-tower-3`): the roof's slit full of stars and the long telescope
  aimed out of it, Morwen's great **orrery** (a brass sun and the seven worlds the Wayfarer knows), star charts on the
  angled walls, the astronomer's desk (an astrolabe, an hourglass), a celestial globe, the window seat, and **the
  scrying pool** (`midnight_pool`, js/midnight.js): the Midnight Sea tonight — the moon's phase, Saltmere's drowned
  bell ringing in the dark of the moon (`BridgeMidnight.bell()`, the three nights either side of the new moon), and
  where the ghost ship is drifting. Each floor's stairwell goes back down.
- **The dead are drawn as ghosts**: a scene actor or walker with `ghost: true` (js/scene.js `drawGhost`) floats a hand's
  breadth up, see-through and breathing a cold light (the sheet faint, then again added on top), no shadow, not lit by
  the room. Their sheets are in the `ghost` skin (`characters.py` FIXED: pale blue-green, cold light not flesh).
  Midnight keeps no living people by design; ghosts and the drowned are its people.
- Test: `world/tower.js`.
- **Cinder Key** (`lumar-cinder`, `scenes/lumar_cinder.py`, chart 0.33 / 0.88 in the Crimson): the pirates' port, a
  Tortuga under a smoking volcano. A world JSON `sea` field (`"crimson"`) gives its water the sea's palette whatever
  island is nearest on the chart (js/worlds/lumar.js `seaPalette`, js/actions.js). The cove: a boardwalk on piles round
  a black-sand beach strewn with wrecks, your pier (`MOORINGS`), the fishing jetty off the east point, stilt shacks,
  drying racks, crab pots, market stalls, a campfire and an old anchor in the square, the gibbet and the battery on the
  point. **Scarlet Dee** keeps the harbour (`dee`, `npc-hm-dee`; js/harbour.js `PORTS`); **Kettle** (`kettle`) works
  the pirates' slip, a raider in frame on it (SHIPWRIGHT); **Vesper Quick** the fence (`vesper`, `npc-fence`) trades out
  of an upturned hull (THE FENCE). Up the switchback, THE CRATER TRAIL; on the east cliff, the STORMWATCH. Its crowd is
  the `pirates` wardrobe. Trade (`MARKET`, js/trade.js): rum and powder cheap, roseite and glass wanted.
- **Hot goods** (js/crimson.js, BridgeCrimson): a pirate ship you sink now and then leaves plunder (`prize(K)`, called
  from js/seafight.js `sink`: a plate 60%, rings 30%, an idol 10%; a drowned ship a locket). They're worth nothing to a
  trader (`value: 0`) and their `fence` price to Vesper, whose `sell` topic is the only one that buys them.
- **The Salt Rat** (`lumar-cinder-tavern`, `scenes/lumar_cinder_tavern.py`): the rogues' tavern in a ship's stern — the
  gallery windows across the back, Rook (`rook`, eyepatch) behind the bar, a parrot, the stove, barrel tables, a cannon
  for a table, a mermaid figurehead; **Bones** with Red Annis and Gash (js/lodge.js `TABLES.rat`, js/together.js).
- **The stormwatch** (`lumar-cinder-stormwatch`): an octagon of stone with a ladder to the lamp; old Wick (`wick`) and
  his BAROMETER BOARD (`stormwatch`, js/crimson.js): the Crimson's weather now and coming (`BridgeWeather`), a cone
  hoisted for it, and a **storm glass** to buy (`storm_glass`, 180, a charm: squalls and lightning do 0.6 of the harm —
  js/sloop.js `stormGlass()`).
- **The Crater** (`lumar-cinder-volcano`, `scenes/lumar_cinder_volcano.py`): the caldera — a lava lake with crust
  plates and hot seams, fumaroles, spires of black glass, the pirates' old shrine, the lookout. **Ember imps** (quick,
  throw fire down the line) and **magma crabs** (slow, a slam once cracked) live there (js/combat.js `ROSTERS`);
  **ember glass** grows in the cracks (forage.json, sold to Vesper); black out and you come round on the boardwalk
  (`onFaint` in the world JSON).
- **Saltmere** (`lumar-saltmere`, `scenes/lumar_saltmere.py`, chart 0.08 / 0.42 in the Midnight): the drowned village,
  half under the water — roofs and chimneys standing out of the sea, plank walks between the three humps of land still
  dry, the chapel and its bell tower, the graveyard, the net loft, the dead harbour light on the quay. No living people:
  the drowned walk it as ghosts (walkers.json, `ghost: true`), Tobiah the bell-ringer (`tobiah`, `npc-ghost-ringer`)
  stands under his tower, and the **drowned** (js/combat.js) come up out of the water at you. No harbourmaster.
- **The Gloaming** (`lumar-gloaming`, `scenes/lumar_gloaming.py`): the ghost ship. At sea she sails a loop in the
  Midnight Sea on the clock (js/sloop.js `GHOST_LOOP`, `ghostChart(T)`: the same place for everyone); come alongside
  with your sail down and the prompt is *Board the Gloaming* (`boardGhost`: where you left your boat is kept in
  `bridge_gloaming`). Her deck: torn sails glowing cold, Captain Marrow (`marrow`, `npc-ghost-captain`) at the wheel,
  her dead crew as ghosts, **spectres** to fight, her stern lantern; YOUR BOAT (`ghost_return`, js/midnight.js) puts you
  back in your boat where you left it (`BridgeSloop.resume`).
- **The quest** `gloaming-light` (*The Gloaming's Light*): Idris tells you Saltmere drowned when its light went out;
  Tobiah says the Gloaming carries it; the scrying pool shows where she is; her captain lets you take the stern lantern
  (a key item, `lantern`); hung on Saltmere's HARBOUR LIGHT it burns again for good (js/midnight.js draws it).
- Tests: `world/crimson.js`, `world/midnight.js`.

### Quests and people
- **Quests** (`assets/quests.json`, `js/quests.js`): each quest is a set of clues tied to interactions by `{world, label}`. Using one finds the clue — the first starts the quest, the last pays its reward through `BridgeProgression.recordAchievement('quest_<id>', coins)` (once per pilot). A toast reports each find; the journal (the game menu's Journal tab: J, or the Menu pill — see *The game menu*) lists what you've pieced together. Progress lives in localStorage. Keep labels stable when re-rendering a world, or the clue stops matching. Order doesn't matter: only what has to come first is enforced — a clue that `give`s a thing waits till its `get` clue is found, and `after: <clue id>` (or a list; `'*'` for a finale: every other clue) for a story step that can't happen otherwise. "Next up" points at a clue you can find now before one still waiting. A quest with `needs: <quest id>` doesn't begin until that one's done (a sequel: its clues can sit on people the earlier quest already used; it never starts on the same interaction that finished the earlier one). **Where quests start, leads, rumours and the tracker** (Alyssa and Beck: mark where a quest starts, not every clue; leads that point the way without giving it away; rumours from gossip): a quest's `begin` (a clue id, else its first clue) is where it starts — while it's unlocked and not begun, a gold ! floats over that person or thing (js/world.js `drawQuestMark`, over the person's scene actor when they have one) and the map marks it (*Quests to start*, `BridgeQuests.startsHere(wid)`); once it's under way nothing is marked. Every clue has a `hint`, a lead written to point the way (a person or a kind of place) without naming the exact spot; `leads(id, n)` gives the hints of the clues you can find now, those where you are first (a quest not begun leads to its start). A topic in people.json with `rumour: <quest id>` (or a list) puts a quest you haven't begun in the journal under *Rumours* (`hear`), with the quest's `rumour` line, who said it and the lead to its start. **The tracker** (`#quest-tracker`, top right under the coins; `refreshTracker`): the quest you're following — the one you chose with *Track* in the journal, else the one you last moved on, else the newest rumour — its progress, what you're carrying for it and two leads (one on a phone); the – folds it to its title, the title opens the journal; hidden in the menu, at sea and in sport. Kept in localStorage `bridge_quest_meta` (`track`, `heard`, `min`). A new quest wants a `begin`, a `rumour`, a `hint` on every clue and a gossip topic somewhere tagged `rumour`. Test: `world/quest_tracker.js`. A clue with `say` has its moment shown in place of the thing's usual words as it's found (`BridgeQuests.onInteract` returns the clues found just now). A person's topic can wait on a quest — `when: 'quest/clue'` (or `'quest'`, done; or a list) — and go once it's moved on (`until`); topics a quest has just opened come first in the list, lit up (`BridgeQuests.has`). **Things in a quest** (`js/keyitems.js`, BridgeKeyItems; Alyssa: you couldn't see you'd got the ribbon, and handing it back was just text — "like in Legend of Zelda"): a quest's `items` (`{ <id>: { name, text, to, icon } }`), and a clue that `get`s or `give`s one. Got: once whatever the clue opened has been read, the pilot turns to face you and holds it up over their head, light turning behind it, a fanfare (`itemget`), and a card along the bottom (*You got* · the thing, big · what it is and who it's for; Space / E / a tap). Given: it flies from your hands to theirs and bursts into sparkles (`give`) before they speak (`executeInteraction` runs again after, with the clues it found). What you carry is worked out from the quest itself (its `get` clue found, its `give` clue not), shown in the Backpack under **Quest items** (gold slots under the pack — tap one for what it is and who it's for; not stacks, so it can't be dropped, sold or stored: js/inventory.js `refreshQuest`), in the journal under the quest and first in Next up (*Carrying*). Icons are drawn in code on the 16-grid (`ICONS`; `grid` takes rows of letters). Sylvara's ribbon and moonflower use it; any quest can (`tools/tests/world/sylvara_quests.js` plays both through). **The log** (top of the journal, localStorage `bridge_log`, the last 250): what you've done, newest first, with where and when — every `BridgeQuests.toast` lands in it (they all say "J Journal", so the journal has to have it; `QUIET` skips passing hints like *Course set*), plus `BridgeQuests.arrived` (the first time you set foot in each place, from js/world.js) and the first time you meet each person (`BridgePeople.meet`). A new event worth remembering: toast it, or `BridgeQuests.note(kicker, title, sub)` to log it quietly. (Added after a new player found the journal empty: before it, a first hour of talking and wandering logged nothing.)
- **People** (`assets/people.json`, `js/people.js`): the cast. A person matched to a dialog interaction by `{world, label}` turns it into a conversation — portrait (their `sheet`'s, see *Portraits*), name and role, a greeting (another once you've met), topics to ask (who they are, their place, rumours that point at quests). Who you've met and what you've asked is remembered. These records are also the seed for any AI conversation layer later (persona + facts they know).

### Talking freely (AI conversations)
- **Server:** `api/talk.mjs` (Vercel function, Claude Haiku 5.5 at low effort, JSON replies). It builds every prompt itself — the browser never sends one — from `assets/people.json`, `assets/quests.json`, `assets/businesses.json` and `api/_personas.json`, the fuller character sheets (voice, story, what they know, secrets gated by trust, wants, how they see the others, story beats tied to quests or crew). Sheets live under `api/` so their secrets are never served to the page. Anyone without a sheet is voiced from their authored lines. GET `/api/talk` says whether it's on (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set); without it the game hides free talk and the authored topics work as before.
- **Client:** `js/talk.js` (BridgeTalk) keeps a bond per person — `feeling` −10…10 (Cold / Wary / Neutral / Friendly / Warm / Trusts you), `mood`, up to 12 `memories` (older ones folded into a `summary` by the model), `met`, `last` — in localStorage and, once `docs/supabase/2026-10-14-pilot-bonds.sql` is applied, the pilot account. Each request sends the bond, found clue ids (so characters can't spoil quests), news since you last spoke (quests finished, crew hired, businesses bought) and flags for story beats.
- **In conversation** (`showConversation`): a text box under the topics (T or / to focus), your last line above theirs, how they feel beside their role; someone you've met greets you from memory (the authored line if the server is slow). Replies can nudge the crew or trade topic. Feeling ≤ −3 and they won't join your crew; ≥ 5 and they pay a tenth more for what you sell.
- **Letters:** mode `letter` writes a short signed letter in the character's voice from the same bond and news; the rack on the bridge holds them (see *The captain's deck*).
- **Story beats** (`beats` in a sheet) key off `quest:<id>` (finished) or `clue:<quest>/<clue>` (found along the way). The people a character knows (`others`, keyed by id) reach the model by name, never by id.
- **Adding a sheet:** a new key in `api/_personas.json` matching the person's id in `people.json`; keep facts consistent with the quests, and gate anything that would spoil one behind `trust`.

### The captain's deck (the bridge)
`scenes/bridge.py` + `tools/bridge-art/assets_deck.py`. Doors: the quarters, the captain's office and the passage aft (the fitted rooms) in the south wall, the hangar west, the game room east. Stations are `deck` interactions handled by `BridgeDeck` in `js/worlds/bridge.js` (`action`): the chart desk opens the map of everything (the game menu's Map tab on *All places*), the wireless and the scope say what's out the window (`BridgeOrbit.getLocation()`), the aether gauges know whether you have an engineer, the letter rack opens your letters. The helm and the chart table open the nav chart; the hologram stands where the world JSON's `holo` says.
- **Decor:** five slots (`deck_plant`, `deck_lamp`, `deck_rug`, `deck_curio`, `deck_flag`), three pieces each, all built from `assets_deck.DECOR` as variants; bought from the Ship's Chandler on the bridge, the Lumar dockside merchant or ArcadiaMart (`js/catalog.js`, which previews them from their own layer renders) and swapped from the quarters' locker. A slot is a decor `slot_key` like the quarters' ones.
- **Crew aboard:** once hired, a crew member leaves where you found them and keeps a day aboard by the ship's clock (js/aboard.js, *The Wayfarer grown longer* below), on duty at their post (`crewPosts` in `bridge.json`: Bram at the chart desk, Okafor at the gauges, Ansel at the wireless, Corra by the ledger, Pip on the sea chest). Talking to them aboard still counts for quests that send you to them (`home`).
- **Letters:** `BridgeTalk.checkMail()` runs when you come onto the bridge. A finished quest's `letter` in `quests.json` (from, text) arrives as a thank-you; with the AI server on, someone who's fond of you (feeling ≥ 3, talked at least once, not in the last 8 hours) writes now and then (`api/talk.mjs` mode `letter`), three a day at most. Envelopes show in the rack (the `mail` layer) while you have letters.

### The captain's office
Beck asked for an office for running his workers (first the ledger's Staff & payroll page, opened from a desk on the bridge), then "keep working on the office space too": the game is heading toward an empire of businesses, ships and staff, and this is its headquarters. A room aboard the Wayfarer (`office`, `scenes/office.py`, the office pieces at the end of `assets_ship.py`, `js/worlds/office.js` BridgeOffice), through the door in the bridge's south wall west of the quarters'. Its things are `office` interactions keyed by `part` (not `action`: js/actions.js takes any interaction with an `action`). Since the empire plan's chunk 3 it's the desk you run it all from (*Running it from the office*, below):
- **The captain's desk** (`books`): a partner's desk under three arched windows onto space (BridgeOrbit through a hole, like every window aboard). Using it opens **your desk** (`desk()`): the morning dispatch (new / read, how many left to decide), standing orders (ships on orders, waiting, what they made in the last day), the Workshop, the treasury (the week's in and out) and the whole ledger. The day's dispatch lies on the desk as a letter (drawn in the `ground` hook between the lamp and the open book): folded with a red wax seal and a glint until you've read it, opened out after. The chair behind it is a seat (actions.json: you sit at your desk facing the room); the windows either side of it open the view.
- **The workshop book** (`workshop`, the ledgers stacked at the desk's right end, x 10): the Workshop on your holm (`BridgeWorkshop.summary`) — each job with who's on it, how far and when it's done (tap one to take it off the queue: its materials go back to the store), the smiths (their card), what's been made lately — and **Queue more**: by group, every recipe you know with what it takes and whether it's to hand (`have`), one, two or five (`queue`); short of something, it says where to get it (`FROM`). A gold ribbon hangs off the ledgers while something's been made since you last looked.
- **The staff board** (`staff`): green baize in an oak frame on the wall, a card pinned up for everyone working for you with their face — the staff at your businesses and the hands aboard your ships (js/look.js portraits, as on the ledger), your crew (the head of their own NPC sheet), the farm's hands — and a lamp under each: green at work, amber off shift / on their way / ashore between ships, cyan at sea, red blinking when a payday's been missed (`BridgeLedger.roster`). Ten big cards (head and shoulders) on two rows, twenty-one small ones (a head) on three past that, then `+N`; empty pegs show the vacancies. It opens the ledger at Staff & payroll. The board's face is a HOLDOUT in the render; js/worlds/office.js draws it behind the render into the world JSON's `office.board` (screen tiles: the frame and its lamp are the render's, on top).
- **The chart table** (`chart`): Lumar's seas under glass in the same way (`office.chart`): parchment, the seas you've charted tinted as on the chart (js/chart.js `charted` / `known`), the rest hatched, the islands you know inked, your holm's flag, and every ship of yours but the sloop as a pin in her type's colour (cutter blue, brigantine green, galleon gold, raider red) — fanned out round the island she lies at, or moving along her passage (`away.passage`, from `out` to `back`), a standing order's leg (`away.order`) or her trading voyage out of the holm, with her course dotted in red ink ahead of her and a wake. **Every standing order is a live line** (`drawRoutes`, from `BridgeOrders.routes()`): its loop inked round its ports in the order's own colour (`COLOURS` in js/orders.js), dashes marching the way she sails, bolder along the leg she's on, a small pixel ring at each port of call, grey while she's waiting; loops sharing a stretch sit a pixel apart. Use it for **orders** (`BridgeOrders.open`): your ships, each with her captain and her order or where she lies; captains for hire; a ship's own screen (her captain, her standing order and its last runs, change it, stop it, or send her somewhere once — `BridgeHarbour.send` → `BridgeFleet.order`, *Sending your ships anywhere*).
- **The counting table** (`treasury`): **the treasury** (js/treasury.js) — a picture of the last seven days on ledger paper (`graph`, the chooser's `art`: each day's money in stacked above the line by where it came from, what went out below it, each half to its own scale with its biggest day in the margin, today's column lit), a row per source with its own little week of bars (`spark`) and in / out / net and how it's going (growing, falling, losing, steady; wages and buying rising or easing), then the hints (what's growing, what's losing, the next coin). Its figures are `info` rows: a chooser row to read, not pick (js/interactions.js).
- **The strongbox** (`accounts`): today's takings across your businesses, what their managers have banked to you, wages a day and what's owed (*Pay what's owed*: `BridgeBusiness.payAll`, as the ledger's button), each business with what's waiting there — tap one for its books (`BridgeInteractions.business`, the card the ledger's staff rows open) — and the holm's strongbox (collected at the cottage, as ever: the row opens the map). Nothing is earned, paid or collected here that the game's own books don't already do.
- Character: shelves of ledgers with a globe and a ship in a bottle, the Wayfarer in miniature in a glass case on the sideboard (`model_ship`), a wall clock and sconces, ship's lanterns hanging in the windows, a compass rug, the lemon tree, the captain's coat on its stand. First time in, a toast says what the room is for; after that, the first time in each day while the dispatch is unread, a toast says it's on the desk (`bridge_office_dispatch`). Tests: `world/office.js` (walks in from the bridge, uses each thing; close-ups of the board and the chart), `world/empire.js` (below).

### Running it from the office: captains, standing orders, the dispatch, the treasury
The empire plan's chunk 3 (`_claude_docs/spec-empire.md`), in Beck's words: an economic engine across worlds, hire people, move toward automation, "manage it all… from the office". Keep it cute, readable and natural to progress into (a few orders early, more as you hire captains); nothing in it is required for the game to work.
- **Captains** (`js/orders.js`, BridgeOrders; localStorage `bridge_orders`): any ship of yours but the sloop can have one — a person with a face (js/look.js, a coat in their grade's colour), a grade and a wage, on the business payroll like the smiths (`BridgeBusiness.engage('captain', cand)`, staff `at: 'captain'`: a day's wage up front, one each Eastern-time midnight, two missed and they go ashore; unpaid at all and they won't sail). Three look for a ship each ET day (`candidates`): a **mate** (120–170 a day: loops of two ports, 60% of every run's profit to you — the rest is wages and wear, as on the holm's voyages), a **captain** (280–380: three ports, 68%, a fifth less trouble; signs on once you have two ships or a mate has run five loops), a **master** (650–850: four ports, 76%, two fifths less trouble and better in a fight; three ships or twenty loops run). Hired at the chart table (or the ledger), straight onto a ship without one; a captain can be moved to another ship (whoever was there goes ashore), brought ashore, or let go. The staff board pins them up (cyan lamp at sea), the ledger's Staff & payroll lists them (`captainRow`).
- **Standing orders** (`set(shipId, spec)`), each repeating by itself: a **trade loop** — two to four ports (by grade) you pick in the order she sails them, or one of today's best loops for her hold (`loops`: every pair, and for a captain every three, by what it makes an hour) — at each port she buys what the next one wants and sells it there (`legRun`: up to three goods, each sized to pay best against the market's depth, js/trade.js prices, and her own glut at a market she keeps selling into, halving every two hours like any glut; `BridgeTrade.noteSold` for the recent legs, so your own prices feel it); or a **supply run** between your own holdings — out of the holm's store (the warehouse: *Put away stock for your businesses* in js/holm.js) and her own fish hold into a business's stock (`BridgeBusiness.supply`; the Tern's Rest, the Crown & Anchor, the fish stall — any of yours on a Lumar island with a harbour and a `stock` list; never more than two days' worth), so it earns full price instead of buying in at 65%, and home again with materials for the Workshop bought at that port (timber, Sapphire glass, spore silk, powder or roseite crates, 5–40 a run: `orders:supply`, into the store, where js/workshop.js breaks the crates open) — or a run for the Workshop alone. Supply runs sail from the holm, so its dock must be built. From wherever she lies: if that's not on the loop she first sails to its first port (trading on the way if it pays).
- **On the clock** (`advance`, every 30 s, on every read, up to a fortnight back): leg by leg — a leg is her passage at her speed (half as long again short of hands) ×1.5 plus a morning's loading (35 min, more for a big cargo), longer in a storm or fog; it rolls its own trouble (`risk`: js/holm.js DANGER for its ports, × the grade, less in a storm or gale, the dispatch's blockades) and her guns and fighters decide it (beaten off: +80 and a knock; not: a third of the cargo lost and a worse one), seeded by the order and the leg so it comes out the same however late it's worked out. A hull under 40% is mended at the next yard (`repair:orders`, the yard's price) while she lies there an hour. What each leg makes is banked to you (`orders:trade`, flushed through `awardCoins`); materials and repairs are charged (`wagerCoins`), and with no coins to pay the captains buy nothing more till there are (`debt`). A ship on orders is away (`ship.away = { order: true, from, to, out, back }`, `s.at = 'away'`), so nothing else takes her (js/holm.js `returns` and js/fleet.js `arrivals` leave her alone; `BridgeFleet.where` asks `BridgeOrders.line`). **Stop** lets her finish the leg she's on and lie at that port. A run (once round the loop) is reported in her book (`last`: what it made and its story, the six latest) and the ledger; waits say why (no captain, unpaid, nothing in the store, storm-bound, in for repairs, the business sold) and the ones that need you go under *Needs you*.
- **The morning dispatch** (`js/dispatch.js`, BridgeDispatch; localStorage `bridge_dispatch`): each ET morning two to four small opportunities and troubles across what you own that morning (made once, the first time the day's read, seeded by the date: `MAKE`, `WEIGHT`), each a choice: a **buyer** wants so many crates of a good at a port by a day for a fee well over cost (take it: a `dispatch: true` contract, delivered at that port's harbourmaster — or by any captain whose leg runs from a port that sells it, who carries it first and is paid on delivery: `BridgeTrade.complete`); a broker's **tip** (note it in the journal, or send a captained ship on that loop); **pirates** blockade a sea today and tomorrow (buy an escort: safe at full speed; go the long way round — the default — safe and two fifths slower; or run it: more trouble, a quarter more for what she sells); a **storm** will close a port tomorrow (lie up — the default — or run for it: two fifths more, and a chance it costs her hull; your businesses there are quiet that day); a rival **bids** for one of your businesses (`BridgeBusiness.sellTo`: sold with what's waiting there, its staff let go; or refuse, and they work the harder for three days); a **market day** by a business (a tenth more trade, a third more with extra hands); a wrecked trader's **stores going cheap** (into the store for the Workshop); a captain asks for a **bonus** (their next three runs bring you 15% more). Whatever's left undecided goes the safe way (`dfltText` says which). What's decided lasts in `fx`, which the orders and businesses ask: `blockade(sea, ms)`, `closed(port, ms)`, `lift(business, ms)` (js/business.js multiplies each step's takings by it). Read from the desk or the ledger's *Today*; `_day(d)` previews another day.
- **The treasury** (`js/treasury.js`, BridgeTreasury; localStorage `bridge_treasury`): money in and out by source a day at a time (ET days, kept a fortnight). It wraps `BridgeProgression.awardCoins` and `wagerCoins` as properties (a getter and a setter, so a test's stub put in their place is watched too) and files every coin by its reason (`catOf`): **businesses** (takings collected or banked — and wages paid out of a business's own takings, which never reach your purse: js/business.js tells it, `internal`, in here and out under wages), **trade runs** (cargo, the catch, contracts, the holm's voyages, standing orders, escorts), **workshop** (what it makes, sold — `cargo:` of a material, or a trader's sale with things it made in it, told just before the coins move: `noteSale` from js/interactions.js and js/inventory.js — and what it buys), **wages**, **buying & building**, **everything else**. `week()`, `trend` / `trendWord`, `hints()` (what's growing, what's losing, and the next coin: takings waiting, a business buying in at cost, a ship lying idle, a captain without orders, the strongbox, idle smiths, today's best run, a business to buy), `graph(canvas)`, `spark(cat)`. A new coin reason: make sure `catOf` files it where it belongs.
- **The ledger** has the same: *Today* (the dispatch), *Treasury* (a row per source with a bar a day, `lg-bars`, and the hints), *Standing orders* (each ship, her captain, her order and where she is on it, the last run; captains ashore), captains under Staff & payroll and *People you can hire*; `BridgeMenu.open('ledger', { focus })` scrolls to a section (`staff`, `today`, `treasury`, `orders`).
- Test: `world/empire.js` — two ships, the holm, the Tern's Rest; captains hired; a trade loop and a supply run given; the game closed for eight hours and opened again (the runs, the coins, the stock delivered); the treasury's columns and hints; the dispatch (the same for the same date, a blockade's escort bought, a storm closing a port, a buyer taken on and carried by a captain on her loop); the office's desk (the sealed letter), dispatch, chart lines, orders changed at the chart table, the workshop book queueing, the treasury; the ledger; stopping an order; a phone.

### Gathering, crew, businesses, the open sea
- **Forage** (`assets/forage.json`, `js/forage.js`): spots per world id, added to the world's interactions as it loads (re-renders can't lose them). A ready spot glints; gathering bounces its items out as loot (`BridgeLoot`); it grows back after `regrow` minutes. Items (crystals of four colours, sea glass, silver nails, pine and oak logs for the workshop) live in `js/items.js`.
- **Fishing** (`assets/fishing.json`, `js/fishing.js`): spots per world id (a water tile off a jetty or a rocky point, a `pool`), added as `fish` interactions as the world loads. Each Lumar island has a long **fishing jetty** of its own, away from the sailing dock (`I.fishing_jetty` in `assets_isle.py`: a plank run with rope rails and piles, a head with a lantern, a bench, a bucket and rods; `I.jetty_cells` makes it walkable) — Sapphire's fishing pier runs six tiles out, Rose's reaches into the cove from the west arm, Crimson's goes east off the end of the beach, Midnight's out into the mist — and a spot off the rocks (Sapphire's west shore, Rose's point, by the wreck on Crimson). **In the world:** you turn to the water, the rod swings back as you charge, the line arcs out and the bobber rides the swell where it lands (further for a strong cast), ticks on a nibble, goes under on a bite, is dragged about as you reel and the catch flies in over the rail (`worldItems`, an `items` hook on the lumar, verdance and hoarfell tilesets); the panel moves to the top of the screen when you cast toward the bottom, and the interaction prompt hides while a line's out. The Lucky Horseshoe (carried) makes them bite sooner; so does the greenheart rod from your workshop, which also gives you a bigger catch zone. Hold to cast (let go near the top of the swing to cast far — far casts favour the rare fish), wait, strike when the bobber goes under, then reel: hold to lift the catch zone, let go to drop it, keep the fish in it until the line's full (tuned so a calm player lands the commons almost every time; moon eels and star pearls fight). Pools by island: silverfin, spore minnows and crabs at the harbour, bluefin and rays off Sapphire, shrimp in Rose Cove, ember snappers and the Heron's silver coins off Crimson, moon eels round Midnight, star pearls in the moon pool, perch and sun carp in the farm pond on Verdance (drawn as fresh water in the sun, lily pads and all); old boots everywhere; messages in bottles (lore, each read once). The catch goes in the pack (fish are food) and sells to Oro, or lives in a fish tank at home (*Homes and furniture*); the game menu's Fish tab shows every fish, the ones caught with how many and the biggest, and the bottles' messages. **From a boat** (`js/sloop.js`): round up into the wind till she all but stops (|v| < 0.6) and the prompt is *Cast a line* (`fishHere` → `BridgeFishing.open({ pool: 'sea_<pal>', boat: true })`, the sea you're on); the panel goes to the top, the passage lies still while it's open (no steering, no update; the sail status reads *lying still*), and `drawRod` draws your line off the rail nearest the camera with fishing.js's own `drawLine` (the rod, the line, the bobber, the rings, the catch swung aboard). The sea pools are deeper water: bigger fish, the **spore tuna** and the **lantern angler** that only a boat finds, each sea's rare fish more often, and each sea's **legend** (only ever from a boat). A one-time hint (`bridge_sea_fish`) tells you about it the first time you're making way well out at sea, and one hail in three from a fisherman's dory is about the legend in that sea (`LEGEND` in sloop.js). The catch goes in her **fish hold** (below), not your pack.
- **Rarity** (`js/fishart.js` `TIER`: common, uncommon, rare, legendary; `TIERS` their names and colours). `FIGHTS` in fishing.js tunes each tier — the catch zone, how fast the line comes in and pays out, where it starts, how long you've got to strike — against a simulated player with a human reaction time: commons always land, uncommons ~85%, a rare one about half the time, a legend is a 13–17 s fight an average player wins about a third of the time. The fish is picked when it bites (so the bite already knows: *Strike! Something big!*, a bigger shadow under the water in the panel, a gold marker on the reel for a legend). Far casts (let go near the top) and the Lucky Horseshoe tip the odds toward the rare ones. The rare ones: **coral dragonet** (Rose), **cinder grouper** (Crimson), **starfin** (Midnight) — rarely off those piers, more often at sea — **jade marlin** (Emerald) and **glass swordfish** (Sapphire) from a boat, plus the sapphire ray, moon eel, lantern angler, sun carp and star pearl. The legends, one per sea, ~2% of bites out there: **Old Mossback** (Emerald), **Crownfin** (Sapphire), **Dawnveil** (Rose), **Emberjaw** (Crimson), the **Nightglass Coelacanth** (Midnight). Values climb with rarity (rares 45–120, legends 420–650); Zephyr pays value for all of them, Maud on Tern Holm pays a quarter more for fish.
- **The panel** (fishing.js `draw`, a 210 × 76 native scene shown at a whole multiple, like the world): the water side on, in the colour of the sea you're fishing (`WATER` by pool: emerald, sapphire, rose, crimson, midnight, the moon pool's silver, the farm pond in daylight, under the ice), the pier's planks and posts (the boat's rail at sea, the ice's lip on Fellhaven), your rod, the bobber on the surface and the hook below it, fish shadows swimming by. What bites is chosen as the cast lands, so its shadow comes in for the hook in the last moments before the bite — bigger for a rarer fish. **Perfect cast**: let go with the swing in the gold (`PERFECT_CAST`, the top 12%) — the longest cast, rare fish likelier, a quicker bite. **The strike**: a ring closes in round the bobber for as long as you've got; strike in its first 45% for a **perfect hook** (a head start on the line). **The reel**: a pixel gauge at the right — the green bar you hold up (bright while the fish is in it), the fish (in its rarity's colour), the line coming back; out of the bar the line goes red and the fish thrashes at the surface. **A chest** now and then (10–20%, more on far casts, more with the horseshoe): it drifts in the gauge; hold the bar on it for 1.4 s to bring it up with the fish — coins, sea glass, silver nails, a Heron coin or a star pearl (`openChest`). Words pop over the water (`pop`: PERFECT CAST, PERFECT HOOK, A CHEST, TREASURE, TOO SOON, SNAP). Landed, the fish leaps out onto the boards; lost, the line snaps or it swims off.
- **The catch card** (`reveal` in fishing.js, `#fish-reveal` in css): every catch comes up on a card — the fish drawn big by `BridgeFishArt.canvas(id, 48, 24, 5)` (crabs, boots, coins, pearls and bottles use their item icon), its rarity in its colour, its name, its size and *your biggest yet*, where it went (pack / fish hold n / cap / let go), what it's worth, and NEW the first time. Rare ones get rays and sparks and a bell chime (`rarecatch`), a legend a bigger burst, a gold flash, a shimmering name and a fanfare (`fanfare` in audio.js). A catch your pack can't take says *Your pack is full - make room for it*; putting the card away puts the rod down and opens the Backpack's swap (`BridgeInventory.offer`: drop something, send it to the ship or throw it away, or let the fish go). It takes no notice of keys or taps for 1.4 s (up to 3 s for a legend) and every press in that time makes it wait a little longer, so someone still hammering the reel can't knock it away; once *Space · carry on* shows, a fresh press puts it away (it goes by itself 3–7 s after that), and the next cast needs a fresh press too.
- **Fish art** (`js/fishart.js`, BridgeFishArt): fish painted from a description (`SPECS`: depth, where it's deepest, tail fork / crescent / round / fan / coel, dorsal low / spines / sail / crown / aft, bill, lure, barbels, finlets, glass, a pattern — spots, stars, stripes, bars, bands, flames, scales, squiggles, moss — and colours; eels and rays are their own shapes), at any size: the card's portrait with its glowing bits haloed, and 16 × 16 icons for the new fish (`art(id)` in items.js), and the fish swimming in your tanks (js/aquarium.js: small in the room, big in the close-up). A new fish: a spec here, a tier, an item, a `FIGHT` row and a pool — and it can live in a tank.
- **Selling**: a conversation topic with `sell: [{id, price}]` (Oro on Sapphire, Joro in Arcadia) opens a sell sheet listing what they buy beside what you carry, each with its icon; you pick a count per item (none to start with, so nothing goes by accident; ←/→ or −/+, "all"), or sell everything they want at once. Goods leave the pack only once `BridgeProgression.awardCoins` succeeds.
- **Gear** (`js/gear.js`, BridgeGear): **suits** are Blender models (`characters.py pilot-<style>`: `coat`, `plate`, `thermal`, `runner`, `mystic`, `ranger`; the Flight Suit is plain `pilot`), rendered for every build and head and recoloured live (see *The pilot is customisable* above), picked by `character.js` from the style and colour you wear and the look you've chosen. Each has a perk: the Voyager's Coat +25 energy, the Neon Runner +15% speed, the Thermal Suit +20 health and keeps out the cold (`BridgeGear.has('cold')`, for the mountains), the Plated Suit +50 health and a little slower, the Starweave Robe +40 energy and the Ranger's Cloak +15 health and a little quicker (both from the Peddler). The colour is free to change any time. **Backpacks** add slots behind the hotbar (Flight Pack 6, Courier's Satchel 12, Expedition Pack 20, Voidweave Rucksack 30; the best you own is the one you carry). **Outfitters**: the travelling tailor in the Market Square (coat, satchel), the import stall on Sapphire (thermal), Cyber Gear on the District street (runner, expedition pack), Joro's back room (plate) and Vex's stash (voidweave) — set up on their interactions as worlds load (`PLACES` in gear.js) or from a conversation topic with `outfit: <shop>`. The **wardrobe** is the coat rack on the bridge, or OUTFIT (O) in the inventory screen; its *Change look* opens the look editor (free). Owned gear lives in localStorage `bridge_gear`; purchases go through `wagerCoins`.
- **The Collection** (`js/codex.js`, BridgeCodex; localStorage `bridge_codex`): the game menu's Collection tab — ten sets (`SETS`: fish, the **sea legends** (1500 for all five), crystals and ore, the farm, the workshop's materials, magic things, treasures, tools and weapons, food and drink, treasure maps; 92 things), each a grid of icons, found ones in colour, the rest as silhouettes with a hint where to look (hover or tap). Anything that lands in your pack counts (it wraps `BridgeInventory.addItem` and looks over the pack every few seconds; what you had already and every fish you've ever landed count quietly); a new find gets a toast, a finished set pays once (150-250, `codex_<set>`). A new collectible item: add it to its set with a hint. **A finished set also gives something for good** (`BONUS`, `BridgeCodex.bonus(set)`, the empire plan's chunk 5), said on its page before and after: fish — they bite a little sooner (js/fishing.js); sea legends — the rare ones come up more; crystals and ore — now and then the rock gives a piece more (js/mine.js); the farm — crops grow a tenth quicker (js/farm.js); materials — your workshop works a tenth quicker; magic things — a bolt or a wand costs one less energy (js/magic.js); treasures — treasure chests hold a fifth more coins (js/treasure.js); tools and weapons — every weapon hits a twentieth harder (js/items.js `strike`); food and drink — it goes a quarter further (`consumableUse`); treasure maps — Ilsa sells hers for a quarter less (js/treasure.js).
- **Inventory** (`js/inventory.js`): the hotbar's ten slots, then the pack's rows; the inventory screen is the game menu's Backpack tab (I; see *The game menu*). Max health and energy follow the suit.
- **Crew** (`js/crew.js`, `crew` blocks in `assets/people.json`): hire someone from their conversation (wage via `wagerCoins`, then a third of it every Eastern-time day on the business payroll; a crew member who's gone a payday unpaid doesn't work their perk, `BridgeBusiness.unpaid`); perks other systems ask about with `BridgeCrew.has(perk)` — `navigator` (voyage speed), `quartermaster` (sell prices), `runner` (forage yield), `trader` (a cut when you make port in Lumar), `engineer` (businesses hold twice the hours of takings). A crew block with `after: <quest id>` answers `notYet` until that quest is complete — Okafor won't leave Enigma until you've found the Wren.
- **Businesses** (`assets/businesses.json`, `js/business.js`; the plan is `_claude_docs/spec-living-worlds.md` §6 and §8). Each is tied to a stall's own interaction (`{world, label}`) or sold by its `owner` in conversation (a *Would you sell up?* / *How's business?* topic added to that person automatically). `kind` sets the ladder and the staff room (`SLOTS`): stall 300–2k (1), shop 8–20k (2), venue 40–90k (3, won't open without a manager), enterprise 200–500k (4), legacy ~2M (the high-roller room). `hours` are js/hours.js anchors on its planet's clock, `'always'`, or `'crowd'` (Arcadia: always open, its trade follows the 8pm–1am ET rush). `rate` is coins an open hour with a hand on; `trade` is what a specialist lifts.
  - **It earns only while it's open and someone's working it** (its staff, or you standing in it). Takings are worked out from the clock in ten-minute steps from where they were left (up to a fortnight), so it earns while you're away exactly as if you'd watched.
  - **Staff** (`TIERS`): a *hand* (80–150 a day) keeps it open and holds the takings for you to collect, up to `cap` hours' worth (twice that with an engineer aboard); a *manager* (600–1,200) runs it at ×1.6 and banks the takings to you as they come; a *specialist* (1,500–3,000) lifts it 60% in their own trade, 20% in another. Staff get 2% better a week (to 30%). Three people are looking for work at each business each day (`candidates`, hashed by the ET day). Move anyone to another business of yours: the same planet within the hour, another planet tomorrow. A crew member can run one as a manager. Every person shows a face (`faceOf`: `BridgeLook.face`, a portrait seeded by their name, dressed for the region — a coat on Lumar, a runner's jacket in Arcadia — and coloured by their tier: white hand, gold manager, purple specialist) and what they'd do there in numbers (`perkOf`: *keeps it open; holds 8h of takings…*, *runs it at ×1.6…*, *+60% here, in their own trade*, and *works at 114%*: their skill, which scales the takings).
  - **Stock** (`stock` in businesses.json: the goods it sells — fish at the fish stall, eggs, milk and honey at the bakery, fish and greens at the noodle bar, cider apples at the taverns): an open hour uses `PER[kind]` units (stall 2 … legacy 10). "Stock it from your pack" puts everything you carry that it sells into its stock (a fish or an egg a unit, dearer things more: value / 10); out of stock its staff buy in at cost and the takings fall to `BOUGHT` (0.65). So your catch and your farm feed your businesses. The screen and the ledger show how many open hours the stock lasts.
  - **Payroll**: wages are paid a day ahead — the first on hiring, then one at each ET midnight — out of that business's takings first, then your coins. A payday that passes with wages still owed counts against them; two and staff walk out. `owing()` lists what's owed (the ledger shows it). The crew on the bridge (a third of their wage) and your ships' hands (`BridgeFleet.wage`: a quarter of their signing fee, by grade) are on the same payroll, keyed `'hand:'+id` (`handOwed`).
  - A save from before staff (businesses kept at the top level) is migrated: what it had piled up is held to collect, and a one-time note says businesses need staff now.
  - The screen at the business (`showBusiness` in js/interactions.js) has views for buying, the books (hours, takings, staff, wages, today, waiting or banked), hiring, and one person (move or let go). Tests: `tools/tests/world/business.js`.
- **People walking** (`assets/walkers.json`): routes of tile points per world, walked there and back with pauses, drawn with `BridgeScene` occlusion. Each world walks its own people: sailors, a fishwife and a dockhand on Lumar's quays, skiers in Fellhaven, a courier and a street samurai in the District (`npc-sailor`, `npc-fishwife`, `npc-dockhand`, `npc-skier-a/b`, `npc-courier`, `npc-samurai`), a high roller up the Royale's red carpet, cocktail servers with trays and gamblers on the casino floor (`npc-server`, `npc-server-b`, `npc-highroller`), three elves in Elderglade; nobody strolls on Midnight Isle. **A walker you can talk to** has `talk`: the label its people.json entry is `at` (and optionally `say`). `js/scene.js` carries a dialog interaction with it (`walker: '<world>#<i>'`, moved to its tile every frame), and while you're within a tile and a half it stops, turns to face you and waits (its clock is held, so it walks on from where it stopped when you go); the map marks it where you see it, or at the start of its route for a place you're not in (`BridgeScene.walkerInteractions`). Keep a talking walker's route on walkable tiles (the audit checks it).
- **The crowd** (`js/crowd.js`, BridgeCrowd; `assets/crowds.json`; Beck: "need lots of people"): everyone who isn't the cast. A place in crowds.json (`n` at its busiest, a `wardrobe`, `busy`: `day` · `night` (the taverns, no children) · `bustle` (Arcadia's 8pm–1am ET rush) · `always`, and optionally `sit`, `pass`, `adults`, `emerge`, `sources`, `spots`, `avoid`) gets passers-by: out of a door or in off the map's edge, across to a stall, a person, a board or a patch of open ground, standing a while facing it, sitting in a seat from `assets/actions.json` (slot *i* only ever takes seat *i*, so no chair has two), then out by the nearest way; on the streets many just walk through from one way out to another and on (`pass`); Sylvara's elves step out of the trees and fade back into them (`emerge`). A place with no entry has nobody (Midnight Isle, the Last Ridge, the Singularity, Enigma, the ship, your homes). **Everyone sees the same crowd**: a place's slots each run visits of their own length (150–240 s), and a visit — who, from where, where to, how long, out where — is a hash of the place, the slot and the visit's number; whether a slot is out is decided at the visit's start from how busy the place was then (the planet's clock through `hoursAt`, `BridgeWeather.at` — rain empties the open air and fills the inns — and a festival there ×1.4), so nobody appears mid-walk and two pilots see the same people with nothing sent. **The art**: seventeen bodies (`characters.py` `EXTRAS`: man, man-cap, vest, worker, elder, traveller, woman, woman-bun, woman-hat, woman-hood, child, child-cap, skier, suit, gown, elf, elf-braid), each standing and seated, rendered by `tools/bridge-art/crowd.sh` into `assets/characters/crowd/<body>[-sit].png` in key colours (`XKEYS`: skin, hair, top, bot, out, hat — keep the two copies in step) that crowd.js swaps as a sheet loads for a wardrobe's skins, hair and clothes (mid tones shaded into three bands, `shade`), as js/look.js does the pilot's; the wardrobes (`WARDROBES`: lumar, crown, pirates, arcadia, casino, verdance, hoarfell, sylvara) pick the bodies by weight, the colours, the names and the umbrellas. In the rain outdoors most adults put up an umbrella in their wardrobe's colours (`brolly`, drawn in code, depth-tested). They're never solid: they keep to their own side of a path (`lane`), ease aside as you pass, turn to look at you when they're standing near, and E on the nearest (when nothing else is nearer) gets their name, a face and a line (`_lines` by wardrobe, rain and night lines joining in); now and then one wants something you might carry and pays for it (`_wants`). The one you're talking to waits. At most 28 out in a place (12 on a phone), only those on screen drawn. **For events**: `BridgeCrowd.gather(worldId, { x, y, r, n, until, face, id, start })` brings people from the nearest ways in to stand round a point facing it till `until` (one gathering per `id`), `react(worldId, { x, y, r, kind })` makes those near a point flee, look or cheer. Test hooks `_here()`, `_count(id)`, `_place()`, `_plan(id, slot)`, `_off(true)`. Test: `world/crowd.js`.
- **The open sea** (`js/voyage.js`): the galleon's chart-scale sea, now only the sloop's fallback (`decorate` turns every Lumar DOCK/GALLEON into a single *Go sailing* — `go_sailing` — with no menu). The sea is five spore seas, like the book's: every point belongs to the nearest island's sea (measured from a noise-nudged position so the borders wander), each drawn in its own palette, with a seam of churning foam where two meet; crossing one names the sea you're entering. Islands are drawn from their own scene renders (cut at the waterline with `shore.png`, shrunk ×0.2); the berth is found from the world's DOCK interaction. NPC ships (traders, patrol, pirate, ghost), shoals to fish, salvage adrift; docking loads the island at its spawn. The same sea is the passage when you pick an island from a DOCK menu (autopilot, above). While it covers the screen the world underneath stops drawing (`BridgeVoyage.covers()`, main.js), and which sea each 4 px cell belongs to is worked out once and kept.

### Sailing the sloop yourself (Lumar)
Every Lumar DOCK says **Go sailing** (`go_sailing` → `BridgeSloop.board(worldId)`, `BridgeVoyage.start` as the fallback) and puts you in **the sloop** (`js/sloop.js`, BridgeSloop) at the world's own scale — no chart, no autopilot, no destination picked first. **Your sloop is the boat at the pier** — there are no galleons at the docks any more (taken out of every island render, by Beck's call: the boat you see tied up is the one that leaves). Each island has her lying broadside across the end of its pier (`MOORINGS`, checked against each render, drawn into the world by a `lumar` `items` hook, `mooredItems`, in the scene's depth order with lines to the bollards); wherever you go, she's at that pier. **Boarding happens in the world** (`board`): you walk down the pier and step down into her, she dips and rocks as you sit, and only then does the sea view take over, already aboard (`start(id, null, {aboard: true})`) — the world keeps drawing her with you in her until the sea view has faded in over it (`handoff`, `coverAt`: it used to drop her as the passage began loading, half a second of an empty pier), and the sea view's camera starts exactly where the world's was and eases onto the boat (where the world's camera is held off the map's edge it isn't on her); people aboard take the island's moonlight (`lightTint`, from `BridgeScene.tintAt` at her mooring), so you look the same in the boat as on the pier; arriving is the same the other way: coming alongside, the sea camera settles on your seat at her berth (`_seatCam`), and as you climb out the island's camera snaps straight there (`BridgeWorld.snapCamera()`, once) instead of panning down the pier from the quay's spawn as the sea fades; arriving, the island loads with you sat in her at its pier and you climb out and walk up onto dry land (`disembark`). With more than one of your ships at an island you're asked which to take (see *Your fleet* below); a bigger ship lies in line with the sloop off one end of the pier's end and you walk across the sloop's deck to reach her. **The sea is the chart** (Beck's call: sail anywhere, everything in its fixed place; without the chart you just can't find things). The water is the chart (`BridgeVoyage._isles`) at a fixed scale — `K` = 720 tiles a chart unit across (600 till Beck wanted the seas a little larger: a fifth more), 0.72 of that down, the chart's own aspect — laid out from the island you cast off from (`toChart`/`toWorld`). Islands come up over the horizon as you near them: loaded within `LOAD_R` (60) tiles of their land, their own render and shore (not through BridgeScene, whose few cached scenes are the worlds you walk in), placed at their true offsets (`place`), let go past `DROP_R` (110). Each is its own render at full size with its own water (`BridgeSea.drawWaterAt`), drawn apart and feathered into the open sea over the last 192 px of its render (`I.feather`, from its shore.png: land and shallows kept, deep water fading out toward the edge) — an island near another sea (Midnight Isle's corner in the Emerald, Tern Holm by the Midnight Sea) used to end in a square of its own water. The open sea always keeps Lumar's sky (`daySea`), whichever island you cast off from.
