Steer the wrong way and you miss them; keep on the right way and you arrive, charted or not. `EXT` holds each island's land (from its shore.png, round its map's centre) for steering round it and keeping the open-sea scatter off its coast — re-measure it if a render's coastline changes. Island to island it's ~130-180 tiles to an Emerald neighbour, ~275 to another sea, ~540 corner to corner — with a scripted helmsman (`tools/tests/autohelm.js`) two to two and a half minutes of sailing either way (Beck: you got to the Crimson too fast; then, the seas a little larger). `K` is the one number to change if it wants to be shorter or longer.
- **Casting off** (`phase 'cast'`, `CAST` ms): (if you didn't board in the world, you walk down the pier and step aboard here), slip the bow line then the stern, shove off and she drifts clear along `castOut`, then hoist the main hand over hand before the sheet is yours. Coming in (`MOOR`) glides her onto the far island's mooring, turns her to its heading, throws the lines and you step ashore.
- **The chart** (`js/chart.js`, BridgeChart; localStorage `bridge_charts`): C (or the Chart pill, anywhere on Lumar; at sea C or the Chart button) opens a parchment chart of the five seas — drawn in a 400 × 264 native buffer scaled by a whole number (a touch under 1× on a phone), each island inked in sepia from its own render cut at the waterline (`shapeOf`), names and notes as text over it: what's there (`NOTES`), your ships at anchor, the contracts you're carrying there (a red seal), the holm's flag, you (a breathing red ring — at sea, exactly where you are on the water: `BridgeSloop.chartPos`, your position at the chart's fixed scale) and your course as a dotted gold line. **You start with the Emerald Sea charted** (Diggen's Point, Crownhaven, Tern Holm, Starling Holm); the other seas are hatched and their islands smudges until you buy their charts (`PRICES` 220-520, cheaper in that sea's own port) from any harbourmaster's book (*Charts*) or Ilsa Venn (*Sea charts*) — or until you've been there. Only islands you know are on the compass and in the course list. **Set a course** on it: ashore, the pier's DOCK then says *Sail for …* and the next passage is laid for it from the start (`BridgeChart.dest()` read by `start`), with the gold mark leading you out of your harbour's channel; at sea it sets one at once (`BridgeSloop.setCourse`, any time; a new course replaces the old). The passage waits while the chart is open. Arriving clears the course.
- **Setting a course:** out of the harbour mouth the compass marks every island on your chart by its initial, at its true bearing from where you are (the one you left too, once you're well away); hold a heading within ~24° of one (`aim`, the top panel says STEERING FOR …) for a couple of seconds at speed and that's your course (`commit`). The gold mark (`route`, laid again from wherever you've got to when you stray more than 24 tiles off it, `offCourse`) leads out of the harbour you're in by its channel, round any island in the way (`detour`: each island an ellipse from `EXT`, passed on its nearer side), and once the island's come up, in by its own channel to her berth. Esc (⋯ on touch) lists *Set a course for …* (or *Skip ahead* and *Change course* with one set) and *Open the chart*. The panel's bar is how much of the course you've sailed (`courseSailed` against what's left, `togo`). A line said while another's only just up waits its turn (`msgQ`), so a warning is never lost.
- **The boat** is a Blender sheet (`tools/bridge-art/sloop_sprites.py` → `assets/scenes/sloop`, cut at the water line with its own `shore.png`): the sloop's hull at 32 headings, a fisherman's dory at 8, and the sea's props (rocks, a reef, red and green buoys, a crate, a barrel), lit by the islands' moonlight, in 5 × 5-tile cells (`cell()`, the thing's centre at (80, 96) px in its cell; the sloop 3.2 tiles long, grown from her old 2.6-tile lines by `S`). The mast, rigging, boom, mainsail and jib, pennant, tiller and the pilot (the seated sheet, on the windward bench) are drawn live in the boat's own frame, so the rig heels to leeward, the boom swings and the sails luff. Other boats' crews are the `npc-regular` sheet; their sails take `CLOTH` palettes.
- **Sailing:** ←/→ the tiller. By default **the sail trims itself** and ↑/↓ **raise and lower the sail** (`b.reef`, 0–1: speed scales with it and the sail is drawn reefed down to a scrap — lowering it is how you slow and stop, to fish or to grab flotsam; a squall hurts less with it down). **T** switches to trimming by hand (remembered in `bridge_sail_hand`): ↑ hauls the sheet in, ↓ eases it out, and the panel shows the sheet against the right band. Speed comes from a polar (nothing within 40° of the wind — in irons — best on a beam reach) times the trim: too far out luffs, too far in stalls. Forgiving where it used to bite: sit head to wind with the sail up and no hand on the tiller and her bow falls away by itself (`ironsT`); *Come alongside* works at any speed (she drops her sail as she comes in) within 3.4 tiles of the berth; flotsam can be hauled at up to 3.6 kn. Explained in plain words: the compass draws the no-go wedge (red) round where the wind comes from; the panel says INTO THE WIND — TURN AWAY / SAIL DOWN — ↑ TO RAISE IT / SAIL FLAPPING / SAIL TOO TIGHT / SAILING WELL and WIND FROM THE SIDE etc.; **How to sail** (`howTo()`: steer, the wind, speed, islands, arriving, stuck?) opens by itself on your first passage (`bridge_sail_taught`), from the menu, or with H. **Uncharted water:** the compass marks only islands on your chart, but every island is out there where the chart has it — sail its way and you'll find it. One you have no chart for comes up as *Land ho!* (`landHo`, 34 tiles off), shows as UNCHARTED ISLAND (`unchartedNear`), offers *Come alongside* with no name, and stays off your chart after you tie up (js/chart.js keeps it in `found`, not `visited`, and says to buy that sea's chart) — so it's hard to find again unless you remember the way. Its sea still takes on its colour on the chart, lightly, as any sea you've been in does (a charted one is full colour): the sea your mark is in (`BridgeSloop.chartPos`, read by js/chart.js each second) is kept in `sailed` and coloured from then on. **The edge of the known seas** (`EDGE`, chart 0.03-0.97 across, 0.04-0.96 down): the swell runs high and confused and a current sets you back toward the islands. Coming about the boom swings over; gybing it slams across. Gusts darken the water before they reach you. Leeway pushes you downwind when slow. Rocks, the reef and the shore push you off with a scrape; shallows drag the keel. Each sea has its own pace (`BASE`).
- **Wind and weather** (`windAt`, from `wind.seed`, where you are and `Date.now()`, so captain and crew see the same; the crew also gets the wind at the boat in `helm`): the wind's own slow veering and backing; a front now and then (`FRONT`, every 95 s a new `slotDir`) swinging it up to ~100° over half a minute, fresher behind a big one, said ~14 s before it comes (*The wind's backing - it'll come round to the north*, `windNews`); lulls where it all but dies (`lullAt`); islands shadow it in their lee and bend it along the shores it blows onto (`islandWind`). Weather systems drift across the sea (`genWeather`, one at most to a `WCELL` square of the chart's frame, each born, drifting ~40 tiles and dying away on its own clock): **rain squalls** — dark rough water under a grey curtain of falling rain you can see from well off (`drawWeatherWater`, `drawWeatherAir`), the wind gusty and swinging inside, rain over everything — and **fog banks**, a pale bank on the water that swallows you (the screen greys in, thicker on the Midnight Sea). One ahead is said before you reach it. The Crimson's squalls (most of its systems, plus its knock-downs) and Midnight's mist are the strong versions. The forgiving sailing holds through all of it (her bow still falls off in irons). Test hooks: `_sky()`, `_systems(x, r, T)`, `_front(T)`.
- **The seas you sail on** are the chart's own regions (the Midnight Sea reaches further than the rest — a weighted nearest island, js/chart.js `SEA_W`, the same in sloop.js and voyage.js — so the Midnight Isle stands 30 tiles of its own sea clear of any other, Tern Holm 24; Beck wanted it well inside its sea): `seaDists`/`seaField` mirror js/chart.js `seaAt` (the nearest island's sea, the borders nudged by noise, in the chart's 400 × 264 buffer; keep the two in step), with a pull toward each island's own sea round it so its render's water always matches, and a wobble at your own scale. `drawSeas` samples it on a 16 px grid, draws the sea most of the screen is in, clips each other sea to its region with marching squares and runs churning foam along every seam — so the water turns colour exactly where your mark crosses into another sea on the chart, course or no course. The sea you're in (`seaHere`) sets the wind's pace, the weather, the fishing pool and the story said as you cross (`crossing`, once it's held a moment).
- **The five seas have their stories** (`LORE`, said as you enter each): the Emerald, Sapphire and Rose are the calm trade water (`CALM`) — busy with shipping (merchantmen on the lanes between the ports, the Crown's navy, sloops about their business, trawlers and dories on the fishing grounds: *Other ships at sea* below), the spore whale surfaces. **The Crimson Sea** is squalls: rain, lightning with thunder after it (`storm`, the `thunder` sfx), hard gusts every few seconds; the traders give it a wide berth, so it's pirate water — pirate sloops and raiders lurking, privateers prowling its edges. **The Midnight Sea** nobody sails who doesn't have to: mist, extra rocks that hole you (a few health, never a knock-out — it stops at 10), a wreck on its side, will-o'-wisps (`wisps`) drifting, a bell somewhere, no traffic and no whale. Midnight Isle has no one walking its paths either (`walkers.json`).
- **On the way:** what's out on the water is seeded by where it lies (`genCell`: `CELLW`-tile squares of the chart's own frame, filled as you come near from their own seed and the sea they're in, never within 8 tiles of any island) — so it's the same every trip and for everyone aboard: rocks alone or in clusters, now and then a reef with rocks either side, a fisherman at anchor who answers a hail with a rumour (`LINES`: the sixth sea, the bell off the Teeth, Tamsin's signal; calm seas only), crates and barrels adrift to haul aboard (coins or goods; hauled ones are remembered by `key`), sloops about their business in the calm seas and pirates lurking in the Crimson (`loopers`, each round its own loop, all of it in its own kind of water), Midnight's wrecks and lights. Rocks, reefs and wrecks keep off the shipping routes and the fishing grounds (`nearRoute`).
- **Other ships at sea** (Beck: "fishing ships versus merchant ships versus fighting ships"; js/sloop.js `plan`, `moveTraffic`): where each one is comes from the chart and the clock, the same for everyone aboard whichever island they cast off from — routes laid once in the chart's own frame (abs tiles), each ship a fixed share of the way round hers at `Date.now()`. **Merchantmen** (`LANES`, between Diggen's Point, Crownhaven, Tern Holm, Sapphire Port and Rose Cove): trading cutters, merchant brigantines and now and then a merchant galleon (her own `merchant` rig, less sail than a fighting one), alone or a few in company, out on one side of the lane and home on the other, round each end in a long turn; in their houses' colours (`HOUSES`: a wash over the hull, their cloth and flag — Ashgrove & Vane, the Sapphire Glassworks, the Rose Silk Company, Tolliver's), crates, bales and barrels on deck (`STOWAGE`, `cargoSprite`), a swivel or a pair of long nines; only where the sea is kind (`calm` along each route: none on the Crimson or the Midnight Sea). **The Crown's navy** (`BEATS`) beats up and down the Emerald in company — a navy cutter, brigantines, a man-of-war — washed green, white sails, guns run out, copper-sheathed, flying the gold pennant over green. **Privateers** prowl the Crimson's edges (brigantine and raider, washed dark red, red sails, black-and-red pennant). **Pirates** lurk in the Crimson (from the cells: pirate sloops tarred black and pirate raiders, black sails, the black flag with a pixel of skull). **Trawlers** (on the cutter's hull, the `trawler` rig — a stumpy mast and a scrap of tanned sail) work the fishing grounds off the islands (`GROUNDS`: bearings off each island, never south where its harbour opens; calm water all round), slowly round and round with the net out astern (`drawNet`: the warps, a float line of corks bobbing, the bag under the water, a fish thrashing in it now and then) and gulls wheeling over it (`gs`), dories at anchor round them (things: `groundDories`). Everyone keeps clear of you (two tiles of water between your hulls at least, measured keel to keel: `apart`), of each other (pushed apart, `moveTraffic`) and of the harbour mouths (every Lumar harbour opens south: `MOUTH`, the pier's end from the map's centre; routes go round each island 12 tiles off and round each mouth, `detourAbs`). Each shows her guns and fittings (`kit`, through BridgeShipKit), hands on deck, a wake astern (`trafficWake`). **Hail** one within hail (*Hail the merchant brigantine*): a line in her own voice (`HAILS` by role — the merchantman's cargo and port, the navy's warning about the Crimson, the privateer's letter, the pirate's sneer; trawlers have the fishermen's rumours and legends). **Each sea's boats look their own** (Beck: "the people and the styles of the ships… feel different for each of these places"; `SEALOOK`, `dress`): a boat wears her home water — a trawler or dory her island's sea, a merchantman her house's port (`PORTHOUSE`: Ashgrove & Vane of Crownhaven, the Glassworks of Sapphire, the Rose Silk Company, Tolliver's of Diggen's Point and Tern Holm), a sloop on her loop the sea she sails: the Emerald tidy green-and-white (a green wash, white sails with two green bands, tan sails on the working boats), the Sapphire blue hulls under white sails striped blue with a glint of glass in the cloth, the Rose pale hulls and rose silk, the Crimson tarred black under patched red-and-black (`clothMarks`: stripes, patches and glints drawn on fore-and-aft and square sails alike); the navy keeps the Crown's colours and the privateers theirs. **The fishing fleet keeps the clock** (js/harbourlife.js, *Harbours that work* below): each trawler and ground dory is one of her island's fleet (`S.fleet`, `fi`) — out of the harbour at dawn from the pier's end down the mouth and round the island to her ground (`fleetPos`, `trips`: `harbourExit` + `detourAbs`, at `VS` 0.9 tiles a second), working it all day, home the same way at dusk, in port by night (so the grounds are empty after dark). Test hooks: `_traffic()`, `_shipsAt(T)` (every route's ship at T, chart frame), `_planCheck()`, `_routes()`, `_hail(o)`, `_msg()`. `_fleetAt(T)` (the fleet at clock time T). Tests: `world/sea.js`, `world/harbours.js`. Channel buoys only at harbour mouths (lamps blinking, bells), gulls round the harbours (one follows you out), flying fish when you're quick, the spore whale once a passage well out in kind water, a glowing wake.
- **Arriving:** E (or a tap) within reach of any island's quay — the one you set a course for or not — brings you alongside (`moor(I)`, `dockAt`) and the island loads at its spawn by the dock; near your own quay you can put back in. Esc offers to keep sailing, skip ahead or put back. Sounds: the `lumar-passage` place (sea, wind, creak) and `luff`, `gybe`, `bell`, `thunder`.
- Test hooks: `BridgeSloop._phase()`, `_boat()`, `_isles()`, `_route()`, `_things()`, `_commit(id)`, `_moored()`, `_loaded()` (the islands up), `_toWorld(cx, cy)`, `_sea(x, r)`, `_snap()` (the camera onto the boat), `_race()`.

### Harbours that work (Lumar)
Beck: "I like harbors that work a lot". Every Lumar harbour but Midnight Isle's (the Ferryman's, left lonely) is at work by Lumar's own clock, the same for every pilot with nothing sent (`js/harbourlife.js`, BridgeHarbourLife; the boats in `js/sloop.js`).
- **The fleet's day** (`fleet(ms)` → home · leaving · out · returning, `times(id, i, ms)` → each boat's `dep` and `arr`): Lumar's day from its start (`dayOf`, its daylight taken at the day's start so the timetable doesn't creep with the season), the boats casting off one by one over the 25 minutes round sunrise and coming in over the half hour before sunset. **In each harbour** the island's fleet (its grounds' trawlers and dories from `plan()`, plus `extra` inshore dories where there are no grounds: the holm, Crimson Reach; at most `max`) lies at berths found in the render's own water as the island loads (`harbourOf`, `berthNear`: alongside the quay or a pier, afloat, on the map, clear of your ships, of each other, of the sloop's channel and of boats painted into the render, `keep`), each with her way out found over open water to past the map's south edge (`wayOut`); at dawn she slips her lines and goes out along it (sail hoisted once clear), by day the berth's empty, at dusk she comes back in bow first and makes fast. Drawn by `drawBoat` (trawlers: the cutter hull, trawler rig, sea colours) or the dory sheet (oars dipping under way) into a scratch canvas, stood in the scene with `BridgeScene.drawOccluded` at her near side and lit by the island's light (`harbourItems`, a `lumar` scene hook). At sea the same boats steam out and home (*Other ships at sea*).
- **The catch auctioned** (js/events.js `catchAuction`): on Diggen's Point, Crownhaven, Tern Holm, Sapphire Port, Rose Cove and Crimson Reach (off a barrel head, no questions), an event **due** once a day (`due(slot)`: `plan` takes it before the random draw) at `auctionAt` — two minutes after the last boat's in — the auctioneer by her chalkboard, boxes of the catch, the crowd gathering (`BridgeCrowd.gather`), a lot a minute to bid on.
- **A merchantman alongside** (`merchant(id, ms)`: half-hour slots, more by day; arriving, alongside, leaving) at Crownhaven and Sapphire Port (Diggen's Point has no room on its sand for a crane beside the shuttle's pad, nor water for her off it), her berth found off the **crane** (`HARBOURS[id].crane`, its post solid via `decorate`): a timber jib crane drawn live (a stone block, the iron-banded post, the winch drum turning while the fall runs, the braced jib swinging, the hook), round a 32-second cycle (`crane(id, ms)`: down into her hold, the sling made fast, up, swung over the quay, down onto the stack, let go, back), the stack on the quay growing; two dockhands carry the crates from the stack (`pick`) to the store and back, a crate on the shoulder.
- **The quay at work** by day and in the dry (`working`): a sailmaker over canvas spread on the stones, the bolt rolled at one end (`sail`), net-menders over a net heaped to mend (`nets`), a ropemaker walking backwards from the spinning wheel paying out the yarn and back to start again (`rope`: Crownhaven and Sapphire). Shuttles and needles glint at their hands. Crowd spots near the work (`assets/crowds.json`) so passers-by stop to watch.
- **The static pieces for the renders** (a jib crane, cargo stacks, a ropewalk, a sail loft, net racks, canvas) are Blender builders in `tools/bridge-art/assets_harbour.py`, for the island rebuilds to place; the life stays live in JS.
- Test hooks: `BridgeHarbourLife._check()` (every quay spot on open ground, the dockhands' way clear), `BridgeSloop.harbour(id)`, `BridgeSloop._harbourAt(id, T)`, `BridgeSloop._fleetAt(T)`. Test: `world/harbours.js` (each harbour by night, dawn, day and dusk, the auction, the crane through a cycle, the quay's work, the fleet at sea, each sea's boats; `shots/harbour_*.png`).

### The spore bloom (Lumar)
Beck agreed it: "a night or two a month the whole sea lights up". `js/bloom.js` (BridgeBloom) keeps the calendar; the
water everywhere draws it.
- **When** (the same for every pilot, from the real moon and the date in Eastern time): each ET calendar month has one
  bloom day — the day the real moon is nearest new in that month (`BridgeClock.moon`) — and in about half the months (a
  hash of the month) the darker of its neighbours too: one or two bloom days a month (November 2026: the 9th and 10th).
  A bloom day's nights are Lumar's nights centred in it (centred on 0:00, 8:00 and 16:00 ET): three Lumar nights a
  day. The strength (`on(ms, worldId)`, 0..1) eases in as the light goes and out at dawn — (1 − the sun)^1.5, the sun
  worked out for that moment from Lumar's own year (`daylightAt`), so every page agrees; on the Midnight Isle, which never
  sees the sun, it's on all the bloom day. `next(ms)` → `{ tonight, days, date, start }`, `label()` → *The spore bloom:
  tonight!* / *…is on: the whole sea's alight* / *Next spore bloom in 9 days*. `nights(y, mo)` lists a month's days.
  Previews: `?at=2026-11-09T08:00` (Lumar's midnight on a bloom day), or `BridgeBloom._force(k)` (`null`: back to the
  calendar).
- **Each sea in its own light** (`HUE`, `core()` its brightest, near white): the Emerald green, the Sapphire blue, the Rose
  pink, the Crimson ember-orange, the Midnight pale violet.
- **The water** (`js/sea.js`, so it's everywhere the sea is drawn — the islands you walk, the sloop's open sea and the
  islands it passes, the galleon's fallback, the window views): slow luminous currents — contour lines of a swirled noise,
  in the sea's light, brighter where the spores have drifted together, in four flat dithered bands like the waves (two
  textures of different sizes, 384 × 192 and 320 × 160, worked out a few rows a frame as the bloom first comes on, so
  there's no stall and no visible repeat) — drifting slowly two ways over the waves (`bloomWater`: drawImage tiles over
  just the rect drawn, quicker than a pattern fill); on an island's water layer they go in `source-atop`, so they keep
  the water's opacity from `shore.png` (thin over the shallows) and run on from the open sea without a seam. The shore
  (`animate`) lights up most: a glowing band out from the waterline in flat steps that swells as each wave comes in, the
  foam lines and the wash at the waterline in the sea's brightest light — round rocks, beaches, piles and hulls. Motes
  (`motes`) drift through the water, a few bright with a small plus of a halo; the night spores in `glints` brighten and
  take the bloom's hue. Older scenes without `shore.png` get their foam band in its light. The brightest pixels are
  saturated and above their neighbourhood, so js/gpu.js's glow (the eye's guess, outdoors) blooms the surf by itself.
- **At sea** (`js/sloop.js` `drawBloom`, one call after the wake): soft dithered glows (`BridgeBloom.glowAt`, small
  sprites added on — `blob`) along your wake, round every hull's waterline, in the V astern of every other ship, and in
  the surf round rocks, the reef and things afloat; the galleon's wake (js/voyage.js) takes the bloom's light too.
- **The chart** (js/chart.js `drawBloom`): a note under the title, every day (*Next spore bloom in 9 days*, glowing green
  when it's tonight), and on a bloom night every sea washed in its own light with specks of it twinkling.
- **People notice** (`arrive`, through the Lumar `ground` hook, checked once a second): a toast the first time you're on
  Lumar's open ground once it's lit (*The spore bloom — the whole sea is alight tonight*), and on the quays of Diggen's
  Point, Crownhaven, Tern Holm and Sapphire Port a few people gather by the pier to watch (`BridgeCrowd.gather`, round the
  DOCK, facing the sea, half an hour). From the boat the rare fish bite a quarter more often on a bloom night
  (js/fishing.js `pick`, `sea_*` pools).
- **Cost**: two blits and a few hundred specks a frame on the open sea, two blits on an island's water layer, a little
  more arithmetic in the shore's animation. Test: `world/bloom.js` (the calendar — one or two days every month, the same
  on two pages, lit only on bloom nights — the note and the crowd, shots of Diggen's Point, Crownhaven, Rose Cove, the
  Emerald and Rose Seas from the sloop and the chart, each beside an ordinary night: `shots/bloom_*.png`, and what it
  adds to the sea's drawing a frame).

### Racing: anyone who's there, and the Emerald Regatta (js/race.js)
Beck: "make it where you can race anybody else that's in there… I want to be able to race Alyssa" — on skis, up the face, across the water — and a sailing race round buoys and islands against the other sloops, on a set day each week.
- **Racing someone** is a play-together game (js/together.js; `BridgeRace.here()` adds it to *Play a game…* where you stand): **Ski race** on the pistes, Fellhaven and the summit (`race-ski`: pick the run), **Climbing race** at the face and Fellhaven's route feet (`race-climb`: pick the route), **Sailing race** at any Lumar pier you can sail from (`race-sail`: a course from `COURSES` — first across the harbour mouth of any island you know near this one, and from Crownhaven the regatta's course). A stake as for any game. Each is taken to the start — the run's gate (side by side, `lane` ±0.75 tiles), the route's foot, your own sloop at the pier — and it **waits for both**: each says `rdy` over the game's wire (`BridgeTogether.send`) when they're in it, and on hearing the other's each sets the gun 3.5 s ahead, so both start within a heartbeat and each times their own run from their own gun. 3-2-1, go. The quicker time takes it: a time is scored as minus its milliseconds (together.js is higher-is-better), labelled as the time; Esc, falling off, or going ashore before the line is DNF. On the way the HUD says how you stand against them (ski: *Alyssa ahead / behind / down*, by who's further down the hill; climb: ALYSSA AHEAD / BEHIND / LEVEL by height, TOP when they're up), and a toast says when they're across (*Alyssa is down in 0:41.2*).
- **Seeing each other race** (js/multi.js): `pos` carries the pose you're drawn in when it isn't walking (`p: [kind, row, col, flip, lift]`, kind from `POSE_KIND`: ski, climb), so a skier or a climber is drawn in their own suit in that pose on the same snow or the same face, the lift eased; `BridgeMulti.nudge(id, dx)` draws someone a little aside (two climbers on the same holds). At sea the other sloop is on the water with her name over her (`sea` as ever, plus `rs` the race she's in, `rg` how far she's still to go, `rf` her time once across).
- **Racing in the sloop** (js/sloop.js, a race `spec` on `start(…, { race })` or `raceBoard(worldId, spec)`): `marks` to round in order (a point off an island's side, its land `off` tiles clear, or a chart point) and a line to cross (`finish`: an island's harbour mouth, a chequered post either side, `drawLine`); each mark an orange buoy with a white band and a numbered flag, the next one ringed (`drawMark`); the gold mark leads out of the harbour, round each mark still to round, to the line (`raceRoute`). `hold` keeps her still off the pier till the gun, side by side by `lane`; the wind is seeded by the race (`layA(seed)`), so everyone in it sails the same wind; no pirates (they've all come to watch), no setting a course, no casting a line. The panel top-centre has the clock, MARK 1 OF 2 / FOR THE LINE and your place live (`racePlace`: everyone by how far they've still to go; across the line, by time). Esc offers *Retire from the race*. **The other boats** (`npcs`, from the regatta): lined up across the harbour mouth, every other one a length back, each sailing the course at her own pace leg by leg for its angle to the wind (a beat is what she makes good up it in tacks, drawn tacking), where she is coming from the clock alone — so everyone racing sees the same boats in the same places, and her time is known from the gun. Hooks: `racing()`, `retire()`, `raceGun(at)`, `_race()`.
- **The Emerald Regatta** (`REGATTA`): **Fridays, noon till midnight Eastern time, a start every quarter of an hour** (`nextStart`: the next start at least 40 s off, on BridgeClock's Eastern day; its `slot` — quarter hours since the epoch — names it) off Crownhaven's harbour mouth — round a mark south of Tern Holm, round a mark south of Starling Holm, back across the line. Every harbourmaster's book has *The Emerald Regatta* (`bookRow`: the next start and how long till it on a regatta day, your best placing that day, else when it sails); at Crownhaven *Enter - to my sloop* (`enter`) takes you down to her and out to the line, the gun at the start's time for everyone who entered it. Five of the Emerald's racing sloops sail each start (`fleetFor(slot, gun)`: which five and how quick each is come from the start alone — a share, 0.66–0.88, of a sloop's pace in the Emerald's wind at the gun, `BridgeWeather.sea(ms)` — so a good passage beats most of them and a great one all), and anyone else in the same start races too. People gather on Crownhaven's quay to watch the start (`BridgeCrowd.gather`). **In a storm it's postponed** (the committee won't send boats out). A placing pays once a regatta day — 500, 250, 120 coins (`prize`); a better one later that day pays the difference — and the first win, **the Regatta Cup** (`regatta_cup`, in the codex's finds); `bridge_regatta` keeps the day, best, wins, runs and your best time. On a regatta day a word comes up once on Lumar (*Today off Crownhaven*).
- Tests: `together/t_race_ski.js`, `t_race_climb.js`, `t_race_sail.js` (two pilots, a stake, the verdict), `world/regatta.js` (the schedule, the book, a storm, entering, a short course against the fleet, the prizes); `tools/tests/autohelm.js` sails for them.

### Your fleet: ships, hands, cargo and trouble at sea (Lumar)
- **Ships** (`js/fleet.js`, BridgeFleet; localStorage `bridge_fleet`): a ladder that each sail differently — the sloop (the starter boat, always at whatever pier you're at), the **cutter** (quick, 40 crates, 1 gun, 1 hand), the **brigantine** (square-rigged forward: slow through a turn, poor to windward, shrugs off storms; 120 crates, 3 guns, 3 hands), the **galleon** (three masts of square sail, 300 crates, 5 guns, 5 hands, sails like a cathedral) and the **raider** (Crimson's pirate schooner, the fastest hull; 4 guns). `SHIPS` holds each one's length, beam, speed, turn, acceleration, how close to the wind she points (`nogo`), hold, hull, gun slots, hands needed, and how much storms and rocks hurt her. Every bigger ship lies where you last tied her up (`at`). `effective(ship)` folds in her fittings and crew; js/sloop.js sails `active()`. Each has a **fish hold** too (`fish`: sloop 8, raider 12, cutter 16, brigantine 30, galleon 50; `ship.catch`, `stowFish` / `takeFish` / `catchCount`): what you catch from her goes in it (the sailing HUD shows *FISH HOLD n / cap* under the passage panel), a full hold means you measure it and let it go, and when you tie up (or put back, or skip ahead) a *Landing the catch* menu offers to sell it all on the quay (`BridgeTrade.sellCatch`, the port's price for fish, with a glut like any sale), carry it ashore into your pack (`carryCatch`), or leave it aboard for a better market; her card at the harbourmaster shows the fish hold and lands it too. A wreck loses it.
- **How they're drawn:** hull sheets per ship (`tools/bridge-art/ship_sprites.py <type>` → `assets/scenes/ship-<type>`, 32 headings in cells of `HULLS[type].cell` tiles, the same 45° camera and moonlight as the sloop's): quarterdecks, forecastles, gun ports with guns run out, hatches, a ship's boat, lit stern windows and lanterns. **Sizes** (Beck: "a little bit bigger, not crazy, and scale up again a little bit bigger, never super crazy"): each a step bigger than the last — the sloop 3.2 tiles long, the cutter 4.2, the raider 4.8, the brigantine 6.0, the galleon 8.2, the sovereign 8.4 (drawn on the galleon's sheet) — with beams to match (fleet.js `SHIPS`, seafight.js `KINDS`). The generators build each hull to her old lines (2.6 / 3.4 / 4.4 / 5.0 / 7.0) and grow her whole by `S` (`frame`); js/shipkit.js `LINES` carry the same `s` (`BridgeShipKit.scale(sheet)`) and js/sloop.js grows `RIGS0` by it (`rigOf`, kept per rig and sheet), so her guns, bands, masts and sails stay on her. Cells: sloop 5 (its sheet), cutter 6, raider 6, brigantine 8, galleon 11. Re-rendering: `nice -n 10 BVENV/bin/python tools/bridge-art/ship_sprites.py OUT <type>` (~7 min a sheet on a busy machine), then `scene_post.py OUT apps/bridge/assets/scenes/ship-<type>`. The rig is drawn live from `RIGS0` in sloop.js — fore-and-aft masts with booms, square masts whose yards brace round with the sheet (`squareMast`), headsails off a bowsprit, a wheel on the quarterdeck for anything over 4.5 tiles (`wheeled`: the raider and up; you stand at it), a tiller below that — at heights that match the sheets (`deck`, `qd`, `bow`, a mast's own `z`). A boat's spec may name a rig of its own (`rig`: the traffic's `trawler` and `merchant`). Other boats can be washed with a colour (`b.tint`: pirates tarred black, the drowned pale green, the shipping's colours — washed a cell at a time, the heading she's drawn at, and kept: `tinted`; a whole sheet per tint ran to hundreds of megabytes with its day and dusk copies).
- **What you fit shows** (`js/shipkit.js`, BridgeShipKit; Beck: "when you upgrade your ships… some sort of visual upgrade, like seeing cannons fire"): every gun and fitting in her spec (`effective(ship).guns` / `.fits`) is drawn on her — at sea, lying at the pier and in the harbourmaster's book — on her own hull sheet only, at the heading her sprite is drawn at (the nearest of 32, not her true heading, so it sits on the sprite exactly), from her lines (`LINES`: half-breadth, sheer, decks at her old size, grown by `s` — keep in step with `ship_sprites.py` / `sloop_sprites.py` and their `S`). **Guns** stand at `STATIONS` along her rails in the order they were mounted, a side gun on both rails (it fires from whichever side bears), a bow chaser in her bows pointing ahead; each look is a little sprite drawn a pixel at a time per heading and kept (`sprite`): the swivel on its post, the long nine on a red-ochre carriage with its trucks, the stubby carronade on its slide with a brass band, the long slim chaser with a brass ring at its mouth, the Ferryman's ballista (silvered bow, a bolt whose moonglass head glows — gone while it reloads), a drowned ship's green-bronze ghostfire guns with a cold glow in their mouths. Pirates and the drowned carry theirs (`b.kit`, seafight.js `foeBoat`; raiders storm-rigged). A gun that's fired (`fired(b, i, side)`) jumps back inboard and runs out over 0.75 s, a tongue of fire out of its mouth (white, yellow, orange, red tips; then torn and thinning, with embers) over a glow. **Fittings**: copper sheathing a copper band at her waterline with a glint running along it; oak doubling a heavier, darker band of planking a pixel proud of her topsides (a light strake, plank seams, butts, trenails); roseite sheathing faceted pink crystal along her waterline with crystals growing out of it, twinkling (`bands`: worked out once per hull, heading and fittings and kept as a canvas, lit by the sky like her sprite through `skyBlit`); storm rigging doubled shrouds, ratlines and backstays, tarred (`stormRig` in sloop.js); crystal-weave sails rose-silver cloth with rose seams and crystal threads catching the light (`CRYSTAL`, `sparkle`); the moon lantern an iron-framed lantern at the main truck (`moonLantern`) and a pool of its cold light on the water round her with the wavelets glinting in it (`moonPool`: faint by day, full at night, half again on the Midnight Sea). Sailing together the captain's kit goes to the crew (`sail-go` `kit`) and to other boats at sea (`sea` `k`: `'gun,gun|fit,fit'`). The harbourmaster's card for a ship has **her portrait** (`BridgeSloop.portrait(canvas, ship)`, a chooser's `art` in js/interactions.js; *Fit out* shows her again the moment something goes aboard): drawBoat on a patch of her sea in moonlight, three-quarters on, sails furled (set, if they're crystal-weave), animated while it's shown, at a whole number of screen pixels to hers. Hull sheets now load for any ship drawn (a pirate raider's own, not the sloop's stretched). Test: `combat/shipviz.js`.
- **At the pier** (`fleetAt`): the sloop lies across the pier's end (MOORINGS); each bigger ship of yours lies in line with her off one end or the other (or rafted outside if the shore's too close), so every ship casts off straight out. Boarding walks you down the pier, across the sloop and aboard (`wayAboard`, lifted onto each deck); arriving reverses it. Lying there they take the island's own light — its day and dusk over the moonlit sheets as its render has them (`drawMooredOn`, `BridgeScene.sky`), so they're not night-dark at a sunlit pier. In the passage the rest of your ships lie at every island you pass (`I.fleet`), and you can't sail through them.
- **The harbourmasters** (`js/harbour.js`, BridgeHarbour): one at the head of each pier but Midnight's (`PORTS`, added as the world loads; people.json `bess`, `aldous`, `mira`, `garro` — sheets `npc-hm-*` in `characters.py`). **The Midnight Isle has no harbour** (Beck's call): the Ferryman (`ferryman`, `npc-ferryman`, label THE FERRYMAN, `ferry: true`) keeps its pier and a trade of his own (`ferry()`): moonglass (the harpoon and the moon lantern), moon salt into a hold, the drowned who'd sail again, the hold — no yard, repairs, contracts or charts. The harbourmasters' *The harbour book* topic (`harbour: true`) opens: your ships (take one out, rename, sell to a yard, move or pay off hands), **the yard** (each port builds its own: cutters on Diggen's Point, brigantines and galleons at Sapphire's Great Yard, the raider on Crimson's pirate slip), **fit out** (`GUNS` for her slots — swivel, long nine, carronade, bow chaser, the Ferryman's moonglass harpoon — and `FITS` fitted once each: copper sheathing, oak doubling, Rose's crystal-weave sails and roseite sheathing, Crimson's storm rigging, Midnight's moon lantern; and your own parts from the workshop, fitted for the yard's labour), **repairs**, **sign on hands**, **the hold**, **market** and **contracts**.
- **Hands** (`recruits`): three people a day per port looking for a berth, from that sea (names, roles and skill by the date): deckhands, gunners (a gun with a gunner reloads twice as fast), navigators (quicker), riggers (handier, safer in storms), fighters (boarding), pursers (better prices). Each is graded by skill — *green*, *able*, *old hand* (`GRADES`) — and the grade is what they do, in numbers (`perk(role, skill)`, shown in the list: a deckhand 4/8/12% less harm in a squall, a gunner's gun twice as fast and 8% more per grade past green, a navigator 3% quicker a grade, a rigger 5% handier and 10% safer in squalls a grade, a fighter worth 1.5/2/2.5 in a boarding, a purser 5/7/9% on cargo prices; the best of each role aboard counts, `crewBest`). The list shows each with a face (`handFace`, js/harbour.js: the name-seeded portrait in the role's colour), their role and grade, the perk, and the signing fee + their daily wage (`wage`, a quarter of the fee; paid on the business payroll each ET midnight; two missed paydays and they go ashore). They live aboard one ship (`berths` = hands she needs + 2); a ship short of hands won't leave the pier (you're offered the sloop instead).
- **Cargo** (`js/trade.js`, BridgeTrade): goods ride in a ship's hold by the crate, never your pack — salt fish, spore-lamp oil and pine timber from Diggen's Point, Sapphire glass, Rose spore silk and roseite, Crimson ember rum and black powder, and the Ferryman's rare moon salt. Each port makes some cheap and wants others dear (`MARKET`), prices drift by the day and sag where you've just sold a lot (`glut`). What you mine, catch and gather goes in the hold too (`HAUL`, several to a crate) and sells at market. **Contracts**: two a day per port, carry so many crates of a good to another port by a day for a fee well over market; handed over when you open that port's book.
- **The journal** has a *Your fleet* section: every ship, where she lies (or how long till she's home from a voyage), what she carries, your hands, the contracts you've taken, the holm's strongbox. Markets have **depth**: a big deal moves the price (`BridgeTrade.price(id, port, side, n)`, `depth`), so a hold of one good sells for less each, and `bestRun` finds the cargo size that pays best.
- **Trouble at sea** (`js/seafight.js`, BridgeSeaFight, given an `api` into the passage by sloop.js): out in open water (a roll every 150 s of it, `openT`/`troubleAt`; the fight sees the stretch ahead through `stretch()`) the sea may bring pirates (now and then on the Emerald, more on the Sapphire and Rose, often on the Crimson, and more the richer your hold) or, on the Midnight Sea, a drowned ship — or one of its **monsters** (below). Pirates hail you: **pay them off** (a share of the hold's worth, or a third of the hold if you can't), **fight** (guns fire by themselves when loaded and bearing — side guns abeam, a chaser ahead; theirs too, so sail to keep them on your beam), or **run** (out-sail them and they give up; let them close and they board — fighters decide it). Beaten, they strike: coins and their cargo. The drowned don't hail; a moon lantern keeps them off (and thins the mist), moonglass bites them twice as hard. **Firing** (Beck: "seeing cannons fire… animations and making things feel cool"): the guns that bear go off together as a **broadside**, one after another down her side from stern to bow (`salvo`, ~85 ms apart; the first time a target bears every loaded gun's in it). Each shot leaves its gun's own mouth (`BridgeShipKit.muzzle`) — the gun jumps back, a flash at its mouth — and smoke rolls out of it (`smoke`: billows thrown out, slowing, swelling, rising, drifting downwind with the wind, thinning in steps; moonlit grey-blue at night, whiter by day, green off ghostfire; each size of billow drawn once and kept, `billow`, soft at the edge with a checkerboard ring, `soft`); the shot flies a high arc with a short trail behind it (smoke behind a ball, green fire behind ghostfire, pale light behind a moonglass bolt). Where it comes down (`land`, `burst`): a miss throws up a column of white water with a crown that falls back into itself, drops and a spreading ring of foam; a hit flashes and throws splinters tumbling and sparks with tails, and a puff of dark soot; ghostfire bursts green, moonglass pale blue; a monster's hide as before. The Midnight Sea's monsters throw up the same splash, bigger. The crew sees it all (`sf` shots carry the gun, its side and whose ship). The hull bar shows top right; a hit shakes the view. **The Crimson's squalls** are deadly to a small boat: you're warned, and if you're carrying full sail when it hits she's knocked flat (hull damage × her `storm`); ease the sheet and she rides it out; now and then lightning takes the masthead. **Midnight's rocks** hole her (× `rocks`). A hull battered to nothing founders: salvors tow her into the nearer port with a harbourmaster (`BridgeFleet.wreck`; never Midnight — the Ferryman mends nothing), her hold lost, to be mended at the harbourmaster's. Test hooks: `BridgeSeaFight._spawn(kind)`, `_foes()`, `_harpoon()`, `_burst(kind, x, r, big)`.
- **The Midnight Sea's monsters** (js/seafight.js `KINDS` with `monster`; Beck: "there should be monsters you have to battle", only in the Midnight Sea, not right at its edge): every 75 s of open water out there a roll (`CHANCE.midnight`), but only once you've been in it 35 s (`MID_WAIT`) and it's all round you 26 tiles out (`deepIn`, `MID_DEEP`); sail out of it and they give you up (`outT`). What comes (`deep()`): **the Gloamserpent** (90 hp) circles under you (a long pale shape and a wake), surfaces in coils (`coil`: the time to hit it), rears (`rear`, a hiss, the strike aimed where you'll be) and lunges — sail hard and its jaws close on empty water — then dives; **wisp eels** (three, 14 hp each) porpoise in, latch onto the hull and gnaw (1 a bite) till you shoot them off or shake them off with speed (over 3 for 1.3 s); **the Deep Maw** (260 hp, rare): a vast shadow under you, then its arms come up round the hull — gripped, you crawl (speed 0.7) — and one at a time rear and slam down where you are; its great eye surfaces a few lengths off: 30 damage breaks the grip; after 15 s it sounds and comes again. A drowned ship is the fourth thing out there. **Every boat carries a hand harpoon** (`HARPOON`: 9, 6.5 tiles, 1.1 s; F, or HARPOON on touch — `#sf-fire`), so a sloop with no guns can fight; guns fire at them too, but nothing hits one that's under (`o.under`); moonglass bites them half again. A kill pays coins and trophies into your pack (`trophies`: Gloamserpent scales `gloam_scale`, Abyss Ink `abyss_ink` and now and then a Deep Pearl `deep_pearl`, js/items.js) — the Ferryman buys them dear (his *Scales and ink?* topic). Drawn in code on the sloop's native buffer (`drawSerpent`, `drawEel`, `krakenItems`: pixel balls with an outline, shadow side and light — `ball` — along each body's trail, `body()`; the Maw's arms as tubes of them, sorted with the boats by row). Sailing together, the crew sees them (`sf` carries each monster's state, timers and aim) and the mate's swivel works on them; a kill gives the crew their share and trophies of their own. The dory fishermen have rumours of all three. Sounds: `roar`, `hiss`, `harpoon`. Test: `combat/monsters.js`.

### Sending your ships anywhere
Beck: "command your ships to be able to sail from anywhere… one of my ships is stuck". `BridgeFleet.order(id, isle)` sends any ship but the sloop (she goes where you go) to any island from wherever you are: tap her on the ledger's **Your fleet** page or the chart table in the captain's office, or **Send her to…** on her card in any harbourmaster's book or the Ferryman's (**Your ships** is in his book too — Midnight's ships can be taken out). She's away on a passage (`away: { passage, to, back }`) for a while by the distance (`passage()`: 12 + 50 × chart distance minutes, by her speed, half as long again short of hands), then lies at her new island (`arrivals()`, every 20 s and on load, with a toast). The holm's trading voyages leave passages alone. Ships left at Midnight Isle while it had no way to take them out were sailed home once, to the holm if you have it (`rescue`, `st.rescued`). Test: `world/orders.js`.

### The capital, the small islands and your own (Lumar)
Three more places on the Emerald Sea (`ISLES` in js/voyage.js; moorings in js/sloop.js `MOORINGS`):
- **Crownhaven** (`lumar-crown`, `scenes/lumar_crown.py`): the capital, the biggest island on the seas. A long straight quay (your pier and the fishing jetty off it, bollards, cargo, lamps), warehouses and the harbour office, the Crown & Anchor; Market Row behind the waterfront; up the avenue the grand square (the fountain, Captain Lumar's statue, market stalls under bunting, trees in beds), the **Merchants' Exchange** (columns, pediment, a gilt clock) and **St Brendan's** (nave, glowing rose window, bell tower); the **palace** above it all behind its curtain wall (drum-towered gatehouse, keep, banners). East, the **Royal Yard**: a galleon in frame on the stocks (`ship_in_frame`: keel blocks, ribs, half her planking, staging) under sheer-legs. West, the Crown Light on the point. The harbourmistress **Rowena Ashgrove** (`npc-hm-rowena`) runs the only yard that builds galleons. The **Exchange board** (an `exchange` interaction, `BridgeHarbour.exchange`) chalks up every good's cheapest buy and dearest sell across the ports today, and the day's best runs (`BridgeTrade.routes`); **Sergeant Hale** and a second guard stand either side of the palace gate, and **Agnes Rook**, the Royal Yard's master shipwright (`wright`), explains how the ships differ. Lay new things out with room round them — nothing in front of a door, a lane between buildings (Beck's call, after a stall on Tern Holm stood on a cottage's step).
- **Crownhaven's rooms** (doors on the street, `door()` in `scenes/lumar_crown.py`; what they do is `js/crown.js`, BridgeCrown): **the Crown & Anchor** (`lumar-crown-pub`, `scenes/lumar_crown_pub.py`) — Hetty Marsh's long bar, the painted chart of the five seas between harbour windows, the Sovereign's anchor over the fire and her wheel beside it, her figurehead (the Landlady) in the corner, ships in bottles over the booth; tables of sailors; **Bones** by the fire with Salt Annie and Cutter Joss (liar's dice — js/lodge.js now has `TABLES`: an interaction's `table` picks the players, the words and the panel's look, `fell` at the lodge, `anchor` here in dark oak); the bosun's table by the door (`hands`: the port's hands looking for a berth, no harbour book needed). **The Merchants' Exchange** (`lumar-crown-exchange`): green-and-cream marble, two colonnades, the great board across the back wall under a gilt clock (`exchange`), the brass trading ring, brokers walking the floor (`npc-broker`, walkers.json), the samples of every good and the scales; the **Charter Office** (`charters`): three long hauls a day (`BridgeTrade.charters`: one a cutter can carry, one for a brigantine, one only a galleon will take; 3-4 days; delivered like any contract at the far port's harbourmaster). **The Harbour Office** (`lumar-crown-office`): a counter behind brass grilles — the Registry window (`registry`: your ships, `BridgeHarbour.registry`), the Chart window (Pim, `charts`: buy sea charts), the Office's own pinned chart of the five seas (`sea_chart` opens yours), the tide clock, the Meridian's model in her case, unclaimed cargo. **St Brendan's** (`lumar-crown-chapel`): pews down a flagged aisle, the great window (a ship under a star) over the altar, a votive ship hanging over the aisle, the memorial board of ships the sea kept, the font, the bell rope (`chapel_bell`) and the **candles for the missing** (`chapel_candle`, 5 coins): your next passage is blessed — squalls and Midnight's rocks do half the harm and pirates come half as often (`BridgeCrown.takeBlessing`, read as she casts off; `api.blessed` in js/seafight.js). Mother Coralie keeps it (the `chapel` organ theme). People: `hetty`, `annie`, `joss`, `bosun`, `registrar` (Ambrose Vane), `quill`, `wynn`, `pim`, `coralie` (people.json, gifts.js).
- **The palace** (`lumar-crown-palace`, `scenes/lumar_crown_palace.py`): the PALACE GATE (`palace_gate`) opens for a captain with three ships or more (Sergeant Hale says so). The throne room: dark polished stone, a red carpet between standing candelabra to a dais of three steps, the gilt throne under the royal arms (a crown over an anchor), green banners, tall windows on the harbour, Captain Lumar's armour either side, the Admiralty table with the Crown's chart and model ships (`commissions`: where you stand), the captains' benches. The **Queen-Regent** (`npc-queen`, a seated sheet with a crown — `characters.py` `crown`; scene actors take `lift`, tiles of height, to sit on a dais, and `light`, the tile they're lit from) gives audience (`audience`) and three **royal commissions** in turn (`BridgeCrown.COMMISSIONS`): *the Crown's Salt* (12 crates of moon salt to Rowena on the quay — a royal contract, `royal: true`, fee 0, paid at the palace), *the Crimson Seam* (beat three pirate ships — `BridgeCrown.noteWin` from js/seafight.js), *Show the Flag* (bring a galleon of your own into Crownhaven). Each pays (3000 / 4000 / 6000) and leaves something for good: a royal **warrant** (the Exchange's charters pay a fifth more), a **letter of marque** (pirates beaten pay double), and the title **Captain of the Crown** (your ships fly a gold pennant over green, `drawBoat`; yards mend them for a quarter less, `repairCost`). Lord Steward Bellamy (`npc-steward`, `steward`) keeps the book; the journal's fleet section shows the commission in hand.
- **Tern Holm** (`lumar-tern`, `scenes/lumar_tern.py`): a fishing village round a shingle cove — whitewashed cottages, the Tern's Rest, a chapel with a lantern in its tower for the boats, the smokehouse, nets on poles, drying racks, boats drawn up. **Old Pell** keeps the pier (people.json `tern_pell`, `npc-hm-pell`); **Maud Tull** at the fish stall buys your catch. Salt fish is cheapest here. **The Tern's Rest** (`lumar-tern-inn`, `scenes/lumar_tern_inn.py`) opens: Bryony Kettle (`bryony`, `npc-bryony`) and her stew (the pot hung in a deep inglenook, settles either side — sit there), a counter of blue-and-white plates, the fishermen's long table (`npc-fisher-a/b`), the record silverfin on its board, the window on the cove, and the spare room (sleep, free).
- **Starling Holm** (`lumar-holm`, `scenes/lumar_holm.py`, `js/holm.js`, BridgeHolm; localStorage `bridge_holm`): **an island of your own**, tucked away south-east of the capital. FOR SALE on the post at the old jetty (the deed, 2500); then build it up a piece at a time from its ledger — **the cottage** (a ruin until then: rest by the fire, the ledger on the table), **the dock** (new planking, lamps, a crane, your flag — and the holm becomes a home port), **the garden** (the brambles cleared inside the old wall: `farm.plot`, farmed with js/farm.js — which now keys tiles by world, `"<world>|x,y"` off Verdance), **a warehouse** (cargo ashore between ships), **quarters** (hands ashore between voyages, `ship: 'holm'`), **the workshop** (once the dock's rebuilt: see *The Workshop* below). Each is a scene variant (`k.variant(slot, key)`; holm.js sets `scene.layers`, the chimneys' smoke and, for the two lots, their walls — `holm.lots` in the world JSON — as the island loads). Variants only ever build on ground or the old pier's own footprint: the water's mask (`shore.png`) comes from the base render. **Voyages**: with the dock built, a ship based here with her hands aboard can be sent out on one of the day's best runs (`routes()`: buy where it's made, sell where it's wanted, from js/trade.js prices); she's away a while (`ship.away`) and comes home with the profit in the strongbox — or a story: on the Crimson and Midnight runs pirates may come at her, and guns and fighters decide it. Or a **circuit** (`BridgeTrade.circuits`: the best run, then the best run on from where she lands, and a third leg if it pays): she trades her way round two or three ports — about twice a single run's profit for one send, a little quicker than sailing the legs one by one (`× 0.85`), every leg rolling its own trouble (`away.legs`). Name the island what you like (it renames it on the chart). **The cottage, rebuilt, is a home**: its door leads inside (`lumar-holm-home`, `scenes/lumar_holm_home.py`: whitewash over a sea-green wainscot, the hearth with your first sloop's model on the mantel — *rest* there — windows on the cove, the table with your ledger, a sea chest, nets and floats, an oar), furnished with js/home.js like the White House (`holm` in HOMES: owned once `BridgeHolm.built('house')`; the joiners leave a bed and an armchair).

### Crownhaven, the capital of districts (Lumar)
Beck: "making the capital city a lot bigger and cooler… each one should feel large but connected and unique" (Denser
Worlds, Workstream A; `_claude_docs/spec-denser-worlds.md`). The island (`lumar-crown`: the quay, the grand square, the
Exchange, St Brendan's, the palace gate, the Royal Yard) is the middle of the city; its districts are worlds of their own
joined by streets you walk, each built in the house style from one kit, `tools/bridge-art/assets_capital.py`
(`town_house` — a tall jettied townhouse: coursed stone or plaster below, oversailing timber-framed floors above,
shuttered windows with flower boxes, shop windows under striped awnings, gable fronts for the merchants; `row` — a run
of them, each its own, `jitter` stepping their fronts so a lane winds; `town_wall`, `wall_ns`, `tower`, `gatehouse`;
`yard_life` — the back yards glimpsed past the roofs and down the alleys; `arch_house` over an alley; `chapel`;
`theatre`; `canal`, `humpback`, `barge`, `gondola`, `mooring_pole`; `ropewalk`, `treadwheel_crane`, `hull_in_frame`,
`cannon`, `shot_pyramid`, `anchor`, `timber_stack`; `boardwalk`, `shack`; `washing`, `balustrade`, `flagged`, `lane`).
Every street is laid out in bands — a lane, the strip of yards behind the next row (hidden by its roofs, never walkable)
and that row of houses facing the camera — because at 45° a row of houses hides about two rows of ground behind it: keep
lanes three rows or more and the houses south of a lane mostly two storeys.
- **The Old Town** (`lumar-crown-oldtown`, `scenes/lumar_crown_oldtown.py`): the walled town on the point west of the
  harbour, the oldest stones in the city. Five rows of tall houses with lanes between that jog and pinch, alleys through
  each row staggered so the way down winds, washing strung across them, the Arch House over one; the Old Keep in the
  north-west corner; the Lantern Theatre on the north lane (`js/capital.js`: a different play every Lumar evening, a
  ticket, the play in three cards, a lift the first time each day); Well Square with the old well, two limes, a stall and
  Barnaby Toll the town crier (`crier`, `npc-crier`); St Elmo's chapel and its spire; Harbour Lane along the sea wall with
  the fish market, nets and pots, and the Sea Steps down to a landing stage; round towers on the corners. Ways: the LAND
  GATE (east) into Crownhaven, the NORTH GATE to the canal quarter, the BRIDGE GATE (south-east) onto the Old Bridge.
- **The canal quarter and Guild Row** (`lumar-crown-canals`, `scenes/lumar_crown_canals.py`): two canals off the harbour,
  each with a quay on its north side and houses fronting it — gable-fronted merchant houses with hoist beams on
  Merchants' Quay (Ashgrove & Vane's counting house), the guilds' working shops on Guild Row: Rigg & Daughters,
  sailmakers (`rigg`, `npc-sailmaker`: sailcloth and rope for your workshop — js/workshop.js `SHOPS.rigg`), Penn the
  chartmaker (`penn`, `npc-chartmaker`: the sea charts), Brassworth's instruments (`brassworth`, `npc-instrument`: the
  **Ship's Chronometer**, the one timepiece that keeps Lumar's hours — js/gear.js `TIMES.chronometer`, shop
  `brassworth`), the Guild Hall; humpbacked bridges, barges and canal boats at striped poles, the Canal Market by the
  water stairs; the funicular's lower station under the hill. Ways: the SOUTH GATE (the Old Town), the FUNICULAR up.
- **The Hill** (`lumar-crown-hill`, `scenes/lumar_crown_hill.py`): the palace's garden front and its dome, the palace
  gardens (parterres of clipped box, a fountain, topiary, statues), the Royal Naval Academy on its parade ground with the
  training mast, the Observatory under its green copper dome (the terrace telescope looks at the stars), the lime walk
  and the Belvedere over the drop to the city's roofs, the funicular's top station and car. The GARDEN DOOR is a
  `palace_gate` (js/crown.js decides who may go in, as at the front gate).
- **The Old Bridge** (`lumar-crown-bridge`, `scenes/lumar_crown_bridge.py`): Crownhaven's Ponte Vecchio over the harbour
  mouth — four arches on cutwater piers, a row of shops and houses along its north side hung out over the harbour on
  brackets (goldsmiths, pies, lenses, a bookbinder…), booths along the south parapet and the loggia in the middle with
  the builder in bronze. Ways: WEST GATE (the Old Town), EAST GATE (the Arsenal).
- **The Arsenal** (`lumar-crown-arsenal`, `scenes/lumar_crown_arsenal.py`): the Royal Yard grown into the Crown's
  dockyard — the ropewalk the width of the yard, the sail loft, the gun foundry (its furnace glowing through the door,
  its stack smoking), the mast house, the Arsenal office and its clock; oak seasoning, guns, shot, anchors; No. 1 Dock
  with a man-of-war in frame on her blocks; the Sovereign II on the stocks under sheer-legs; the fitting-out quay with a
  warship alongside and a treadwheel crane. Ways: the YARD GATE (Crownhaven's east), the BRIDGE ROAD (the Old Bridge).
- **The Stilts** (`lumar-crown-stilts`, `scenes/lumar_crown_stilts.py`): the slums under the quays, down the QUAY STAIRS —
  the quay wall towering at the back with families in its old vaults, shacks of odd planks and tin on stilts over the
  water joined by boardwalks and a rope bridge, paper lanterns strung between, the mudlark and her heap of finds, the
  Undertow (a tavern on an old barge), boats tied up everywhere.
- **The doors** are `js/capital.js` (BridgeCapital), never the island's own scene or world JSON (another session rebuilds
  those): `BUILT` lists the districts on main — a way into one that isn't is left a closed `dialog` with a word on why;
  `GATES` turns a district's labelled way into a door once the far side exists; `CROWN` adds Crownhaven's own three (THE
  OLD TOWN on its west edge, THE ARSENAL on its east, QUAY STAIRS on the quay), each placed on the walkable tile its
  `find` scores best in the island's collisions as it loads, so a re-render of the island moves them with it; `BACK`
  points each district's way home at that tile. Re-run `python3 tools/bridge-art/atlas.py` after adding one (its
  `EXTRA` lists Crownhaven's).

### The Sapphire and Rose seas: Lanternfall, the floating market, Rosewell (Lumar)
Workstream B1 of the denser worlds (`_claude_docs/spec-denser-worlds.md`; Beck: "the Emerald, Sapphire and Rose seas are your different places… each of their people and cities should probably be a little bit more unique"). What each sea's places do is `js/sapphire.js` (BridgeSapphire, interaction types `lanternfall` and `sapphire`) and `js/rose.js` (BridgeRose, type `rose`).
- **Lanternfall, the cliff town** (`lumar-cliff`, `scenes/lumar_cliff.py`, kit `tools/bridge-art/assets_cliff.py`; on the chart in the Sapphire's south, `ISLES` 0.68 × 0.90; `MOORINGS`, `EXT`, `MOUTH`, `GROUNDS` and a merchant lane from Sapphire Port in js/sloop.js): Santorini on Lumar — a drowned volcano whose rim stands out of the sea as a crescent, the town stacked along the top of the caldera's inner wall on three terraces (`scene.heights`: the rim at z 9, the upper terrace 7.5, the promenade 6), white houses with flat roofs and barrel vaults, Oia's ochres and rose among them, blue doors and shutters, the great church's three blue domes and its bell wall, windmills on the crest, cypresses and an old olive, bougainvillea, pots of geraniums, paving in white grout; a banded cliff (rust, black, ochre, pumice) falling to the old port with cave houses dug into it, café balconies jutting out over the drop, and **the zigzag**: four legs of white-edged steps across the face, each with its whitewashed wall and a ledge before it, a lantern at every turn, down to the quay — the fish taverna under its vine, boathouse arches in the rock, caiques, the donkeys; out in the caldera a black lava islet with a glowing vent (steam) and a little chapel. **What it does** (`lanternfall`): Yannis's **donkeys** ride you up or down the zigzag for 5 coins (`donkey_up` / `donkey_down`); the red gondolas of the **cable car** down the face (`cable_up` / `cable_down`, 3 coins); **Despina's taverna** on the rim and **Marina's** on the quay serve dishes eaten at the table (health and energy); a **lamp in the great church** (2 coins) keeps the cold off for an hour (`BridgeHoarfell.warm`); the **bell**; the **lantern maker** sells a caldera lantern (an item); **the view** from the promenade, different by the planet's part of the day — at dusk the sunset, once a day, lifts you. People (`people.json`, persona-free): Marina, Yannis, Despina, Father Ilias, Aris the painter (`npc-cliff-*`). A crowd in the Sapphire wardrobe, walkers (one climbs the zigzag), fishing off the jetty and the quay.
- **The floating market** (`lumar-sapphire-market`, `scenes/lumar_sapphire_market.py`): through the gate at the east end of Sapphire Port's quay (`BridgeSapphire.decorate` adds it as Sapphire Port loads — `MARKET_GATE`, 44, 22 — so a re-render of Sapphire Port never loses it). The old inner basin: two barges for squares, trader boats lashed all round them under striped awnings, a plank walk out from the quay and a bridge between, masts strung with paper lanterns, the breakwater's two blue lamps. Six traders who buy what you find (`sell` topics, a little over the other ports in their trade): **Nerys** fish, **Kassia** crystals and ore, **Old Doran** curios and pearls, **Lyra** sea glass (the glassworks melts it), **Theo** farm honey, eggs and milk, **Melina** fruit and the farm's crops (`npc-market-*`). Busiest at night. On the quay the glass shop (`vendor`: glass floats, caldera lanterns, panes) and **the Sapphire Glassworks** under its brick cone (`lumar-sapphire-glassworks`, `scenes/lumar_sapphire_glassworks.py`): the furnace's three glory holes, benches, the marver, the lehr, shelves of blue glass; Master Stavrou and Ione (`npc-glass-master`, `npc-glassblower`). **Blowing glass** at the pipe (`glassblow`): three moments — gather, blow, shape — each a needle swinging through the glass's heat, Space / E / a tap in the band (dead centre perfect); six is a pair of flawless floats, four or five a float, two or three a lopsided one back into the cullet (sea glass), less a blue glass potato. The first go each day is free, then 5 coins (`_glass()` for tests).
- **Rosewell, the bathhouse town** (`lumar-rose-springs`, `scenes/lumar_rose_springs.py`): up the path from Rose Cove's east side (`BridgeRose.decorate`, `SPRINGS_PATH`, 52, 22). The springs step down the hill through terraces of travertine — white and blushing, scalloped lips, cascades — each a row of steaming pools with bathers in them up to their shoulders (seated actors sunk by `scene.heights`); the two by the stair you can wade into yourself (`soak`: a little energy and warm for half an hour, a proper soak once a day). Along the top the **silk groves**: silk trees with the moths' cocoons glowing in them, spore silk drying on lines, the weavers' shed — gather the ripe cocoons once a day (`silk` → `silk_cocoon`) and Seren weaves five into a **spore silk scarf** (`looms` → `silk_scarf`, a gift) or buys them. Mother Amaryllis at the foot of the terraces reads the steam (`reading`, 4 coins: a true tip said strangely, into the journal). **The bathhouse** (`lumar-rose-bathhouse`): the great round bath sunk in a floor of the glassworks' blue tiles (wade in down its steps), the bronze fish, columns, Oda's counter; **the bath** (`bath`, 12 coins) rests you to full and keeps the cold out for two hours. **The tea house** on its stilts over the lowest pool (`lumar-rose-teahouse`): mats, low tables with cushions to sit at, the sunken hearth and Master Lior's **pots** (`tea`): rosehip (energy), emberleaf (warm an hour and a half), moonleaf (health). People: Seren, Mother Amaryllis, Oda, Master Lior, two bathers (`npc-rose-*`).
- **The seas' people look their own**: wardrobes `sapphire` (whitewash and deep blue, gold sashes, the glassblowers' aprons) and `rose` (plum, rose and cream, robes, now and then pink or violet hair) in js/crowd.js, on four new crowd bodies (`characters.py` EXTRAS: `merchant` in a waistcoat and sash, `woman-shawl`, and the robed `mystic` / `mystic-m`), each with its lines and wants (`crowds.json`); Sapphire Port, Lanternfall and the market dress Sapphire, Rose Cove and Rosewell dress Rose.
- Test: `tools/tests/world/seas_b1.js` (every place loads and every interaction can be reached, the ways in from Sapphire Port and Rose Cove, the heights, what each place does, shots by day, at golden hour and by night: `shots/b1_*.png`).

### The Workshop: materials, smiths and making things (Lumar)
Beck: "move toward crafting… more materials and mining… you can buy upgrades at certain places, but you have to go there with your ship, or take it to your courtyard and hire smiths… progression should feel natural". The plan is `_claude_docs/spec-empire.md`, chunk 1. `js/workshop.js` (BridgeWorkshop; localStorage `bridge_workshop`).
- **The building**: `workshop` in the holm's `BUILD` (9,000, `needs: 'dock'`), built from the holm's ledger or the WORKSHOP interaction on its lot by the well (`LOTS['workshop'] = (10, 6, 15, 9)` in `scenes/lumar_holm.py`; door at (13, 9)): an open forge under its chimney and a timber shop (`k.variant('workshop', 'built')`). It's in the holm's render now (the forge under the roof's west wing, glowing at night). Before it was, js/holm.js stood the quarters' bunkhouse on the lot (`BridgeScene.standIn(id, slot, key, [fromSlot, fromKey], dx, dy)` in js/scene.js — another variant's art and depth moved by whole tiles, used only while the scene has no layer of its own for that slot/key) and added the lot (`holm.lots.workshop`) and the interaction if the world JSON lacked them; that's still how a building can go in before its render does.
- **Materials**, each with a place to get it (`FROM`, said wherever you're short): **pine logs** (WINDFALL PINE forage on Diggen's Point and on the holm, Bess Tolliver sells them; a crate of pine timber cargo is eight), **oak logs** (the LOG PILE on Verdance, OAK OFFCUTS at the Royal Yard, Agnes Rook sells them), **copper ore** (out of the Rose Mine's iron and silver rock, Hob's cart), iron ore (the mine), **flax** (a crop: flax seed at every stall that sells carrot seed — `decorate` adds it; grows on Verdance or in the holm's garden, `CROPS.flax` in js/farm.js), **glass** (Aldous on Sapphire; a crate of Sapphire glass is six), **sailcloth** (Mira on Rose Cove; a crate of spore silk is four bolts; or woven from flax), **black powder** (Garro on Crimson, by the horn; a keg is six), **moonglass** (the Ferryman, three a day) — and what's made from them: **planks**, **oak planks**, **iron bars**, **copper sheet**, **rope**, **pitch**. Plus what's already found: roseite, sapphire and midnight crystals, star iron, songstone, Gloamserpent scales, moon salt. Items in js/items.js with their icons (`logIcon`, `plankIcon`, `ingotIcon`, …), a **Materials** set in the Collection, `HAUL` entries in js/trade.js (several to a crate in a hold, sold anywhere at their worth). Salvage: flotsam hauled aboard is now and then a ship's stores (rope, planks, a bar, sheet, pitch, sailcloth — `salvage` from js/sloop.js `haul`).
- **Where the workshop takes them from**: your pack first, then the holm's store (`BridgeHolm.stored/storeAdd/storeTake` — the warehouse's store, shared), then the holds of your ships at the jetty (the sloop too when you're on the holm), breaking open a crate of cargo when it has to (`CRATES`; what's left over goes on the racks). The inputs are taken when the work starts.
- **Recipes** (`RECIPES`, on the clock: `mins` is an apprentice's real minutes): materials (planks, oak planks, iron bars, copper sheet, pitch, rope, sailcloth; moonglass from glass, a midnight crystal and moon salt), **guns** (swivel, long nine, carronade, bow chaser, moonglass harpoon — js/fleet.js `GUNS`), **fittings** (oak doubling, copper and roseite sheathing, crystal-weave sails, storm rigging, the moon lantern — `FITS`; and two no yard sells, `craft: true`: **ironwood doubling** (hull ×1.6) and **gloamscale sheathing** (quicker, rocks do less than half), each `replaces` what it betters), **tools** (an iron pickaxe; the star pickaxe; the **prospector's pick** — finds a piece more, and plain rock now and then hides the depth's best; the **greenheart rod** — carried, a quicker bite, a bigger catch zone, rare fish a little likelier), and **for your homes** (oak table, old trunk, crystal lamp, telescope: straight to your furnishing tray, `BridgeHome.givePiece`). All cost far less in materials than a yard's price, and take time instead.
- **Who makes them**: **your bench** works one thing at a time, only what you set on it, at your pace (half an apprentice's, +1% a piece to 80%), and carries on while you're away. **Smiths** (three benches) are hired at the workshop from three a day (an apprentice ×1, a journeyman ×1.5 "now and then better", a master ×2.2 who won't come till the workshop's made eight things), each a face, a grade and a wage, through `BridgeBusiness.engage(place, cand)` (business.js: staff at a place that isn't a business, `at: 'workshop'`, on the same payroll — a day's wage up front, unpaid two paydays and they walk; unpaid at all and they down tools). They work the **queue** by themselves, wherever you are; the better ones now and then make a piece more, or save the offcuts. `advance()` works it all out from the clock a minute at a time (up to a fortnight back), every 30 s while there's work, and whenever anything reads it. What's made goes **on the racks** — the holm's store — guns and fittings as **parts** (`part_<id>` items).
- **Fitting what you made**: take the part off the racks into your pack (or load it into a ship's hold from the warehouse) and any harbourmaster but the Ferryman fits it for the yard's labour (a sixth or so of their price): js/harbour.js *Fit out* lists your own parts, in your pack or her hold (`partsFor`, `usePart`), then `BridgeFleet.mount(shipId, what)` (no price; `canFit` says slots / fitted / better). For your own slipway (chunk 2): `BridgeWorkshop.parts()` (what's on the racks), `takePart(what)` and `BridgeFleet.mount`.
- **Plans** (`plan: true` recipes): known from the start are the materials, the swivel gun, oak doubling, the iron pickaxe and the home pieces; the rest are learnt from people with a `workshop: <key>` topic in assets/people.json (`SHOPS`: what they sell, the plans they draw for coin, the favours they teach for): **Agnes Rook** at the Royal Yard (oak; long nine, bow chaser, copper sheathing), **Hob** (copper ore; star pickaxe, and the prospector's pick once you've been down to floor 15), **Garro** (powder; carronade, storm rigging), **Mira Quell** (sailcloth; roseite sheathing — and crystal-weave sails for four sapphire crystals, her loom's weights), **the Ferryman** (moonglass; the moon lantern and harpoon — and moonglass-grinding for a star pearl, gloamscale sheathing for a jar of Abyss Ink), **Aldous** (glass, copper sheet), **Bess** (pine, pitch, rope), **Old Pell** (rope; the greenheart rod), **Ilsa Venn** (the telescope). Or found, as `plan_<id>` items you read to learn (`onUse`): ironwood doubling under a rock below floor 8 of the Rose Mine (`maybeFind`), the greenheart rod in a fishing chest (`chestFind`), now and then a plan in flotsam. A favour done warms them to you (`BridgeTalk.nudge`).
- **Seeing it**: the workshop's own screens (`open`: your bench, *Make something*, the queue, smiths, the store, plans); a line on the holm's ledger; the game menu's **Ledger** — the workshop under *Your places* with each job, who's on it and when it's done, smiths under *Staff & payroll*, and *Needs you* for things made since you last looked and smiths standing idle. `BridgeWorkshop.summary()` is the one read for all of them (and the Captain's Office): `{ built, line, jobs: [{ id, recipe, name, by, working, left, done }], bench, queued, working, smiths, idle, made, next, log, since }`; `queue(recipe, n)` starts work from anywhere. Test: `world/workshop.js`.

### Your own shipyard: the slipway (Lumar)
Beck: "build your own ship from enough timber and iron… progression should feel natural". The empire plan's chunk 2 (`_claude_docs/spec-empire.md`). `js/shipyard.js` (BridgeShipyard; localStorage `bridge_shipyard`).
- **The slipway**: `slipway` in the holm's `BUILD` (12,000, `needs: 'workshop'` — the timber and iron are worked there first), built from the holm's ledger or the SLIPWAY interaction at the head of the old slip below the garden wall (`SLIPWAY_AT` (10, 19) in js/holm.js). The holm's render builds it (`tools/bridge-art/assets_yard.py`, called from `scenes/lumar_holm.py`): the old slip's bed is in the base render — weathered planks on piles running down into the water, the old ways along them, the shore's rocks and weed cleared off it (`clear_parts`) — because the sea's mask comes from the base render and anything a variant built over water would have the sea drawn over it; `slipway` old | built adds the rebuilt slip on it (`slip_new`: fresh sleepers, two oak ground ways greased along their tops, chocks at the foot, sheer-legs over the head with a block and tackle and guys pegged out behind) and a seasoning stack of planks by the garden wall (`lumber`); and the hull on it, `hull` keel | frame | planked (`hull_on_slip`: keel blocks and shores, the keel, stem and sternpost; then ribs, ribbands and staging; then planked sides with a wale, deck beams, half her deck laid and the rudder hung — `BridgeShipyard.stage()`, set as the island loads).
- **Hull plans** from the yard that builds each kind (`HULLS[type].sold`, listed in that person's `workshop` shop — js/workshop.js asks `planRows`): the cutter's from Bess on Diggen's Point (900), the brigantine's from Aldous at Sapphire (4,500), the raider's from Garro (6,500), the galleon's from Agnes Rook at the Royal Yard (16,000).
- **A build** (`HULLS`, `STAGES`): lay a keel (name her), then three stages — the keel, framing and planking, masts and rigging — each wanting its own materials (oak and pine planks, iron bars, pitch, copper sheet, rope, sailcloth), put in from your pack, the holm's store and your ships' holds at the jetty as the Workshop takes them (`BridgeWorkshop.have` / `take`), and a third of the hours (`hours`, an apprentice's: a cutter 8, a brigantine 20, a raider 22, a galleon 40). Work goes on only while the stage's materials are all in and someone's on her. **Shipwrights** (js/business.js `engage` at the place `'slipway'`: faces, grades, wages on the same payroll): apprentice ×1, journeyman ×1.5, master ×2.2 (not till you've launched a ship); two at once. Worked out on the clock a minute at a time (`advance`), so she grows while you're away. Finished, she waits for you to **launch** her (`BridgeFleet.launch`: lying at the holm, `built: true`, a yard gives a quarter of her price). Break her up and what went in goes back on the racks.
- **Her colours** (`PAINT`, `SAILS`, `FIGURES` → a ship's `look`: paint, sails, figurehead), chosen on the slip and for any ship of yours lying at the holm after (`BridgeFleet.setLook`), with her portrait in them as you choose (`lookView`'s `art`, `BridgeSloop.portrait`). Drawn wherever she is — at sea, lying at a pier, in the harbourmaster's book, and on other pilots' water (`sea` carries `lk`): her paint over her topsides from the waterline to the rail, planked, the deck left bare (js/shipkit.js `PAINT`, `painted`: worked out per heading like the bands, under any copper or roseite), her sails in their own cloth (sloop.js `SAILCLOTH`; crystal-weave still wins, and coloured cloth is set in her portrait to be seen), her figurehead at the stem under the bowsprit (`FIGS`, `figurehead`). `lookOf(b)` finds a boat's look (her own `look`, or her ship's by `ship` id). **Fit out**: parts your workshop made, fitted to ships lying at the holm for nothing (`BridgeWorkshop.takePart` + `BridgeFleet.mount`).
- Seen in the holm's ledger, the game menu's Ledger (*Needs you*: finished, waiting on materials or hands; *Your places*; shipwrights under *Staff & payroll* and on the office's staff board). Test: `world/shipyard.js`.

### The hall of finds (Lumar)
The empire plan's chunk 4 (`_claude_docs/spec-empire.md`; Beck: the monsters you beat and the relics you dig up yourself go in a trophy hall at your base — no hired warriors, the rarest finds always yours to go and get). `js/trophies.js` (BridgeTrophies; localStorage `bridge_trophies`).
