- **The building**: `hall` in the holm's `BUILD` (7,500, `needs: 'house'`), on the east lot past the warehouse (`LOTS['hall'] = (33, 10, 37, 13)` in `scenes/lumar_holm.py`, door at (35, 13)): stone and slate, tall windows, THE HALL OF FINDS lettered over the door (`k.variant('hall', 'built')`, built on its own dice so nothing else on the island moves). js/holm.js adds its interaction and lot if the world JSON lacks them, and its door goes in.
- **Inside** (`lumar-holm-hall`, `scenes/lumar_holm_hall.py`): a chevron-parquet hall under a stone wall — five oak boards along the back wall for the sea legends (the Nightglass Coelacanth's the biggest, in the middle), stone plinths with gilt plates down the floor (the Lantern Beyond on the tallest, under the chandelier), two glass-topped cases on velvet by the door. The render holds only the room and its empty furniture: js/trophies.js draws each find on its spot once you have it, from its own art (an item's `draw`, a legend's js/fishart.js canvas), depth-tested through the `'*'` items hook — glowing things (the lantern, the heartsong, a Gloamserpent's scale) breathe a light of their own, the cases' finds lie under a sheen of glass. Where they go is the world JSON's `hall.spots` (`[id, x, row, z, kind]`, kind `board` | `plinth` | `case`), so a new find needs a spot in the script and an entry in `T`, never a re-render of anything else.
- **What counts as found** (`check`, every 5 s): anything the Collection has noted (js/codex.js), any fish ever landed (the fishing journal — from a boat too), the Undercroft's relics (`bridge_dungeon.relics`), or the thing in your pack now; kept once found, so selling a scale doesn't empty its plinth. A new one goes in the journal's log (*For the hall*).
- **Every spot has a plate** (interactions of type `trophy`): a find's name, where it came from and when, with its picture; an empty one says where to look (`T[id].hint`). The finds now: the five sea legends; the Lantern Beyond, the Heartsong, the Bell Warden's staff, the Regatta Cup, a Gloamserpent's scale, the Deep Pearl, the Veilpiercer; the Drowned Bell, a Wight's Lantern, the Ferryman's Coin, the Barrow Crown, a Heron Coin, a Star Pearl.
- Test: `world/trophies.js` (the cabinet too).

### The Cabinet of Wonders (Lumar)
The empire plan's chunk 5 ("magic things are the prize": the Workshop's highest tier, made from rare things you found yourself). `js/wonders.js` (BridgeWonders; localStorage `bridge_wonders`), loaded before js/workshop.js, which merges its recipes in (kind `wonder`, group *Wonders*).
- **The wonders**, one of each, made at your Workshop on the clock like anything else (your bench or the smiths; a second one is refused, `reason: 'one'`), then straight into the Cabinet of Wonders in your hall of finds, where they work for good, wherever you are:
  - **The Storm Compass** (moonglass, sapphire crystals, a songstone, copper sheet; Tamsin Gale teaches it for two sapphire crystals for the lens): fog doesn't slow or shield your standing orders (js/orders.js), and at sea the mist lies thin round your own boat (js/sloop.js) — `fog()`.
  - **The Gilded Ledger** (three Heron coins, oak planks, copper sheet, a sapphire crystal; Ambrose Vane the registrar sells the plan): your businesses take a tenth more (js/business.js) — `takings()`.
  - **The Tidecaller's Pearl** (a star pearl and the Deep Pearl, moonglass, sailcloth; Old Pell sells the plan): fish bite a third sooner and the rare ones come up more (js/fishing.js) — `bite()`, `rare()`.
  - **The Ferryman's Bell** — the Drowned Bell, woken (with moonglass and a songstone): the Midnight Sea's monsters and the drowned come half as often (js/seafight.js) — `deep()`.
  - **The Barrow Crown, woken** (star iron, a songstone): everyone who works for you asks a tenth less in wages (js/business.js's payroll) — `wages()`.
  - **The Ferryman's Coin, woken** (moonglass, copper sheet): every yard mends your ships for a third less (js/fleet.js `repairCost`) — `repairs()`.
- **Plans**: the three people's are `workshop` topics in assets/people.json (`keeper`, `registrar`, `tern_pell`; js/workshop.js `SHOPS`); a relic's waking is known as soon as you've found the relic (`wakes`, js/trophies.js `has`).
- **The cabinet** is two glazed walnut cabinets in the hall's back corners, three shelves each (`scenes/lumar_holm_hall.py`, spots of kind `shelf`): js/trophies.js draws each wonder on its shelf once made (from its own pixel art, `wonder_<id>` items) behind a pane of glass, its plate says what it does and when it was made; an empty shelf names what goes there and whose plan it is. A woken relic stays in its case in the hall, its plate saying it's been woken.

### The Peddler, magic things and the curios
`js/magic.js` (BridgeMagic; localStorage `bridge_magic`). **Wendel Crane, Peddler of Oddments** (`npc-peddler`, a witch-hatted Blender NPC; people.json `peddler`, found by the label PEDDLER in any of his worlds) sets up somewhere different each day (`PLACES`, by the date: the Market Square, the District street, Verdance's farm road, Fellhaven, Sapphire's quay, Enigma's docking bay) — added to that world's interactions and actors as it loads. *What have you got?* opens his wares (`BridgeInteractions.choose`):
- **Moonlit Wand** (weapon): F casts a bolt of moonlight seven tiles the way you face, through the first creature it meets (30, via `BridgeItems.strike`), 2 energy a cast (most of it back after a breath — see *Energy and the second wind* under Fighting); bolts are drawn and moved in a `'*'` scene `over` hook.
- **Glimmer Lantern** (tool): held, hidden curios nearby glow; used, it says which way is warmer.
- **Heron Feather**, **Ring of Hoarfrost**, **Tide Charm** — charms that work while carried: walk 12% quicker (`BridgeGear.speed`), keep out the cold (`BridgeGear.has('cold')`), the sloop 12% faster.
- His clothes rail (`SHOPS.peddler`): the **Starweave Robe** (`mystic`, +40 energy) and the **Ranger's Cloak** (`ranger`, +15 health, +8% speed) — `characters.py build_mystic` / `build_ranger`, rendered walking, seated, skiing and climbing in all six colours.
- A **rumour** (40 coins): where one of the curios you haven't found lies.
**The curios** (`CURIOS`): twelve old things hidden by landmarks across the worlds (the drowned bell clapper by Midnight's graveyard, Edda's thimble by the windmill, the Wren's patch by the Kestrel, a rune stone on the Hoarspire…), each a piece of one tale — *the Lantern Beyond the Last Ridge*, a light past the Hoarspire that everyone half-remembers. A curio is a `curio` interaction that glints now and then (brightly to the lantern); picking it up adds its lore to the journal; all twelve pay 300 coins (`lantern_beyond_tale`) and tell the whole of it. The tale ends past the Hoarspire (see *The Last Ridge*).

### The Last Ridge (the expedition past the Hoarspire)
`js/expedition.js` (BridgeExpedition; localStorage `bridge_expedition`) — where the curios' tale ends. An old bolt on the summit's east rim (`THE LAST RIDGE`, added as the summit loads, two wands drawn there) lowers you down the far side onto **the Last Ridge** (`hoarfell-ridge`, `scenes/hoarfell_ridge.py`): one long ridge bent in a U over the cloud sea (a backdrop world — its outline is a smooth-joined union of rounded boxes with noise, traced with marching squares and lofted down in strata; the walking surface is quads inside the outline with the boundary vertices projected onto it, so nothing runs along the tile grid), `coldRate: 2` — the cold bites twice as fast.
- **The crevasse field** (the west arm): open crevasses are slots lofted down in blue ice into the dark (solid tiles); each crack runs on hidden under the snow past its open stretches (`expedition.crevasses`, a faint seam in the render if you look). Step on one and the snow gives way: a fall into the dark, −25 health, and you climb out by the last cairn you passed (`expedition.cairns`); the hole stays open (solid) till the next day's snow. Bamboo wands with orange rags (Ivo Venn's line, `expedition.wands`) cross each crack on its one snow bridge. Ysolde sells the **avalanche probe** (`probe`, 50; F: hollow or solid up to three paces ahead, and the crack round it shows from then on); the glimmer lantern held shows the near ones.
- **Whiteouts** blow over every few minutes: white past a few paces, blowing snow, only the nearest wands' flags showing, the cold half as hard again (`BridgeExpedition.whiteout()`, read by `js/worlds/hoarfell.js`).
- **The Ice Step**: a wavy band of seracs across the arm (`assets_isle.ridge` in ice strata), climbed in its own world (`hoarfell-icestep`, `scenes/hoarfell_icestep.py`, a close-up face like `hoarfell-face`). Climb routes can live in any world now: `ROUTES[id].world`, `foot` (where you stand to start) and `exit` (where topping out takes you); `away` keeps it off Fellhaven's route board till you've climbed it.
- **The high ridge**: a frozen tarn, the **bivouac hut** (rest by the stove — the weather blows over; the logbook: Ivo Venn, the Wren's crew, a pirate who followed a dragon, someone writing for Edda, a young Ysolde; sign it), **the old camp** (two summit crystals, coins and its last page), rime towers, the last cairn and its prayer flags.
- **The bowl** (the east arm), skied free from the carved stone at its head (`ski_here`): any world with a `ski` block can be free-skied now (`ski.name` / `ski.hello` for the HUD); the bowl's has wind-packed snow, powder at its edges, an ice patch, a wind lip to jump and rime towers to miss.
- **The ice door** at the bowl's foot, in a buttress of ice: twelve hollows up its pillars glow for the curios you've found (`expedition.door.hollows`, screen tiles); it opens to the rune stone from the Hoarspire's summit (the `summit_rune` curio) and stays open. Behind it **the Hollow** (`hoarfell-hollow`, `scenes/hoarfell_hollow.py`): blue ice in great blocks, a frozen fall glowing from inside, carvings of the tale, and on a dark plinth the Lantern Beyond (motes and glow drawn live). Light the tin lantern from it: the **Lantern Beyond** item (`lantern_beyond`: carried, the cold can't touch you; held, crevasses within five tiles and curios show; F lights up the snow round you), 500 coins, `lantern_beyond`. The journal has a *Last Ridge* section.

### Companions
`js/pet.js` (BridgePet; localStorage `bridge_pet`): a pup from Hollin's litter (once the farm is yours), a kitten from Gran Wilde, a hover-drone from Tinkers Tech (400 coins) — conversation topics with `pet: 'dog' | 'cat' | 'drone'`, named as you take them (a chooser `input`). One goes with you at a time through every world; the **whistle** (an item, given with the first) calls the next or sends them home. They follow your steps a pace and a half behind (a trail of your positions), sit when you stop, and E pets them (a `companion` interaction that moves with them). Once a day in the open air the dog digs something up (by world: crystals and edelweiss on Hoarfell, apples and honey on the farm, sea glass and nails on Lumar, and a few coins) and the cat brings you a fish; the drone's scanner pings every few seconds — gold, with a beep, when an unfound curio or a treasure dig is within seven tiles. Drawn in code on the 16-unit grid (`dogParts` / `catParts` / `droneParts`: walking, sitting, a wagging tail, the drone's rotors and lamp), mirrored for left, depth-tested through the `'*'` items hook; hidden while a mode (skiing, climbing, dancing) has the pilot.

### Checking the worlds
`python3 tools/bridge-art/audit_worlds.py` flood-fills every world from its spawn and every way in, and reports interactions nobody can reach, exits to missing worlds or onto solid tiles, NPC actors on walkable tiles (people you can walk through), and people / quest clues / businesses whose `{world, label}` isn't there (a talking walker's `talk` counts as being there; its route must not cross solid tiles). Run it after re-rendering a scene or moving things; anything added at load time by the JS goes in its `ADDED` list.

### Places within places
Worlds nest: a door, stair or ladder is an `enter_world` interaction with a `spawn`, and the room it leads to sends you back to the same spot. A `password_gate` with a `targetWorld` opens onto a world once you type its `password` (case doesn't matter) and stays open after (`bridge_gates`) — the District's alley gate opens on **the Backs** (`arcadia-alley`, `scenes/arcadia_alley.py` + `assets_backs.py`: the alley behind the shops, the skyline and rain over it) and NULL's shipping container there on **NULL's den** (`null-den`), where Vex runs the crew and the rig hands out **Splice** jobs (`js/splice.js`, interaction type `splice`: turn junctions to route a feed from the tap to the screen before the trace runs out; boards grow from 5×5 to 7×7 and pay coins, five paid jobs a day). An interior that isn't the Arcade can set `"interior": true` for a plain dark backdrop on the arcadia tileset. Ashcombe House's rooms open off its great hall (the drawing room west, the library east, the dining room through the double doors at the back, the master bedroom up the stairs) and the Starling's lobby off the holm (see *Legacy*).

**The Skywalk** (`arcadia-skywalk`, `scenes/arcadia_skywalk.py`) is where the street's SKYWALK LIFT goes: a glass bridge strung between two towers two hundred floors up, a backdrop world with the live skyline (`skyline` block) and rain all round. The street lift at its west end, the lift up to Skyport 7 at the east (Skyport's LIFT comes down here now); glass floor panels over the drop with the city's lights in them, a vending machine, benches facing the view (seats with the `skyline` view), a holo-ad, commuters crossing (`walkers.json`), and a viewing bay with a coin viewer. **ZOLTA.EXE** (`fortune`, js/district.js) tells a fortune for ten coins — true tips dressed as prophecy (`FORTUNES`: the Last Ridge, who bluffs at Fell Dice, floor twenty, the moon pool's pearl…); **B-SIDE** (`busker`), a robot on a crate with a pink keytar, plays you something for a five-coin tip, and the first tune of the day lifts your energy.

**Club Void** (`arcadia-club`, `scenes/arcadia_club.py`; `js/dance.js`, BridgeDance) is behind the street's CLUB VOID door: members only — sixty coins for the stamp (free in the Neon Runner suit), kept in `bridge_dance`. A black room under VOID in neon tubes: Kiri Vale on the decks on a riser between speaker stacks (`npc-dj`), Dez behind a lit wall of bottles (`npc-mixer`; the BAR sells Void Fizz, +30 energy), booths in purple velvet, a mirror ball, dancers (scene actors with `dance: true` — `js/scene.js` turns, steps and bounces them on the beat), and an LED dance floor of dark glass tiles (the world JSON's `club.floor`) that `dance.js` lights from the `arcadia` scene hooks in four patterns that change each bar. The music is the `club` theme (124 bpm, `drive`: the kick never drops out) and everything keeps its time by it — `BridgeAudio.clock()` gives the song's step, less the output latency (`BridgeDance.beat()`; without sound the module keeps the same tempo itself). **The dance** (`dance`, on the floor): Easy (on the beat), Club (eighths, a few jumps) or Void (sixteenths); arrows rise in four lanes in a screen-space panel (a 120 × 210 native canvas scaled ×2, tap pads on touch) to the receptors at the top on their notes; ←↓↑→ or WASD — perfect / great / good by timing (55 / 105 / 160 ms), a miss breaks the combo, the combo lifts the score up to double; the pilot turns to each arrow and the tile under them flashes. Charts come fresh each day from the date. A grade (S-D), the best per level, coins for a first clear (40 / 80 / 150) and once a day after. **The dance-off** (a keyboard row in the same menu): two people on one keyboard, WASD on the left panel against the arrows on the right, the same Club chart for both (`run.players`, each with its own notes, score, combo and judge); player two dances beside the pilot as a runner-suited partner (a temporary scene actor that turns and hops on their steps), and the floor goes to the higher score, with a rematch. No coins or bests from a dance-off.

The District's shops do things (`js/district.js`, BridgeDistrict, set up on their interactions as they load): Cyber Noodles' pot serves Spore-Broth Ramen (energy and health) and Neon Udon; the Chrome Clinic's exam chair patches you up to full for twenty coins and its cabinet sells med-kits and stim packs; Joro's junk pile can be rummaged for twenty coins (`RUMMAGE`, a weighted table: mostly boots and nails, sometimes coins, a crystal, a gold pocket watch Joro buys back at 120, a treasure map you don't have). Miyu buys the farm's eggs, milk and vegetables at a kitchen's price, better than Marlo's.

Enigma goes on below the relay deck: its south door leads down into **the ring** (`enigma-ring`, `scenes/enigma_ring.py` + `assets_ring.py`) — a corridor laid round an arc (deck plates, walls and windows are built along the curve; the walkable cells are the ones whose centres fall inside the annulus), windows onto the ringed giant (a world JSON `giant: [col, row, radius]` draws it in the station backdrop), pressure doors sealed at both ends, the crew rota. Off it, **the crew quarters** (`enigma-quarters`): the Wren crew's five berths, each with a keepsake on the shelf (the `keepsakes` quest, *Five Berths*), Okafor's stripped bunk, the mess table.

Sapphire's lighthouse opens from its door on the west hill (`LIGHTHOUSE`): **the keeper's room** (`lumar-sapphire-lighthouse`, `scenes/lumar_sapphire_lighthouse.py`) — a whitewashed octagon with an iron stair winding up the back wall (`assets_light.spiral_stair`), forty years of logbooks, this year's log open on the table, the stove, the oil — and up the stair **the lamp room** (`lumar-sapphire-lamproom`): the great lens turning round its blue lamp (`assets_light.fresnel_lens`, lights in group `pulse` and the scene's `flicker.mode: "pulse"`, so the room breathes with the beam), windows all round, the clockwork, the signal lamp aimed at Midnight, and the keeper, Tamsin Gale (`npc-keeper`, a persona sheet). Her quest is `keepers-log` (*Three Long, One Short*).

Arcadia's **Lucky 7** (`arcadia-lucky-7`, `scenes/arcadia_lucky_7.py`, `assets_cyber.pachinko_machine` / `prize_wall` / `maneki_neko`) is the pachinko parlour behind the bulb sign on the District street: a row of machines under the neon, an island of them back to back, the prize counter where Sev Akana (`npc-sev`, a persona sheet) cashes balls for coins, the lucky cat by the door. **Pachinko** (`js/pachinko.js`, interaction types `pachinko` with `machine`, and `pachinko_prizes`) is a real pin board in a native buffer: hold to fire, turn the handle (slider or ←/→) so balls ride the rail round the arch to the return rubber and drop beside the screen; the centre pocket pays 2 and spins the reels (four held), three alike (1 in 32) opens the attacker for a fever — guide rails swing over the funnels — ten balls at 8 (three 7s: two rounds at 10); tulips pay 2. Each of the six machines has its own seeded pins and art. Balls live in your box (`bridge_pachinko`): a free house tray of 30 a night, trays of 50 for 10 coins, the counter pays 5 balls to the coin up to 150 coins a night. Tuned in simulation (`BridgePachinko._sim`) to return about 85%.

### Getting around Arcadia: express lifts, air cabs, the Loop (js/transit.js)
Arcadia is a vertical city — "ritzier as you go up, poor as you go down" — in five levels, each with a tag on its signs:
**the Spires** (L480, above the smog, real stars), **the Mid-Levels** (L200: the Skywalk, Skyport 7, the Terraces),
**the District** (L0: Lantern Row, the neon street, the spaceport), **the Undercity** (B20) and **the Sump** (B60) — the
plan is `_claude_docs/spec-arcadia-above.md`. Beck: "the skytrain, express lifts, and stuff like that" (no funicular).
- **`assets/arcadia-lifts.json`** is the one file all of it reads (js/transit.js, js/map.js's cutaway, `atlas.py`,
  `audit_worlds.py`): `levels` (top first: `id`, `name`, `tag`, `floors` — the range the lift counter shows, below the
  street negative and shown `B20`), `places` (each main place's `level` and `x`, 0 west … 1 east, and `name`, for the Map;
  rooms inside them aren't listed), `shafts` (`{ id: { name, x, lobbies: [{ id, world, floor, spawn }] } }`: an express lift
  and its stops, `spawn` where you step out), `loop` (`{ name, stations: [{ id, name, world, spawn }] }`, in order round the
  ring) and `cabs` (`{ pads: [{ id, name, world, spawn, level }] }`). A stop whose world isn't in the game yet stays dark
  (js/transit.js asks the atlas), so another workstream's lobby can go in before its place does. Workstream C adds the
  Undercity's and the Sump's lobbies to the `core` shaft.
- **In a world** they're interactions: `express_lift { shaft, lobby }`, `air_cab { pad }`, `loop_station { station }`
  (registered by js/transit.js with `BridgeInteractions.register`; the old `enter_world` lifts on the street, the Skywalk
  and Skyport 7 are lifts now: shafts `skywalk` — the street and the Skywalk's west pod — and `skyport` — its east pod and
  Skyport 7). The audit checks every one names a stop in the file, that stop is in its own world and steps out onto open
  ground; the atlas makes every stop an exit to every other on its shaft, ring or rank (kinds `lift`, `loop`, `cab`).
- **A lift**: pick a floor (the stops top first, with their levels and floors; yours greyed), the doors close and you're
  in the car looking out through its glass (`#transit-ride`: a 320 × 180 canvas scaled by a whole number): the floor
  counter rolling over odometer-style, the city's own rendered layers (`BridgeSkyline.layers()`) sliding down past as you
  rise, the near towers' faces whipping by, the level's name lighting up as you pass into it (*Through the smog* between
  the Mid-Levels and the Spires — the car fills with pink fog — then stars, the smog sea below with tower tops through it
  and the white-and-gold spires standing out of it; below the street, the shaft's concrete and sodium lamps). Gold trim on
  the Spire Express. Space, Esc or a tap skips to the doors opening; then the world, under its own fade.
- **An air cab**: pick a pad and pay (12 coins a hop, 6 more a level, 10 more to or from the Spires: `fare`); the ride is
  the cab's side window — the layers streaming past, traffic lanes the other way, a tower close enough to see into, the
  driver's little route screen, a charm swinging.
- **The Loop** (the carriage, `arcadia-loop`, `scenes/arcadia_loop.py`: three bays of glass along her side with sliding
  doors between, cyan benches, grab poles, the route map over each door, ads, riders; 3 coins): board at a station (where to, or *ride round*), sit by the window
  and she runs station to station round the ring — 22 s a leg, 9 s at each stop with the doors open (`RUN`, `DWELL`) —
  the skyline streaming past at speed (the world's `skyline.motion: "loop"`: `BridgeSkyline.setMotion` scrolls and
  repeats the layers by how far she's gone), towers flashing by close, each station's platform sliding in and stopping
  outside the windows with its name board; a banner says the next stop and yours. The doors (`loop_doors`) step you off
  onto that station's platform while she's in; stay on and she carries on round. The route map (`loop_map`). Reload
  aboard (or arrive some other way) and she's waiting at the first station with the doors open. The carriage's world
  JSON has `loopcar` (`window`: the glass's screen rows, `seats`, `board`); round the glass js/transit.js fills the
  screen with her shell, and the skyline block's `rain: { none: true }` keeps it dry inside.
- **The Loop's train in the city** (the same moment for everyone, from the clock): a world whose skyline block has
  `loop: { row, from, to, scale }` (screen tiles) has her viaduct across its sky and sees her cross every 150 s, west then
  east (`PASS`, `CROSS`; a rumble, `train`) — the neon street; a station world's `loop: { track: [x0, x1, row, lift] }` (`lift`: screen tiles above the row the rails stand at — their height × 0.7071 at the scenes' 45°) sees
  her brake in, wait and pull out on its track every 90 s (`stationItems`, depth-tested at the track's row). Her sprite is
  `assets/scenes/loop-train` (`tools/bridge-art/loop_train.py`).
- **The Map** (js/map.js): Arcadia's card on *All places* opens with a side-on cutaway of the city (`cutaway`, a
  360 × 226 canvas at a whole number of device pixels): the starlit Spires over the smog, the Mid-Levels and the Loop's
  ring, the street in neon, the Undercity's sodium lamps, the Sump's water; every main place where it stands — named
  where you've been (a button: its map), "?" where a way you know leads; the lift shafts between their stops, the cab
  routes arcing between pads, the Loop's stations on its ring; the levels down the left (a "?" until you've been on one).
  The tree of every room is still under it. A lift, a cab rank or a station on a place's own map is a marker that says
  where it goes (`BridgeTransit.links`).
- Sounds: `ding`, `cab`, `doors`, `train` (js/audio.js). Test: `world/arcadia_transit.js` (the lifts up and across and
  back down, a ride's frames to the Spires and below the street and in a cab, the Loop boarded and ridden to a stop, the
  train over the street, the cutaway; `shots/transit_*.png`).

### The Neon Royale (the casino)
Beck: "instead of Runouts being like a game or a casino game, it's an actual interior space where you go… actual poker, actual blackjack… craps, roulette… make it an actual casino… really lean into the casino vibes." Then: big and ritzy (Monaco money under Arcadia's neon), two or three of each table, the old Runouts games in their own corner, a high-roller room you earn at poker, a bar and a restaurant. The Neon Royale is the oldest house in Arcadia, at the east end of the District street, owned by **the Duchess**. It is built in two halves: **the place** (these scenes, people, life and sound) and **Royale Link** — `js/casino.js` (`BridgeCasino`) and the games, which register the `casino` interaction type and run every table (see *Royale Link* below).
- **The contract.** Every playable spot is `{ "type": "casino", "game": <id>, "table": <unique id>, "label", "prompt": "PRESS E TO PLAY" }`, game one of `blackjack`, `roulette`, `craps`, `poker`, `slots`, `derby`, `crash`, `lobby` (a Royale Link terminal: your account, chips) or `runouts` (with `mode`, a Runouts mode id from `apps/runouts/src/games/runouts.js`; without one it opens the whole Runouts section). The Crown Room's tables add `"stakes": "high"`. The map shows them as games (`js/map.js` `CASINO`), and they get the gold ▶ badge (`js/world.js`).
- **The way in** (`arcadia-street`, re-rendered 60 wide): past Club Void, the Royale's frontage (`assets_casino.royale_facade`): a black-glass tower ribbed in gold rising in setbacks to a gold crown and a beacon, two searchlights sweeping the sky (the street's `royale.beams`, drawn in the `arcadia` behind hook), NEON in pink script and ROYALE in gold letters across a name board framed in chasing bulbs (they're in the street's `bulbs`), a neon crown, two tall screens playing the house's ads (`ads` kind `royale`, `js/skyline.js` — the skyline's tower screens show it now and then too), a marquee over a portico of gold columns and three revolving doors (THE NEON ROYALE at 48/50/51/53, 7 → `arcadia-casino` 21,26), a red carpet to the kerb between velvet ropes (`rope_line`), gold palms, a hover-limo waiting past a gap in the railing (THE LIMO), **Gideon** the doorman (54, 8) and the VALET booth at the end. The Royale is built on its own dice (`random.Random(1955)`), so the rest of the street renders exactly as before; every interaction, door, label, spawn and walker route at x ≤ 43 is unchanged, and the east SHIP exit moved to (59, 10).
- **The floor** (`arcadia-casino`, `scenes/arcadia_casino.py`, 44 × 29): oxblood carpet with a gold lattice, a hall six tiles high. Along the back wall: the **race & sports book** (a video wall over three betting windows: `derby` derby-1..3 at 2/5/8, 3), the arch to **the Card Room** (11–12, 3), the **cashier's cage** behind brass bars (Penny at 16, 3; `lobby` cage-1 / cage-2 at 14 / 18, 4), the **Crown Room** arch behind the velvet rope under the frieze (THE NEON ROYALE; Bruno at 23, 3; `royale_vip` at 21–22, 3), the **crash** screen and its rail (`crash` crash-1..3 at 26/28/30, 3), the arch to **Runouts** (32–33, 3) and **the stage** (Lady Velvet at the mic, 39, 4, lifted onto it; the pianist at the grand). The pit: **blackjack** bj-1..3 (15/22/28, 7; dealers behind at row 6, stools on row 8), **roulette** roulette-1/2 (14 / 28, 12; croupiers at the wheels) round the pit boss's podium (Lou, 22, 11), **craps** craps-1..3 (13/21/28, 18; a boxman sat behind each, a stickman in front). **Slots**: four banks of nine down the left (rows 7, 12, 17, 22) and two on the right (HIGH LIMIT, VOID STARS), eleven of them playable (`slots` slots-1..11 — their lights run hotter and they wink), the rest decor. On the right the lounge's tub chairs before the stage (seats), **the island bar** with its bottle tower (Ozzie behind it, talk at 37, 14; THE BAR vendor at 39, 14: Gold Rush, Royale Martini, Void Fizz) and the door to **Aurum** (42, 21–22). The marble foyer: **Royale Link** (`lobby` link-1..4 at 13–16, 22), the prize hover-coupe turning on its plinth, the **boutique** (Mimi at 28, 23; THE BOUTIQUE vendor at 30, 23: snow globes, gold chips, retired dice), the host's desk (**Rafe**, who shows you round), columns with lit globes, chandeliers, the crown mosaic.
- **The Card Room and Runouts** (`arcadia-casino-cards`, 34 × 15): through the floor's two west/east arches. Green carpet and low gold light: three oval **Texas hold'em** tables (poker-1..3 at 4/11, 6 and 8, 11; labelled TEXAS HOLD'EM, and the floor's arch THE CARD ROOM · TEXAS HOLD'EM, so it's findable — Beck missed it as plain POKER), a dealer at each, regulars in the chairs, the tournament board, a painting of robots at cards. Behind a glass-and-gold screen, **the Runouts lounge**: a cabinet per mode (its neon pictogram on the marquee, `assets_casino.RUNOUTS_ICONS`; its attract loop on the screen), eight under the big RUNOUTS sign (row 3) and eight on an island (row 7), each `runouts` with its `mode` (table `runouts-<mode>`), and **Kit**'s desk (`runouts` runouts-hall at 25, 11: the whole section; Kit at 27, 10).
- **Aurum** (`arcadia-casino-dining`, 20 × 13): plum and gold, a great window onto the live skyline (a HOLDOUT pane; the world's `casino.sky` rect, drawn by the `arcadia` behind hook), tables for two (seats in `assets/actions.json`; the window tables open the skyline view), **Chef Mori** at the open kitchen's pass (he buys starfruit, pumpkin, honey, strawberries, bluefin and sun carp dearly — a `sell` topic), **Laurent** at his podium with THE MENU (a vendor: Wagyu Royale, Gold-Leaf Sushi, Spore-Truffle Risotto, Souffle Royale, Champagne — `js/items.js`, they restore health and energy).
- **The Crown Room** (`arcadia-casino-vip`, 22 × 14): up the steps behind the rope. Bruno lets you up once your **poker winnings** reach the threshold — `BridgeCasino.unlocked()`, i.e. `pokerWon()` at `HIGH_ROLLER` (js/casino.js; no BridgeCasino, the rope stays shut; a `stakes: "high"` table opens at high limits for anyone, so the rope is what keeps them earned) — and otherwise says how far short you are (`royale_vip`, registered by js/worlds/casino.js; atlas EXTRA). Black marble, a window the width of the room onto the city, the Duchess's own **poker** table (poker-vip, her gold chair at its end), high-limit **blackjack** (bj-vip) and **roulette** (roulette-vip), all `"stakes": "high"`; the champagne tower, the crown on its plinth (the stake that won her the house), the bar cart, a velvet sofa.
- **Live parts** (`js/worlds/casino.js`, BridgeRoyale; `arcadia` scene hooks keyed off each world's `casino` block): the scene scripts collect positions in an `assets_casino.Live` record (screen tiles) — `wheels` (the pocket ring turning, the ball run round the track, slowing, dropping in and riding round, cleared for the next spin), `screens` (`race` — a horse race the camera tracks; `sports`; `odds` boards; `crash` and `crashbet` — one round for the whole house, `BridgeRoyale.crashAt(now)`: the rocket climbing on its curve, the multiplier in a 3 × 5 pixel font, the bang, the next round counting in; `link`; `board`; `runouts:<mode>` attract loops), `chases` (each slot's light pipes and topper bulbs), `bulbs` (the stage apron, the prize plinth), `glints` (chip stacks) and `dice` (tumbling down the craps tubs).
- **People** (people.json, gifts.js; sheets in `characters.py`): Gideon (`npc-doorman`), Rafe (`npc-host`), Penny (`npc-royale-cashier`), Lou Varga the pit boss (`npc-pitboss`: shades, an earpiece, a chrome arm; a persona sheet), Bruno (`npc-doorman`), Ozzie (`npc-royale-bartender`), Lady Velvet (`npc-singer`, red sequins), Mimi (`npc-boutique`), Kit (`npc-runouts-host`), Laurent (`npc-waiter`), Chef Mori (`npc-royale-chef`) and **the Duchess** (`npc-duchess`, seated, a stole and a circlet; a persona sheet — she won the house on four queens; Bix of the Wren left a marker on her table). Dealers (`npc-dealer`, `-b`, `-c`), croupiers, stickmen and boxmen, the pianist, gamblers sat at the tables and slots (`npc-gambler-a..d`) are scene actors; servers and a high roller walk (walkers.json). The gossip ties into the District: Halcyon wants the Royale's screens, NULL borrows them once a year, the Stranger plays one Runouts hand after close, Lady Velvet sang at Club Void when it was a supper club. ZOLTA has two Royale fortunes.
- **Sound**: the `casino` theme everywhere inside; the floor over `crowd` and `slots`, the Card Room over `murmur` and `chips`, Aurum and the Crown Room over `murmur`.
- **The kit** (`assets_casino.py`): floors (`carpet` with bare patches, `marble`, `medallion`, `runner`), walls (`deco_wall` — pilasters with neon flutes, smoked mirrors and gold sunbursts, a cornice of fans; `clear` stretches stay plain behind anything mounted — `side_wall`, `front_wall`, `arch`, `panorama` for the great windows), `column`, `chandelier`, `neon_crown`, `crown_3d`, the tables (`blackjack`, `roulette`, `craps`, `poker` with chairs on tile centres so people sit on them), `slot_machine` / `slot_bank`, `video_wall`, `bet_terminal`, `link_kiosk`, `cage`, `island_bar` / `bottle_tower`, `stage`, `grand_piano`, `mic_stand`, `cocktail_table`, `club_chair`, `runouts_cabinet`, `dining_table`, `kitchen_pass`, `champagne_tower`, `throne_chair`, `prize_coupe`, and for the street `royale_facade`, `revolving_door`, `rope_line`, `gold_palm`, `hover_limo`. Shapes are `prism` (an extruded outline), `rim` (a wall between two outlines: tubs, ring counters) and `rail` (a tube along a path: padded rails). Light: a warm area light over every table (`table_lamp_light`), the slot glow in the `pulse` light group (the scene's `flicker.mode` is `pulse`, so the slot floor breathes).

### The Spires (Arcadia)
The top of Arcadia (L480; the plan is `_claude_docs/spec-arcadia-above.md`, Workstream D): above the smog, the only place
in the city with real stars — white stone, gold and soft pastel neon, quiet. Beck: "really cool views and stuff as you get
up above the ground". `js/spires.js` (BridgeSpires) is what its places do; the kit is `tools/bridge-art/assets_spires.py`.
- **The crown plaza** (`arcadia-spires`, `scenes/arcadia_spires.py`, 96 × 46): a platform of white stone hung on the side of
  Halcyon's tower, a backdrop world in the skyline's **spires mode** (js/skyline.js `drawSpires`, the world JSON's
  `skyline.mode: 'spires'`: a clear sky of real stars, a galaxy band, a white moon and the odd shooting star; the other
  spires — the `peaks` layer, white stone and gold rendered by `tools/bridge-art/spires_backdrop.py` into its own
  `assets/backdrops/spires/` (its own cine.json, loaded by `drawSpires` alone; `BridgeSkyline.layers().peaks`) — standing out of a sea of smog that glows pink and cyan where the city shows through; the Mid-Levels'
  tallest towers, the `far` layer sunk to its crowns, poking up through it; under the rail the smog seen from above with the
  needles of towers piercing it). No rain falls up here (js/worlds/arcadia.js leaves it out in spires mode). West, **the sky
  gardens**: lawns with drawn grass, real trees (some in blossom) in stone rings, clipped hedges with flowers at their feet, a
  long reflecting pool with the stars in it, gravel walks, the orangery behind; **the tea salon** — Clémence's counter
  under a striped awning (a samovar, cakes under glass, tins of tea), tea tables under a pergola of wisteria (THE TEA SALON:
  jasmine pearls, petit fours, honey cake). The middle, **the crown plaza**: gold-strip paving round an inlaid star,
  Halcyon's crown fountain (a gilt crown spilling falls of water into a bowl), winged statues, lamps, benches, a flower
  cart; the foot of **Halcyon's tower** (HALCYON in gold, `L480 · THE SPIRES`): LUMEN's jewel-box windows, the **Spire
  Express** (`express_lift`, shaft `spire`, at 40, 9), the great doors (by appointment; Aurelio the concierge), the **Core
  Express** (shaft `core`, 54, 9), the Orrery's bronze door. East, **the Lyra** (the opera) up its steps behind a colonnade,
  **Aurelle** behind its round windows and velvet ropes, and **the sky docks** out over the drop: teak decking, the **air
  cab** pad (`air_cab` pad `spires`, 70, 34), Harlan Quay's dockmaster's kiosk, luggage trolleys, champagne crates, and the
  yacht *Lumière* lying alongside on her lift fields with her steward at the gangway. A white balustrade round it all, frosted
  globes on its piers, a telescope at the front (`spires` view: js/actions.js draws the skyline's spires mode in it; seats
  facing the edge open it too). People: Clémence (`clemence`), Old Ferris the gardener (`ferris`), Aurelio (`aurelio`),
  Harlan Quay (`harlan`); the crowd in its own wardrobe (`spires`: suits, gowns and pastels; `assets/crowds.json`).
- **Its rooms** (`scenes/_spires_room.py`: night light in two states, glass as HOLDOUT panes that js/spires.js fills with the
  Spires' night from the world JSON's `spires.windows`):
  - **LUMEN** (`arcadia-boutique`, `scenes/arcadia_boutique.py`): the designer implant atelier — arched niches each holding one
    piece in its own pastel light, glass cases on white plinths, a gold mirror, the fitting chair under a ring of light behind
    voile; Iris Kade (`iris_kade`, `npc-atelier`) and Nox (`nox`). THE COLLECTION is an `implants` interaction (`shop: 'lumen'`): js/implants.js's designer tier
    in LUMEN's own shop (`BridgeImplants.shop('lumen', …)`, js/spires.js) with two pieces of the house's own — Starlight
    Optics (eyes: criticals and energy) and Gilt Lace (arm: damage and a little plate).
  - **The Lyra** (`arcadia-opera`, `scenes/arcadia_opera.py`): the grand foyer — cream and red velvet, a gallery on fluted
    columns, the grand stair to THE BOXES (the `opera` view: the stage from a box, drawn by js/spires.js — the curtain, the
    soprano in her follow spot, the chorus, the pit, the house in the dark; an act every two and a half minutes by the clock,
    the interval with the house lights up), the doors to the stalls, the box office (Madame Fleur), the foyer bar, Tobias the
    usher, and Seraphine Lune, tonight's soprano.
  - **Aurelle** (`arcadia-champagne`, `scenes/arcadia_champagne.py`): the champagne bar and sky lounge — glass across the back
    onto the stars, a racetrack bar of white onyx with a tower of bottles and a pyramid of coupes (Coco; THE BAR: Starlight,
    champagne, sky oysters, petit fours), Bastien the sommelier, a white grand piano, banquettes, and the sky lounge's sofa and
    chairs turned to the glass round a fire table.
  - **The Orrery** (`arcadia-members`, `scenes/arcadia_members.py`): the private members' club — midnight silk over walnut, a
    great brass orrery of the seven worlds on its drum, bookcases with the ladder, the fire and the wing chairs, a card table,
    the bar cart, glass onto the stars, Mr Ashdown at the members' book, Lady Perpetua and Sir Augustin. Its door
    (`orrery_door`) lets members in; otherwise Mr Ashdown at the grille takes an introduction (the Penthouse or Ashcombe House
    is yours, or the Neon Royale's Crown Room is open to you) or 25,000 coins (`MEMBERSHIP`); kept in localStorage
    `bridge_spires`.
- **Happenings** (js/events.js): *The Lyra lets out* (the interval — Seraphine comes down the steps, the crowd round her) and
  *Fireworks over the sky docks* (shells bursting in pastel over the *Lumière*, the crowd at the rail).
- Music: the `spires` theme (a slow lydian harp, bells high up) over a high wind; the rooms `casino`'s lounge jazz or the
  spires theme over a murmur. Items: `jasmine_pearls`, `petit_fours`, `starlight`, `sky_oysters` (js/items.js). Test:
  `world/arcadia_spires.js` (the hub west to east, every room through its door and back, the box, the Orrery's door both
  ways, the lift and the cab, the happenings; `shots/spires_*.png`).

### The Rose Mine (mining)
Rose Cove's OLD MINE leads into **the hall** (`lumar-rose-mine`, `scenes/lumar_rose_mine.py`): the lift under its headframe, rails, a cart of roseite on a siding, the pickaxe rack (take an Old Pickaxe), the foreman's slate (your deepest floor), and Hob Dunmore (`npc-miner`), the last miner — he buys ore and stone and forges better picks (Iron: 5 iron ore + 150 coins; Star: 3 star iron + 400). Iron rock gives copper ore a third of the time, silver rock a quarter, for the workshop; the workshop's prospector's pick gets a piece more now and then, and finds the depth's best hidden in plain rock (the floors stay the same for everyone on them). **Floors** are three cave layouts (`lumar-rose-mine-a/b/c`, `scenes/lumar_rose_mine_floor.py <a|b|c>`, rendered without rocks) reused for all twenty floors. `js/mine.js` (BridgeMine) scatters rocks over a floor's open ground as it loads (seeded by floor and day; a rock is only placed if every open cell stays reachable), one of them over the ladder down. Rocks by depth: stone and iron at the top, roseite (to 8) and silver (from 4), sapphire (6-16), moonstone geodes (from 10), star iron (from 13), songstone (from 17); floor 20 holds **the great songstone** (its heart is the `singing-deep` quest's last clue). Swinging costs energy (`BridgeInventory.useEnergy`); a pick's power is its bite (old 1, iron 2, star 3). The lift stops at every fifth floor you've reached (`bridge_mine`). Rock sprites are a sheet rendered like a scene with no floor (`tools/bridge-art/mine_sprites.py` → `assets/scenes/mine-rocks`, KINDS order = `SPRITES` in mine.js), drawn depth-tested through the cave tileset's scene hooks (only in worlds with a `mine` block); the light takes on the depth band's colour (rose, sapphire, violet). Quest clue worlds may end in `*` to match any floor.

### The Mid-Levels: the Terraces (Arcadia)
Workstream D's Mid-Levels east (`_claude_docs/spec-arcadia-above.md`), L200: glass and cyan, two hundred floors over
the District. Beck: "each part definitely unique and alive… spacing where you can go side to side and see different
places, and up, down… really cool views". Kit: the cyber, District, penthouse, casino and estate kits; the toy finish,
Arcadia's night (`full` / `dim`).
- **The Terraces** (`arcadia-terraces`, `scenes/arcadia_terraces.py`, 88 × 42, a backdrop world with the skyline and
  rain): a deck of smoked glass the width of a block on the shoulder of **Cascade Tower**, whose foot rises behind it in a
  curtain wall of lit floors (CASCADE up its face, L200 in neon, two facade ads). West, **the skybridge** to Skyport 7
  (a glass tube, `SKYBRIDGE · SKYPORT 7` at (0, 19–20) → `arcadia-skypad` [27, 8]; you come in at [2, 19]) and, across
  the gap, the next tower's flats lit close enough to see into. Along the tower's foot: **Moonglow**'s door (15, 12)
  under a blue script and a canopy, **TSURU** noodles (vendor (21, 13)), the **Atrium**'s great glass front (three storeys
  of galleries glimpsed behind open glass; doors (31, 12)), the **express lobby** — the Spire Express (43, 11) and the
  Core Express (48, 11), brass doors under their indicators, Augustin the lift attendant — and **Glass & Steam** the café
  (vendor (54, 13)). East, the Loop's **TERRACES station** (`loop_station` (73, 9); platform rows 8–11): a platform under
  an open lattice canopy, roundels, a departure board, the track on its viaduct coming out of a portal in the tower's
  flank (x 56) and running into the next tower's (x 86) — the world JSON's `loop.track` (`[58, 86.4, 6.2, −0.28]`) is
  where js/transit.js stops the train. Down the deck: a promenade, glass floor panels with the city under them, four
  real trees in round steel planters (a sky garden), the Halo (a steel ring sculpture lit from inside), signposts to
  everywhere, a newsstand, a scooter dock, benches, lamps, bollards. Out over the drop, cantilevered terraces with glass
  balustrades (drawn as glass over the drop live, `terraces.glass`) and steel brackets under them: **HALO** (Chef Bastien's
  grill, tables under festoons, heaters; vendor (8, 29)), **LUMEN** (Hiro's izakaya: a counter of stools, low tables,
  lanterns on a line; vendor (31, 30)), **CIRRUS** (the rooftop cocktail bar: a round bar with Nadia inside it, a lounge
  round a fire table, loungers along the rail, festoons; vendor (59, 32)), and the **air cab pad** (`air_cab` (79, 31),
  Dove leaning on her cab). Below them more terraces step down the tower's face, lit and laid, out of reach.
- **Moonglow** (`arcadia-jazz`, `scenes/arcadia_jazz.py`, 22 × 14): the jazz bar — navy silk over walnut, a dark chevron
  floor, small round tables each with a candle in red glass, a low stage under a great round window where the moon
  hangs in the smog (a hole: js/worlds/terraces.js draws the skyline and the moon in it, `midlevels.sky` / `moon`), the
  long bar down the left wall with Sol before a wall of lit bottles, velvet booths down the right, the old players in
  gilt frames, the coat check. The **Odette Lark Quartet** on the stage (actors with `lift` 0.36): Odette at the mic, Ray at
  the piano, Mingo on the bass, Lou-Lou on the drums. THE BAR sells the Blue Note and the Moonglow Old Fashioned.
- **The Atrium** (`arcadia-atrium`, `scenes/arcadia_atrium.py`, 32 × 20): the tower's mall — the back wall rising gallery
  over gallery of lit shops behind glass rails out of sight, escalators climbing across the void, two glass lifts riding
  their shafts (drawn live, `atrium.lifts`) and people on the escalators (`atrium.escalators`), a fountain under a
  chandelier of light-rods, palms, benches, a florist's cart and a coffee kiosk; on the ground floor NOVA (stims and
  med-kits), PETAL & WIRE (posies), PAGES, a shop coming soon (the Spires' chrome boutique coming down), and **Iris** the
  concierge at her desk, who knows the way to everywhere (the levels, the Loop, the lifts, the cabs).
- **People** (`characters.py`, portraits, people.json, gifts.js): Nadia (`npc-mixologist`), Chef Bastien (`npc-grill-chef`),
  Hiro (`npc-izakaya`), Dove (`npc-cabbie`), Augustin (`npc-liftman`), Odette (`npc-jazz-singer`), Ray (`npc-jazz-pianist`),
  Mingo (`npc-jazz-bassist`), Lou-Lou (`npc-jazz-drummer`), Sol (`npc-jazz-bartender`), Iris (`npc-concierge`). Items
  (js/items.js): cloud espresso, glass cake, Halo skewers, Lumen yakitori, warm sake, the Cirrus Sour, the Skyline Spritz,
  the Blue Note, the Moonglow Old Fashioned.
- **Life**: the `midlevels` crowd wardrobe (js/crowd.js: office money out for the evening — suits and smart coats in
  glass, ice and slate; its own lines and wants in crowds.json) on the Terraces, in the Atrium and seated at Moonglow;
  seats in actions.json at Halo's tables, Lumen's counter, Cirrus's bar, Moonglow's tables, bar and booths; the chase and
  NULL's hijack come to the Terraces' sky too; two happenings (js/events.js): **a proposal at Halo** (the deck goes quiet,
  he asks, she says yes, the deck cheers and petals fall; congratulate them for a flute of champagne) and **a jam at
  Moonglow** (a guest sits in; listen for energy and a note in the journal). Music (js/audio.js): `midlevels` on the
  decks and in the Atrium (the street's synthwave lifted, airier, bells over a soft pulse), `jazz` at Moonglow (a slow
  minor ballad, brushes and a walking upright — the Royale's `casino` is brighter and quicker).
- **Getting there** (`assets/arcadia-lifts.json`): the Spire Express and the Core Express lobbies `terraces`, the Loop's
  `terraces` station, the `terraces` cab pad. Test: `world/arcadia_terraces.js`.

### Treasure maps
`js/treasure.js` (BridgeTreasure): a map is an item (`tmap_<id>`); using it unfolds the sketch — the spot cut from its island's own scene render (14 × 10 tiles round it), inked onto parchment (luminance in five sepia tones, dark edges, the sea left as bare paper) with a red X and a line in the finder's hand. The map never names the island; you know it by the landmarks. Holding the map, an **X MARKS THE SPOT** dig appears at the place (`decorate`); digging (6 energy) brings up a chest: coins, goods, and on some maps the next map in a chain (the old light → the Heron's strongbox → the Crimson watch; the drowned → the abbey). Ilsa Venn the cartographer sells three (a topic with `treasure: 'shop'`); others turn up under rocks in the Rose Mine from floor 4. `MAPS` holds each spot (world, tile, reward); dug spots are kept in `bridge_treasure`.

### The District grown out: Lantern Row and the spaceport (Arcadia)
Workstream D of `_claude_docs/spec-denser-worlds.md` (the plan: `_claude_docs/spec-arcadia-above.md`): the District
(tag L0 on the signs) runs on past both ends of the neon street: Lantern Row west, the spaceport east. Each comes
back to the street by `THE NEON STREET` (Lantern Row's at (63, 11) to the street's [1, 10], the spaceport's at (0, 11) to
[58, 10]); the street's own two edges (0, 10) and (59, 10) lead to them, arriving at [62, 11] and [1, 11]. The ground is
the rebuilt street's own (`assets_arcadia_rich.py`: `paving`, `kerb`, `bollard`, `tactile`, `hazard_stripes`,
`floodlight`, `windsock`, and wet after the rain, `k.wet`), so the seams with the street match: Lantern Row's setts are
`paving` slabs of warm stone in a running bond, the street's own paving where its east end meets it; the spaceport's
concourse starts in the forecourt's big dark slabs and runs on in the street's paving. Kits: `assets_oldquarter.py` (the old quarter: `shophouse` — a timber
ground floor `lattice` / `open` / `shutter`, a tiled pent over it, a framed or slatted upper floor, a **front gable**
`gable_roof` toward the lane, because a roof whose eave faces the camera hides the whole facade at 45° — `tile_roof` for
the eave-on kind where nothing's behind it, `pent`, `noren`, `lattice`, `stone_lantern`, `torii`, `shimenawa`,
`goldfish_stall`, `market_stall`, `awning` — stripes that sag into folds between the ribs, rolled at the back, a
scalloped valance along the front — `sake_barrels`, `potted_plant`, `cat`, `bicycle`, and the Loop's `viaduct` and
`station_block`, shared with the spaceport) and `assets_spaceport.py` (`apron` slabs and stains, `bay_marks`, `hauler`
and `container_ship` freighters on their legs — plated flanks and backs (`plates_side`, `plates_top`), `hatch`es, grime
and rust run down them (`streaks`), soot round the engines (`scorch`), their names painted on the flank and across the
back, cargo spilled off the ramp (`spill`) — `floodlight` masts with a beacon, `beacon`, `landing_lights` round each
berth, the air cab and its `cab_pad`). **The viaduct** is the thing it is: a deck poured in spans between deep edge beams
with stiffeners and a drip line, a strip of cyan light under the front beam pooling down onto the roofs, ballast,
sleepers and two rails, a low front parapet so the train shows over it, catenary masts, cantilevers, wires and droppers,
and piers of twin columns with a hammerhead and cross-bracing standing behind the houses (`gaps` leaves a station's own
deck to `station_block`). It runs at z 5, low over the roofs, and each place frames above you (`cameraLead` −4), so the
Loop's train is seen pulling into the station from the lane: the station's platform is out in front of the track — its
yellow line and tactile strip, benches, lamps, the name board and a light canopy over the platform only — and its
`loop.track` z is where the train sprite's rails land on ours (0.3625 + 0.7071 × the rails' z: the sprite is drawn by
screen rows, the scene by world height).
- **Lantern Row** (`arcadia-oldquarter`, `scenes/arcadia_oldquarter.py`, 64 × 20, a backdrop world under the skyline):
  the District before the towers — wet stone setts under strings of paper lanterns, two-storey shophouses under curved
  tile roofs: Granny Ume's tea house open to the lane (a raised tatami floor, low tables, the kettle steaming; TEA HOUSE
  sells green tea, matcha, mochi), Tokuda the lantern maker's (LANTERNS: a paper lantern, an omamori), Hoshi Ramen's
  counter on the lane with stools (seats; shoyu ramen), a shuttered old house with a bicycle and a cat, the Loop's
  **Lantern Row station** under its viaduct (`loop_station` `lantern` at (33, 8), the DEPARTURES board), Mama Keiko's
  Akachochin (red lanterns, sake casks, crates; yakitori, warm sake), the bathhouse and its chimney, the kampo herbalist
  (a kampo tonic), a tall old house of potted bamboo; across the lane the stalls — the **goldfish scoop**
  (`goldfish`, js/oldquarter.js: 5 coins a paper net, now and then a goldfish in a bag, a gift), greens, dried goods,
  the charm stall, a yakitori cart, fruit — a red parasol over the tea house's bench, a little Jizo between two stone
  lanterns; along the railing a promenade of benches over the drop (seats with the skyline view) and a coin viewer. The
  Loop's viaduct strides over the roofs the whole width (`loop.track` [29.2, 37.2, 6.2, 4.02]: js/transit.js stops the
  train between the station's portals). People: `ume`, `lanternmaker`, `hoshi`, `keiko` (sheets `npc-*`, portraits,
  gifts.js). Crowd: the `lantern` wardrobe (older folk, indigo and rust, paper umbrellas). Music: the `lantern` theme
  (a slow plucked line over a warm pad) over rain and murmur.
- **The roof shrine** (`arcadia-oldquarter-shrine`, `scenes/arcadia_oldquarter_shrine.py`, 22 × 13): up the stone stair at
  the lane's west end (`SHRINE STEPS` (8, 8) → [19, 6]; `STAIRS DOWN` (20, 5) → [8, 9]) — its own world because a walkable
  terrace above the lane would hide whoever stood on it (characters are depth-tested as standing at z = 0), and the
  quarter's render shows it up on the roof all the same. Raked white gravel and stepping stones, the little hall under
  its tile roof with the bell rope, the stone fox guardians, a vermilion torii, stone lanterns, a bent pine (needles,
  `k.foliage`), the ema rack of wishes, the omikuji cord; round it the quarter's tile roofs just below, the Loop's viaduct
  passing at eye level, a tower's lit foot. The **offering box** (`omikuji`, js/oldquarter.js: 5 coins, the bell, a
  fortune — great blessing (Kanna gives you an omamori), blessing, small blessing, or a curse you tie to the cord).
  Kanna keeps it (`kanna`, `npc-kanna`).
- **The spaceport** (`arcadia-spaceport`, `scenes/arcadia_spaceport.py`, 72 × 30): a concourse along the buildings —
  customs with its scanner arch (Inspector Halloran, `halloran`), **the Last Burn** (the spacers' bar out of an old hull,
  portholes, a neon rocket; Brannigan, `brannigan`; reactor stout, spacer's stew; drum tables and crates to sit on),
  **the Core Express** — the deep lift's tower, as wide as a street, rising out of sight, passenger doors and a striped
  freight door (`express_lift` shaft `core` lobby `spaceport` at (25, 8), spawn [25, 10]; Workstream C adds the
  Undercity and Sump stops to `core` in `assets/arcadia-lifts.json`), Halcyon Freight and its departures board, the Loop's
  **Spaceport station** (`loop_station` `spaceport` at (41, 8), spawn [41, 10]; `loop.track` [37.2, 45.2, 6.2, 4.02]), the
  Spacer's Rest (capsule bunks), the engine shop — then the crew line and the apron: **the Old Mercy** (a battered bulk
  hauler on her legs, her ramp down and crates spilling off it; Captain Desh, `desh`, by it), **the Halcyon IX** (a
  corporate container carrier under a gantry crane), **the Slow Hazard** (small, rusty, destination ???), and **the air
  cab rank** (`air_cab` pad `spaceport` at (57, 17), spawn [57, 18]), floodlight masts, fuel lines, crates and drums.
  Crowd: the `spacers` wardrobe. Music: the `arcadia` theme over rain, city and engine.
- **Happenings** (js/events.js): **sky lanterns** let go from the roof shrine (`skylanterns`, Lantern Row: they rise and
  drift up among the towers; let one go yourself for a little energy) and **a freighter lifting off** past the railing
  (`liftoff`, the spaceport: engines lighting below the drop, then she climbs up through the view on three plumes of
  thrust, the crowd at the railing watching). The sky chase and NULL's hijack reach both places (`arcadia-sky`).
- Test: `world/arcadia_district.js` (the ways back to the street, each place walked and shot, the people, the goldfish
  and the fortunes, the transit stops, both happenings, a phone).

### The farm on Verdance (farming)
Verdance is the fifth system on the nav chart (`garden` in `js/orbit.js`: a green planet quilted with fields, lakes and farm lights, two small moons) and the Bridge's one daylight world: `scenes/verdance.py` + `tools/bridge-art/assets_farm.py` (split-rail fences, the barn, hay, scarecrow, pond, the field's bare earth, lane, skeps, mailbox, windsock, wildflowers, apple trees, the seed stall, log pile) and `assets_town` houses, lit by one sun (`Kit.sun`). The farmstead (farmhouse, barn, tool shed, windmill, the landing pad), the field, the well and scarecrow, the pond and jetty (fishing: perch and the odd sun carp), the orchard and the skeps (forage: apples, honey), and down the lane the village corner: **Hollin Ashby**'s seed stall (`npc-hollin`, seeds) and **Marlo Fenn**'s cart (`npc-marlo`, buys all of it), the notice board, Hollin's cottage.
- `js/farm.js` (BridgeFarm) works the field — the world JSON's `farm.plot` (tiles, inclusive; the last earth row under the fence is a headland, left out). Face a tile and press E: one `farm_plot` interaction sits on the tile you face and says what E will do there (Till / Plant / Water / Harvest / Look), rebuilt whenever that changes. The tool shed (`farm_tools`) gives the hoe, the watering can and eight turnip seeds; the well (`farm_well`) or the pond fills the can (20 waterings; a watering wets the tile you face and one either side). Tilling costs 2 energy. The hoe, the can and seeds also work from the hotbar (F) on the tile you face.
- **Growth is real time while the ground is wet**: a watering keeps a tile wet for 20 minutes; crops accrue growth only over wet time (`advance`, worked out lazily from timestamps, so it carries on while you're away). Turnip 3 min, carrot 5, strawberry 8 (then a crop every 4), sunflower 8 (gives seed back), pumpkin 20, starfruit 40; flax (for the workshop's rope and sailcloth: seed wherever carrot seed's sold) two watered days. Five stages: seeded, sprout, young, grown, ripe — drawn in code from 16-unit ASCII art (`ART` in farm.js) and sorted with the characters; tilled earth (dry and wet) is drawn in the `ground` hook, under everyone. Ripe crops sparkle; starfruit glows. Harvest bounces the produce out as loot (`BridgeLoot`). Kept in localStorage `bridge_farm`.
- **Making it yours** (`js/homestead.js`, BridgeHomestead; localStorage `bridge_homestead`): you start alone on Edda's kitchen garden — the first five columns of the field, marked off with twine and stakes (`canWork`); the rest of the field says EDDA'S FIELD. Her note on the FARMHOUSE door sells the deed (500 coins); then the whole field is yours, and the farmhouse — go in and furnish it like any home (H); the ledger on the kitchen table (`farm_ledger`) has the takings, *Rest by the stove* and *Name the farm* (the name heads the journal and greets you on arrival). The **shipping bin** by the mailbox (drawn in code, its red flag up while something's waiting) takes anything Marlo buys at his cart's prices; **Marlo's round** comes every twenty minutes, wherever you are, and pays (a toast). The **seed box** in the BARN holds seed for Odile. The NOTICE BOARD is the **order board**: three orders a day (seeded by the date) from people across the worlds — Tove's pumpkin pie, Chef Miyu's broth, the tavern's stew, Joro's "client" — at 1.4–3× Marlo's price, and the person thinks the better of you (`BridgeTalk.nudge`). **Hands** for hire by the day (`npc-tam`, `npc-odile`, people.json, a `hand` topic): Tam keeps every planted row wet (`keptWet`), Odile brings in what's ripe into the bin and replants it from the seed box (`BridgeFarm.pickFor`; anything ripe over a minute is hers even while you're away). Hired, they work the field in front of you (walking to tiles with work on them, depth-tested); otherwise they wait by the notice board. **Sprinklers** (Hollin's stall, 150) keep the ground one tile round them wet for good; E on one lifts it.
- **Animals** (`js/animals.js`, BridgeAnimals; localStorage `bridge_animals`): once the farm's yours, the barn sells hens (150, up to six) and cows (700, up to two), each with a name. They wander the meadow south of the field (`PEN`), pecking and grazing — drawn in code on the 16-unit grid (`henParts` / `cowParts`: stand, step, peck or graze; brown or white hens, Holstein cows), depth-tested through a scratch canvas and `drawOccluded`, each with a `farm_animal` interaction that moves with it. Once a day each has an egg or milk for you (an egg or a milk drop bobs over it); E takes it and pets it — a pet a day is a heart, up to five; four hearts and hens lay large eggs now and then, five and the odd golden egg. Odile, hired, brings whatever's waiting into the bin. Marlo buys eggs, milk and golden eggs; orders on the board now want eggs and milk too.
- **The hands work whether you're there or not**: js/farm.js `work()` runs every half minute wherever you are, and when the game opens it works through the time you were away in ten-minute steps (`worked` in the save, up to a fortnight): Odile brings each crop in as it ripened and replants from the seed box, Tam keeps rows wet and stakes up storm-flattened crops (`BridgeHomestead.activeAt(hand, moment)`). Odile gathers each day's eggs and milk for the days she was on (js/animals.js). Test: `world/hands.js`.
- **Rain** on Verdance waters the field (js/homestead.js asks `BridgeWeather.rainDay()`): it is one of the weathers below now.
- **Seasons** (`js/seasons.js`, BridgeSeasons): Verdance turns through spring, summer, autumn and winter, a week of real time each (Monday to Sunday by the date; the week of 12 Oct 2026 is autumn). The farm's render is regraded per season as it loads — `BridgeScene.setGrade(id, fn, key)` reworks a scene's composited pixels once (like decor layers, never per frame): autumn turns the meadow gold with rust drifts, winter lays snow over it, spring freshens it, summer is the render as built; the trees, hedges and bushes are js/foliage.js's (see *The trees through the year*), and this (`meadow`) leaves them be. Petals, falling leaves or snow drift over it (the verdance `over` hook); in winter the bees and butterflies stay in, the birdsong goes, and a wet day is a snowy one (`js/weather.js`). Each season favours three crops (30% quicker, `FAVOURS`), winter slows everything to three-quarters (`growth`, read by `js/farm.js advance`); a toast says so as you land, once a season, and the journal's farm section shows the day.
- **Gifts** (`js/gifts.js`, BridgeGifts): *Give a gift* in any conversation (while you carry something giftable) opens a picker of your pack; each person has tastes (`TASTES`: loves, likes, can't stand, and a line for what they love), a gift moves their feeling (+2 / +1 / +0.5 / −1, one that counts a day) and is remembered in their bond for the AI conversations. Once you've given something, the picker shows how they took it.
- The verdance tileset's `over` hook is the life of the place: chimney smoke (`scene.smoke`), cloud shadows drifting over the farm, butterflies, bees at the skeps, glints on the pond. Music: the `verdance` theme (a skipping folk tune), beds `breeze` and `birds`; farm sounds `till`, `water`, `sow`, `harvest`.

### Hoarfell: Fellhaven, climbing, skiing, the cold
The sixth system on the nav chart (`ice` in `js/orbit.js`: snowfields, dark ranges, frozen seas). Two scenes, both the `hoarfell` tileset: **Fellhaven** (`scenes/hoarfell.py`, `tools/bridge-art/assets_snow.py`: snowfields, trodden paths, snowy firs, log chalets with icicles, the chairlift station, pylons and cable, the frozen pond and its ice-fishing hole, the snowman, sleds, ski racks, the cocoa hut, signposts, woodpiles, the route feet, the icefall, a summit flag) — the village street under a two-tier massif (`assets_isle.ridge`, `patchy=False` for a clean snow crown): Ysolde's guide hut and route board, the Snowcap Lodge (rest), the ski hire (a door now — see below), the lift with Ansgar, Tove's cocoa hut, the pond, two chalets, the pad; and **the summit of the Hoarspire** (`scenes/hoarfell_summit.py`, a `backdrop` world — one traced snow surface, its rim lofted down in strata over a sea of cloud): the lift's top station, the ski start gate, the summit register, the observatory (telescope view), prayer flags, a bench facing the range (a `peaks` view) and the three routes' anchors (abseil back down).
- **Day and night on the mountain**: Fellhaven and the sport worlds (the summit, the pistes, the Park, the face, the Last Ridge, the Ice Step) render with `cycle='day'` — the alpenglow they were built in is their day, the kit adds a deeper dusk and a moonlit night — and the sky js/worlds/hoarfell.js draws behind them (`sky`, `PAL`) blends the same three by the scene's own weights (`BridgeScene.sky`): the alpenglow, a deeper dusk, navy with the stars out and the moon in the east. A scene with one state keeps the alpenglow sky.
- **The cold** (`js/worlds/hoarfell.js`): a world with `"cold": true` takes 2 energy every twelve seconds (then health) unless you wear the Thermal Suit (`BridgeGear.has('cold')`) or have drunk hot cocoa in the last five minutes (`BridgeHoarfell.warm`); frost creeps in at the screen's edges and your breath fogs. The same hooks draw the snow falling, chimney smoke, the chairlift's chairs going round (`scene.heights.lines`, the cable runs in screen tiles), and the summit's sky, far range and cloud sea (`registerBackground('hoarfell')`).
- **Skiing and climbing happen in the world, at its own scale** — no panels. A *mode* (`BridgeWorld.setMode({update, focus, solo, end})`) takes over from the grid steps: it moves the pilot itself each frame, says where the camera looks (`focus`), keeps interactions quiet (`solo`), and is ended by any world change. It *poses* the pilot (`BridgeCharacter.setPose({kind, x, y, row, col, flip, lift})`: the floor point under them in tile units, a frame of a sport sheet, mirrored or not, lifted off the ground in native px — the shadow and the depth test stay at the foot row, so a lifted pose is an upright billboard at that row, as high as it's drawn). Sport sheets come from `characters.py pilot-ski[-<style>]` (40 × 56 frames, feet at (20, 45): rows down the fall line / across / 45° / tricks; columns glide 0-3, tuck 4-5, carve 6-7, snowplough 8, air 9, poling 10-11; the tricks row is a spin 0-7 and a fall 8-11) and `pilot-climb[-<style>]` (32 × 64, feet at (16, 61), back to you: hang 0-3, reach left/right 4-5, pull up 6, mantle 7, stand 8, falling 9, chalk 10, shake out 11). The sheets face right; left is the mirror (`BridgeCharacter.sportImage(key, flip)`).
- **The pistes** (`hoarfell-piste`, `scenes/hoarfell_piste.py`, 46 × 104): three runs from start huts along the crest — the Couloir (black, a gully between rock walls: moguls, ice, a big kicker), Pinewood (blue, through the forest, a jump in a glade), the Nursery Slope (green, broad, down the east side by the chairlift) — to one finish arch, the timing hut, the lift's bottom station and the path down to Fellhaven. The ground is flat in the render; the slope is the game's. The script writes what the game needs into the world JSON's `ski` block: per run its start, gates (`[x, row, width]`) and line; `jumps` (`[x, lip row, width, length, height]`); `grade` and `zone` (a character a tile: the pull down the fall line 0-9; groomed `.`, powder `~`, moguls `m`, ice `i`); `obstacles` (`[x, row, radius, kind]` — trees, rocks, lift towers: skiing collides with these circles, not their tiles, and with every other solid tile as a wall).
- **Skiing** (`js/ski.js`): steer any angle from straight down to right across (←/→), tuck (↓), snowplough (↑), turn across the hill to stop; speed from the grade under you, drag, friction; powder bogs you down, moguls shake you, ice won't turn. Space/E poles you on when slow; off a kicker you fly (a ramp height, a launch from its slope) and Space spins you — land a whole turn or go down. Trees, rocks and walls put you in the snow; you get up beside them, toward the run. The camera runs ahead of you down the hill. Tracks in the snow (`ground` hook), spray (`over`). **Races**: from a start hut, the lift (Fellhaven, the summit, the finish) or the summit's start gate — 3-2-1, every gate (a miss +3 s, a landed spin −1 s), the clock stops at the arch, you coast to a stop and get the result. Your best run is saved as a ghost and skis beside you, translucent, next time. **Liv** (`npc-liv`, at the finish) races you: her line is the same physics steered gate to gate by a careful hand (`rivalLine`, paced per run), drawn with the red coat sheet; beat her once on a run and she pays. **Free skiing**: anywhere on the mountain, no clock; stop and press E to take your skis off. Runs: the Nursery Slope, Pinewood, the Couloir (once you've finished Pinewood). Saved in `bridge_ski` (per run: runs, best, the ghost; Liv's beaten runs); a finish pays (first, then once a day).
- **The Park** (`hoarfell-park`, `scenes/hoarfell_park.py`, through THE PARK at the pistes' finish — `BridgeSki.decorate`): a slope cut through the forest under a THE PARK banner — a drop-in deck, a line of three kickers (small, medium, large) down the left, a line to slide down the right (a flat box, a straight rail, a down rail, a kinked rail), the Big One at the bottom, the finish, the judges' tower, the scoreboard, a rope tow back up (`park_start`, `park_tow`, `park_board`). The `ski` block adds `rails` (`[x0, row0, x1, row1, h0, h1, kind]`, a kink is two pieces end to end) and `park` (drop-in, finish). `BridgeSki.park()` is a scored run: in the air Space spins and holding ↓ grabs (mute, safety, Japan air by how long), a long hang is big air; ride onto a rail's foot or drop onto it and you slide it — the rail tips you one way and the other (a balance bar over your head), ←/→ lean back against it, Space pops off; tricks landed within 2.6 s of each other chain into a combo (x2 … x5), banked when the chain runs out or at the finish; a fall loses the chain. The judges pay the first run and once a day, keep your best (`bridge_ski.park`), and **Juno Pike** (`npc-juno`, people.json `juno`, who built the place) holds 7500 to beat (200 coins, `beat_juno`). The park plays the arcade theme over the snow wind.
- **The face** (`hoarfell-face`, `scenes/hoarfell_face.py`): one great wall of rock facing you — strata, snow on its shelves, ledges with bolts, a corniced crest against the evening (a backdrop world) — with three lines up it: the Staircase (jugs, a yellow fixed line, ledges close together), the Icefall (a frozen waterfall: ice holds need an axe) and the Hoarspire (crimps and slopers, rust-coloured loose rock, few ledges, wind). Every hold is built where the world JSON's `climb` block says (`holds: [x, z, kind, screen row]`, `ledges: [x0, x1, z, screen row]`, `face`, `top`, `topRow`, the routes' feet), and it's one face: traverse between lines if you like. The routes' feet in Fellhaven bring you here.
- **Climbing** (`js/climb.js`): you hang from the holds (hands on the hold, the body below; on a ledge you stand), ←/→ choose among the ringed holds in reach, ↑/E reach, ↓ climb down, C chalk (+25), Esc lowers you off. Stamina: each reach costs more the longer it is and on crimps and ice; hanging drains it (faster in the cold, faster still in the Hoarspire's gusts unless you're on a jug — snow streaks warn you first); ledges are rests and bolts. A fall costs health and drops you to the last ledge with a climbing rope (or on the Staircase's fixed line), else to the foot. Loose holds crumble a second after you take them; slopers spit you off when you're tired; hoar crystals in the rock are loot. The top pays (first ascent, then once a day), keeps your best time (`bridge_climb`), and walks you out onto the summit. Ysolde sells chalk, rope and the axe; the route board lists your ascents. **Races**: Ysolde's *Race me up* topic races you up the Staircase, the Icefall or the Hoarspire — her line is a careful hand's (`rivalLine`: the highest good hold in reach each time, never loose rock, a breather on each ledge, paced per route), drawn beside you on the same holds in a red coat (`climb-coat-red`) after a 3-2-1; the HUD says who's ahead; beat her once on a route and she pays (80 / 150 / 300, `beat_ysolde_<route>`).
- **Brask's Ski Hire** (`hoarfell-skihire`, `scenes/hoarfell_skihire.py`, through the SKI HIRE door; log rooms share `scenes/_snow_room.py`): Inge Brask's shop (`npc-inge`) — skis tips-up along the wall, the trail map, a window on the range, a wall of boots behind the COUNTER (skis, goggles, cocoa), the goggle case and the till, the WAX BENCH (`ski_wax`, js/worlds/hoarfell.js: ten coins for a hot coat — `BridgeSki.wax(20)`, and for twenty minutes `js/ski.js` runs your skis with less drag and friction), the OUTERWEAR rail (Alpine Outfitters, the Thermal Suit) and a tall MIRROR (the wardrobe), a barrel of poles, the STOVE (warms you). Fellhaven's shops hang painted signs (`assets_snow.hanging_sign`: an ice axe and rope for the guides, a bed for the lodge, crossed skis for the hire, a mug for the cocoa hut), and skiers in bright shells walk the street (`walkers.json`).
- **The Snowcap Lodge** (`hoarfell-lodge`, `scenes/hoarfell_lodge.py`, through the SNOWCAP LODGE door; an `indoors` world — no snow, no cold): the great room at alpenglow — log walls, a river-stone fireplace with old skis crossed over the mantel and boots steaming on the hearth, two deep chairs facing it (seats), windows either side onto the range going pink (`peaks` views), Barley the St Bernard asleep on the rug with his keg, the BAR (Mette Rask, `npc-mette`: cocoa, fondue, mulled cider, chestnuts — all of them keep the cold off a while), the stair up to the ROOMS (`lodge_room`, 15 coins: you walk across the floor to it — a search over the open tiles — and climb it, posed on each tread with the ordinary walking sheet (`setPose({kind: 'walk', …, lift, light})`: lifted by the tread's height, lit from the floor at its foot) and through the lamplit door at the top; the night passes in the dark; you wake rested and warm and come back down), a long table, the ski rack by the door. `js/lodge.js` (BridgeLodge): the FIRE warms you for a quarter of an hour; THE BOARD lists your best times on the pistes and the face, the park against Juno's, who you've beaten and your dice; and the round table is **Fell Dice** with Old Bryn (`npc-bryn`, careful) and Sigrun (`npc-sigrun`, the lift mechanic, bluffs) — liar's dice for a stake of 10 / 25 / 50 each (the pot is three stakes): five dice a cup, flakes (1s) wild, bid how many of a face are under all the cups or call the last bid a lie, the loser of a call loses a die, last with dice takes the pot. The AI weighs each bid by the binomial odds from its own dice (`chance`) with a calling threshold and a bluff rate per player (`RIVALS`). Sitting down warms them to you once a day (`BridgeTalk.nudge`); saved in `bridge_lodge`. Music: the `hoarfell` theme over `fire` and `murmur`.
- Sounds: the `hoarfell` theme (lydian bells) over `snowwind` and `chimes`, the summit's sparse `summit` theme over `gale`. Fishing: arctic char and frostfin through the ice (`pool: 'ice'`). Items: chalk, climbing rope, ice axe, skis, ski goggles, hot cocoa, roast chestnuts, edelweiss, hoar and summit crystals. The journal lists your ascents and best runs.

### Sylvara: Elderglade and the wood elves
The seventh system on the nav chart (`forest` in `js/orbit.js`: one forest pole to pole, dark lakes, silver rivers, gold elf-lights, mist, a silver-green moon). Built for Alyssa: the wood elves' queen is Queen Alyssara and Beckolas is her first sword. (Alyssara is Alyssa's elven name, as Beckolas is Beck's — and it keeps the AI from mixing the queen up with a pilot named Alyssa. Her id and sheet stay `alyssa` / `npc-alyssa`, so bonds and saves carry over.) Two scenes on the `sylvara` tileset, kit `tools/bridge-art/assets_elves.py` (giant trees with root flares and moss, the Great Tree with its glowing arched door, round windows and a lantern-hung walkway, birches, ferns, glowing mushrooms, stepping-stone trails, leaf lanterns and lantern posts, the Moonwell, a willow, the herb stall, a harp, mossy rocks, fallen logs):
- **Elderglade** (`sylvara`, `scenes/sylvara.py`): the glade at dusk among giants of the old wood; the Great Tree at the top (THE GREAT TREE door into the hall), elves' doors in two trunks, the waystone where the trails meet, Thaelin the bard on his stone seat (`npc-elf-bard`, people.json `elf_bard`), Mirelle at the east end of her herb stall (`npc-elf-herbalist`, `elf_herbalist`; she used to stand behind it, where its leaf awning hid her completely — the tiles behind it are blocked now, and she's lit by the lantern beside her, `light`), the Moonwell under its willow (west), and the landing ring (south-east) with Aerin the warden (`npc-elf-warden`, `elf_warden`). Three more elves walk the glade (`walkers.json`, each one you can talk to): Faelan the lamplighter (`elf_lamplighter`) slowly up and down the main path between the Great Tree and the ring, Sefa the weaver (`elf_weaver`) along the west path from the waystone toward the Moonwell, and young Pip (`elf_child`) running about the Moonwell and the flowers. Every elf has tastes in `js/gifts.js` (bar the queen and Beckolas).
- **The Heartwood Hall** (`sylvara-hall`, `scenes/sylvara_hall.py`): inside the Great Tree — ribbed heartwood walls, roots arching over a great round window on the evening wood, the floor polished across the tree's rings, a moss runner to a dais and a throne of living branches under a canopy of boughs and leaf lanterns; Queen Alyssara on it (`alyssa`), Beckolas at her right hand (`beckolas`); the harp, the Moonwell basin, root benches, banners of a gold leaf on green.
- **The quest** `queens-favour` (*The Queen's Favour*, 300): kneel to the queen and she asks you, quietly, to find the ribbon Beckolas lost from his sword; the bard and the herbalist have seen things; it's caught high in the willow over the Moonwell; give it back to Beckolas and he writes to you. Any order (Alyssa: who you talk to first shouldn't matter): the ribbon's in the willow from the start, and the quest is done once all five are found.
