- **The sequel** `under-the-willow` (*Under the Willow*, 400; `needs: 'queens-favour'`), Alyssa's: the queen loves her first sword, he loves her, and the whole wood knows except possibly the two of them. Sweet, with humour. The queen confides in you (*Something on your mind?*); Beckolas denies everything, then admits it (three hundred years, two hundred and twelve tries, one letter he ate); Thaelin (the Moonwell at dusk, where everyone says the true thing), Mirelle (a moonflower that only opens for someone who means it), Faelan (the good lanterns, saved six hundred years) and Sefa (ribbons of his favour's silk — she wove a whole bolt in the spring); Beckolas with the flower, then the queen (told: the Moonwell, at dusk); and at the willow, once everything else is done (`after: '*'`), the moment (the clue's `say`): the flower opens in his hand, he says it all in one breath, she says "Finally." All of it in any order — start with the glade, the queen or Beckolas — except what has to come first: the flower before you can give it, telling Beckolas before telling the queen, everything before the moment. Each step is a topic that appears on its person as you find it (`when` / `until`), first in the list and lit up; Pip has gossip before and after. **The willow keepsake** stays up for good afterwards: Faelan's lanterns and Sefa's ribbons in the willow (`E.willow_keepsake`, its own dice so the rest of the glade renders as before; the scene's `willow: lit` layer, switched on by `BridgeSylvara.decorate` as the glade loads or the moment it happens, each lantern breathing a little warm light from `scene.keepsake`), and the willow's own words follow the story. The queen and Beckolas have story beats for it in `api/_personas.json`.
- `js/worlds/sylvara.js` (BridgeSylvara, the `over` hook): fireflies wandering and blinking, gold leaves falling, motes off the Moonwell; dust turning in the hall's window light. Music: the `sylvara` theme (a slow lydian harp, flute answering) over `breeze`, `birds` and `chimes` (the hall: `murmur`, `chimes`). Both the queen and Beckolas have persona sheets in `api/_personas.json`.


### Sylvara's city, the Silverrun and the Deepwood (workstream F)
Beck's asks for Sylvara (`_claude_docs/spec-denser-worlds.md`, *Sylvara*): the elves' city in three tiers, a canoe down the river to Mirror Lake, the Deepwood, brewing with Mirelle, and bows. Every place is a scene in the house style on the `sylvara` tileset, built with the city's kit `tools/bridge-art/assets_sylvan.py` (a heightfield `Land` with its own albedos, `water`, the ridged `ancient_trunk` and `old_tree`, ferns, moss, leaf litter, mushrooms giant and small, stalls under leaf awnings, looms, bow racks, the basket lift, spiral stairs, rope bridges and decks, pod houses, the hollow bough, branch lanterns, zip posts and anchors, hay and leaf targets). Elderglade and the Heartwood Hall are another session's; this workstream never edits their scripts or JSON — the ways out of them are added as the worlds load (`BridgeSylvara.JOINS`, `joins()`: Elderglade's east edge at (44, 18) into the Rootmarket, its south path at (17, 31) down to the Silverrun, and the hall's stair at (1, 7) up to the Crown).
- **The forest floor — the Rootmarket** (`sylvara-roots`, `scenes/sylvara_roots.py`, 48 × 36): a square of mossy flagstones among the giants' roots, eight stalls under leaf awnings — **Corin** the bowyer at the great trunk (`elf_bowyer`, `npc-elf-bowyer`: elven bows, rope arrows, the Heartwood Longbow — `SHOPS.bowyer`), **Wren** the lantern maker in the south-west (`elf_lanternmaker`: fire arrows), **Ysane** at the weavers' looms (`elf_loomer`, seated), **Merrow**'s grocery (`elf_grocer`: starpears, waybread, honey), the basket lift up to the Boughs and its keeper **Brannoc** (`elf_liftkeeper`), a stair round a trunk to the same. East, **Aerin's archery range** behind hedges (the world's `range`: the line, three lanes of hay targets on rails, the pennant): Aerin keeps it from the ninth hour to the seventeenth of Sylvara's day and the landing ring the rest (`BridgeSylvara.aerin` moves her; a RANGE BOARD says so). The crowd's `sylvara` wardrobe fills the square.
- **The canopy — the Boughs** (`sylvara-boughs`, 48 × 36): ring platforms round five trunks high in the trees (the ground far below, `FLOOR`), joined by rope bridges that run straight (movement is four ways), elves' pod houses on hung decks, the **hollow bough** whose door leads into **the tavern** (`sylvara-tavern`: Fennick, `elf_tavernkeeper`, cider, elderflower wine and honey cake; a firebowl), the lift and stair down to the Rootmarket, the queen's trunk stair up to the Crown. Things only a bow reaches: five **high lanterns** (`lanterns`, group `boughs`) lit by fire arrows; a leaf target on a rope (`switches`) that lets the ladder down into the **nook** (a carved box, `CACHES.nook`); and the **zip line** to the Owl's Rest — a rope arrow into its anchor tree rigs it (`zips`), E rides it, and the owl's tree has the wardens' cache. `arrows: 'open'`: arrows fly over the gaps.
- **The crown** (`sylvara-crown`, 40 × 30, a backdrop world over the sky — `registerBackground('sylvara')`, the moon in its real phase): the queen's bower above the leaves, a garden ring round the Great Tree's top (its heart door down into the hall), and the **star platform** with **Ithil** the star-reader (`elf_starreader`), the telescope (the night sky) and the moon dial (`BridgeClock.moon`: the phase, and how long till full).
- **The Silverrun** (`sylvara-river`, 52 × 40): down from Elderglade or the Rootmarket to the bridge village — pod houses on a deck across the river — the canoe dock with its rack and **Tamsin** the boatwright (`elf_boatwright`), the **otter weir** (a chute through it; otters play at (27.6, 21.6)) and the **river-island shrine** (leave a moonpetal: the river's blessing, the canoe 25% quicker for 20 minutes). On to **the falls** (`sylvara-falls`, 50 × 40, two levels — the gorge built 1.9 low and shifted north so its rows line up with the tiles; nothing on the lower level may hide a player): rapids through rocks, a landing above the falls with a warning post and a stair down, the pool below (spray and a rainbow by day), the ledge **behind the falls** into the cave (`sylvara-fallcave`: crystals, a carving of the river's story, a box in a dry niche), and the **fork** — the Deep Run off east into the Deepwood. Then **Mirror Lake** (`sylvara-lake`, 50 × 38): still enough to hold the moon (the live light's still water), a beach landing and a rack, the **Moonwell's source** on the west shore (once a day it fills your health and energy; on a full-moon night it gives three moonpetals), and a path home to Elderglade. No lake house.
- **By canoe** (`js/canoe.js`, BridgeCanoe; see its header for the world JSON's `river` block): take one from a rack (E), paddle with the arrow keys — the canoe turns toward the way you push and the current carries you; white water (`'2'`) runs fast and bumps; banks slide you along. Off a stretch's end you're carried into the next still in your canoe (`down` / `up` / `forks`); E beside a landing steps ashore and moors it there (E on it again to go on). A mode (`BridgeWorld.setMode`, solo) and a pose (`setPose({ kind: 'canoe', item })` — js/character.js draws a pose's own `item`); the hull and the near gunwale from `assets/scenes/canoe` (`tools/bridge-art/canoe_sprites.py`, 16 headings) with your seated sheet between, a paddle drawn live, glints on the current. Others see you in yours (js/multi.js `POSE_KIND.canoe` → `BridgeCanoe.remoteItem`).
- **The Deepwood** (`sylvara-deepwood`, 52 × 38; off the falls and the Rootmarket's north-east path): wonder over menace — glowing mushroom groves under giant caps, a stream and the frogs' pool, flowering meadows, butterflies, glowmoths at night; **the Mossback**, an ancient tortoise asleep under a shell grown over with moss and a birch (`kind: 'mossback'`: its dreams, and on a full-moon night it opens an eye and speaks); **Sorrel**, the Deepwood's forager, at the hollow (`elf_hermit`); the heart oak's mossed-over box (`CACHES.deep`). Herbs grow here and along the river (assets/forage.json).
- **Brewing with Mirelle** (`js/brewing.js`, BridgeBrewing; her *Brew with me* topic, `sylvan: 'brew'`): herbs from the wood (moonpetal, emberroot, owlmoss, windthistle, silverleaf, dreamcap — forage, or her stock), a crystal from Lumar, honey from Verdance, into potions that matter elsewhere — the **Hearthfire Draught** (the cold can't touch you on Hoarfell, 30 min), **Owl's-Eye Tincture** (night eyes: Arcadia's blackout lifts round you, js/weather.js), **Swiftwater Tonic** (×1.15 walking, 15 min), **Tidecalm Elixir** (the sloop quicker), **Silverleaf Salve** (health and energy at once), **Dreamcap Draught** (hidden things glint, as the Glimmer Lantern shows them). js/magic.js `has` / `speed` ask `BridgeBrewing.active`, so everything that already asks about the cold, speed and the lantern hears it. Food and drink of the wood: waybread, starpears, starpear cider, elderflower wine. Kept in `bridge_brewing`; the city's own state (caches, the source, the shrine) in `bridge_sylvara`.
- **People talk the city's business** through `{ sylvan: '<kind>' }` topics (js/interactions.js hands them to `BridgeSylvara.topic`): Corin's and Fennick's shops, Aerin's *Shoot a round*, Mirelle's cauldron. Interactions of type `sylvan` (`BridgeSylvara.use`, registered lazily as interactions.js loads after it) do the rest by `kind`: `range`, `zip`, `canoe`, `launch`, `brew`, `lift`, `cache`, `stars`, `dial`, `shrine`, `source`, `mossback`.
- Tests: `world/sylvara_places.js` (every way between the places lands on open ground, the joins, Corin's shop, a round at the range, the Boughs' lantern / switch / box / zip, brewing and what the potions do, the shrine and the source, shots of every place at noon, golden hour and night) and `world/canoe.js` (down the river by canoe to the falls, ashore, up the Deep Run into the Deepwood; the pool's rack down to Mirror Lake, ashore on the beach); `combat/bow.js` for the bows.

### Fighting
Creatures are `hostile` interactions run by the stalk-and-strike machine in `interactions.js` (idle → winding, the telegraph → striking → recovering; a strike lands only if you're still on the tile it locked onto, so stepping aside on the wind-up dodges it). `js/combat.js` (BridgeCombat) gives them a `creature` kind — health, damage, pace, drops, coins — and draws them in code on the 16-unit grid, gliding between tiles and depth-tested in scene worlds: **rock mites** (mine floors 3+), **cave crabs** (8+), **shades** (14+). Weapons are items with `damage`; F (or B on touch, or the right mouse button) swings the selected one, a weapon with `arc` cuts the tiles either side of the one you face too: the Iron Dagger (25) and the **Cutlass** (40, arc; the smithy on the square) and the **Star Blade** (80, arc; Hob forges it from 2 star iron, a songstone and coin). A pickaxe on F digs the rock you face, or hits a creature. At 0 health you black out (`BridgeCombat.faint`) and come round somewhere safe with half your health — in the mine, on a crate in the hall with Hob watching; on Midnight, on the pier under Morwen's blanket. The Plated Suit's +50 health is for this.

Playing together, creatures are shared and the fighting is co-op — see *Fighting together* under Multiplayer.

**How a weapon swings** (`js/arms.js`, BridgeArms) — added when Beck asked for "better animations… each weapon its own unique animation and a good kind of feel to it". Every weapon belongs to a **class** (items.js `cls`; anything else with `damage` counts as a blade, or a sword if it sweeps), and each class has its own rendered swings off the pilot's attack sheet (see *Character sprite sheets*), its own timing, sound and weight. A swing is a list of key poses with milliseconds each (`CLASSES`), and the blow lands on its **landing frame**, not on the key press — whatever's on the tiles then is struck (a frame late enough to skip the whole swing — a quick blade's is ~120 ms — still lands it; such hits used to go missing). Through the wind-up and the landing you're held where you stand; in the recovery a step cuts it short, and pressing again chains into the next swing (within 420 ms of the last, `CHAIN_GAP`; a press during a swing is remembered). A weapon's `rate` scales its class's timing (the Veilpiercer's 170 against a blade's 260 runs every swing at 0.65).
- **Quick blade** (Iron Dagger, Veilpiercer, Twin Fangs): a jab, a backhand cut, then a lunging stab — ×1.5 and a tile further. ~180–300 ms a swing. Light hit-stop (40 ms).
- **Sword** (Cutlass, Star Blade, Moonglass Sabre): a wide sweep from right to left, back again left to right, and the third of a chain a full spin that cuts all eight tiles round you (×1.25).
- **Axe** (Rime Hatchet, Emberbrand — a broad blade that chops): up over the shoulder and down in a diagonal chop with weight behind it; the second, from the other shoulder, bites ×1.3. The camera kicks harder.
- **Polearm** (Boarding Pike): drawn back two-handed and thrust; a second thrust; the third a long lunge a tile further (×1.3) that pushes the creature back.
- **Maul** (War Maul): swung back, heaved up overhead, slammed down: a ground shock (`BridgeFX.spawnShock`, a ring and cracks) hits the tiles either side and beyond for 40%, the screen shakes, 110 ms of hit-stop.
- **Greatsword** (Tidebreaker, class `great`): swung off the maul's poses (`poses: 'maul'`) — lifted overhead two-handed and brought down in a cleave across three tiles.
- **Staff and wand** (Wispcaller, Moonlit Wand): raised, then thrust out — the bolt leaves the tip on the thrust (`BridgeMagic.cast` plays the swing and launches on its landing frame).
- **Pick** (the pickaxes, the ice axe, the hoe): up over the head and down on the tile in front — on a rock it breaks rock (`BridgeMine` strikes it on the landing frame, by E or F), on tilled-to-be earth it tills (`js/farm.js`), on a creature it's a blunt blow.
- **Bow** (the Elven Bow, the Heartwood Longbow — Sylvara, workstream F): F held **draws** (nock → half → full in 900 ms, `DRAW_MS`), let go and it **looses** — the longer the draw, the further and harder: a snap flies 3 tiles for a third of the damage, a full draw the bow's `range` (10, the longbow 12) for ×1.25. `js/bows.js` (BridgeBows) flies the arrow: like the wand's bolts, down the way you face, over low things (a world's `arrows: 'open'` lets it over every gap), stopped by walls and trunks, sticking in what stops it; the first creature it meets is struck. Plain arrows are free; **rope arrows** and **fire arrows** are items used from the hotbar with a bow in your pack (the bow comes to your hands): a rope arrow into a zip line's anchor tree rigs it (`zips` in a world: E at either end rides it, hanging from the line), a fire arrow lights a high lantern (`lanterns`, in groups) and burns what it hits. `switches` (a leaf target on a rope) let something down when hit — a rope ladder, the cells it opens. Aerin's archery range (`range`: the line, lanes of targets sliding on rails, the pennant showing the wind, which blows arrows sideways; the same wind for everyone that minute): ten arrows, scored 10/8/6/4/2 by how near the middle (far lanes ×1.5), prizes the first time (60 fire arrows, 80 rope arrows, 90 the Heartwood Longbow). Kept in localStorage `bridge_bows`. **Its poses are a sheet of their own** (`pilot-bow[-<style>]-<a|b>-<head>.png`, `attacks.BOW`, laid out in `assets/characters/bow.json`; `characters.py … pilot-bow-<a|b>`, `MODES=bow pilots.sh`), so the attack sheets never re-render for it; the class says `sheet: 'bow'` and js/character.js draws from it. The bows (`weapon_sprites.BOWS`) are built upright with their string and a nocked arrow added by `attack_rig.py`, which pulls the string to the drawing hand frame by frame (`draw`, `arrow` in a pose); `WEAPON_IDS=elven_bow,…` renders only those weapons. Others see your draw and your arrow (`atk` `k: 'd'`, then the loose with its power, `p`; BridgeBows.ghost flies it on their screen, harmless). Test: `combat/bow.js`.
- **Charged swing**: keep F (B, the right button) held after a swing and the class's wind-up pose holds, motes drawn in to the hand, the pilot shaking once it's full (650 ms, `CHARGE_MS`); let go full for the big one — energy by the class's weight (`cost`: 2 for a blade or a staff, 3 for a sword, an axe, a pike or a pick, 4 for a maul or a greatsword): the blade's lunge ×2.2 a tile further, the sword's double spin ×2 all round that knocks them back, the axe's chop ×2.3 across three tiles, the pike's long lunge ×2.2 two tiles further, the maul's quake ×1.8 over the 3×3 in front, the greatsword's cleave ×2, a staff's bolt ×2, bigger, going on through whatever it meets, a pick ×2 into the rock. Let go early and nothing happens. (The item's own use runs again, charged — `pendingCharge` — so a pick still finds its rock.)
- **Dodge roll**: Shift (the ROLL button on touch, over A): up to two tiles the way you're pushing the stick or keys (else the way you face), stopping at whatever's in the way, 380 ms, 3 energy; for the first 85% of it nothing touches you (`BridgeArms.dodging()`: a strike that resolves on you then misses — *DODGED*). Rendered (the `roll-` columns: a dive, three frames balled up head over heels, up again), with dust where you set off and land. `BridgeCharacter.roll` moves you there, easing out.
- **Energy and the second wind** — Beck: "it uses a little bit too much energy too quickly". A plain swing costs nothing; a roll 3 (was 5), a charged blow 2–4 by class (was 4 for all), a staff's or wand's bolt 2 (was 3; a charged bolt 4, was 7). Everything a fight takes goes through `BridgeArms.spend(n)`, and three quarters of it comes back once you stop for a breath (`breathe`, each frame): 1.2 s with no swing, charge, roll or cast (`WIND_DELAY`) and it returns a point every 125 ms (8 a second, `WIND_EVERY`, `WIND_BACK` 0.75). Keep swinging and nothing comes back, so spamming still empties you; energy spent off the fight (a pick on rock, the cold, the hoe) never comes back — food and rest still fill you up. The EN bar shows what's still to come back as a pale end (`BridgeArms.winded()`, js/inventory.js). Too tired to roll, charge or cast: *TOO TIRED* over your head (no dialog box mid-fight). Before: no regen at all, so twenty rolls emptied you till you ate or slept.
- **Feel**: a landed hit freezes the swing and the creature for a moment (the class's `stop`: 40 ms for a blade up to 110 for the maul, longer for a finisher or a charged blow; the creature's own clocks are held too, so it doesn't strike through it) and it shudders, then recoils a few pixels away from you; the camera leans toward the blow (`BridgeWorld.kick`); the swing leaves a crisp arc of light through the tip's path (`BridgeFX.spawnTrail`, from the weapon's anchors, in its colour — steel, ember orange, frost blue, moonglass, star gold…); the hit throws an impact in its element (`BridgeFX.spawnImpact`: a steel star and sparks, frost shards, embers rising, a pillar of holy light, green drain wisps drawn back to you, dust and grit for heavy blows). Each class has its own whoosh and its own blow (`js/audio.js` `swing_<class>`, `impact_<class>`, plus `roll`, `dodge`, `charge`, `charged`).
- **Knock back**: `heavy` a tile (as before), a pike finisher or a charged sword spin a tile, the charged maul a tile — `w.push`, settled by `BridgeCombat.onHit` (a tile at a time, stopping at whatever's in the way; never the boss).
- **Weapons in hand**: the selected hotbar weapon is carried while you stand and walk (blades forward and down, the pike and the staff upright, the maul over the shoulder) and is in your hands for every swing — rendered with the pilot (see *Character sprite sheets*). New weapons: a model in `weapon_sprites.py`, an item with `cls`, `WEAPONS=only pilots.sh`.
- **The smithy on the square** also sells the **Twin Fangs** (18 a cut, quick, `twin`: a knife in each hand, every cut lands twice; 200) and the **Tidebreaker** (62, sweeps, slow; 480).
- **Stats everywhere a weapon's shown** (`BridgeArms.card` / `stats` / `line`): damage, speed (from the class's swings and the weapon's `rate`: Very quick, Quick, Normal, Slow, Very slow), reach, the class and what it does, each trait as an icon and a line (*Frost — slows what it hits for 3 s*, *Holy — ×1.6 against the undead*, *Sweep — hits the tiles either side*, *Heavy — staggers a wind-up and knocks back a tile*, *Drain — heals you a quarter of the damage*, *Twin*, *Quick*, *Bolt*, *Breaks rock*) and how it compares with what you're holding. See *The game menu* for where.

**Midnight Isle is where the fighting is** (the mine aside). As the island loads, `BridgeCombat.decorate` swaps the old JSON sentinels for its `ROSTER` (all spawned in code, on walkable cells): **Stone Sentinels** on the processional way (slow and heavy; cracked past half health, their blow becomes a slam that hits the tiles round the one they lock onto), **Drowned Wights** in the graveyard (struck, they may blink 2–3 tiles away — `blink`), **Will-o'-wisps** by the moon pool and the north woods (casters: `range` 4 — they keep their distance, line up with you and throw a bolt down the line; step off it), **Barrow Hounds** by the abbey (fast, short wind-up), and once a day **The Bell Warden** in the abbey ruins (a boss: a health bar across the top, a slam over a 3×3, a toll that rings out on the square two tiles from it — stand close or stand clear — and at two thirds and a third it calls two wisps; `bridge_midnight.day`). It drops the **Wispcaller Staff** the first time. One wight carries the **Shell Key** for the violet TOMB (`midnight_tomb`), which holds the **Veilpiercer**. Nobody walks Midnight's paths.
- **Reading a fight:** every creature's wind-up marks the tiles it will hit on the ground (`dangerCells`, a `ground` hook on the lumar and cave tilesets): under each, a pool of colour lying flat on the floor (a soft round glow, a body and an edge of whole pixels) with a second pixel ring closing in onto its edge as the blow comes, flaring when it strikes — red for a blow or a slam, gold for the toll's ring, violet for a bolt's target. A creature's blow rakes three slashes across the tile it struck, in its colour (`BridgeFX.spawnStrike`, `RAKE`); a wisp's bolt arcs over as a round glow with a pixel core and motes behind, its shadow on the ground under it, and lands in a ring; the warden's toll goes out as a pixel ring. A hit on you shakes the screen (`BridgeCombat.shake`) and flashes you white-hot then red on your own pixels with a flinch (`BridgeCharacter.hurt`, drawn in `drawFighter`; the room's light lifts while it lasts so the flash isn't muddied); a hit on them flashes them white while the blow holds them, then a red blush fading (their shadow and health bar go on after, so the flash lights only the creature). The marks, bolts, rings, flash, frost and burning are all drawn on `performance.now()`, the clock the hits and wind-ups keep — the world's draw clock is `Date.now()` (the marks, the wisps' bolts and the toll were once on the wrong one: the bolts and the toll never showed). Creatures path round headstones and plinths (`pathStep`, a short BFS when the straight step is blocked).
- **No boxes** — Beck: "it's a little janky with those red squares that pop up… we don't need those. And there's also a little square around the floating orbs when they fall." Nothing a fight draws is a filled or outlined rectangle: no red/orange square over a struck tile or over you (the old `spawnHitFlash` / `spawnDamageFlash`, gone), no square marks on the ground, no square bolts, no square halo round loot. Round things go through `BridgeFX.disc` (a disc of whole pixels, `squash` to lie it on the ground), `ring` (its pixel outline) and `glow` (a soft radial glow); sparks are short streaks, not outlined squares; loot glows round and soft with its icon the crisp core, over a pixel-oval shadow; numbers are in Pixelify Sans (the font index.html loads — they were asking for one it doesn't) with a dark outline all round, popping in. `tools/tests/combat/look.js` shoots each moment and fails on any rectangle 6 × 6 or bigger from the fight's own drawing (sprites and health bars aside).
- **Weapon traits** (items with `damage`, read by `weaponUse` and `BridgeCombat.onHit`): `arc` sweeps the tiles either side; `reach` 2 hits the second tile too; `heavy` staggers a wind-up and knocks the creature back a tile; `frost` slows it (moves and wind-ups ×1.7, 3 s); `burn` sets it burning (3 a tick); `holy` bites the undead (wisps, wights, shades, the warden) ×1.6; `drain` gives you back a quarter of what it takes; `rate` scales its class's swing timing (js/arms.js); `twin` strikes twice; `cls` is its class. The smithy adds the **Boarding Pike** (34, reach 2; 160) and the **War Maul** (58, heavy, slow; 240); **Morwen's ARCANE CASE** in her tower sells the **Rime Hatchet** (38, frost; 340), the **Emberbrand** (44, burns; 380) and the **Moonglass Sabre** (50, arc, holy; 460); found: the **Veilpiercer** (32, quick, drains) and the **Wispcaller Staff** (42, holy; a wisp thrown seven tiles, 2 energy — `BridgeMagic.cast` with `bolt: 'wisp'`). Wisps drop **Wisp Embers**.

### Arcadia below: the Undercity and the Sump (js/undercity.js)
Workstream C of `_claude_docs/spec-denser-worlds.md` (Beck: "ritzier as you go up, poor as you go down… dangerous at the
bottom… bars and nightclubs at the bottom… below it should still have restaurants"). Two levels under the District —
**the Undercity** (B20) and **the Sump** (B60) — built in the house style (`tools/bridge-art/assets_under.py`, the kit:
`shanty` Kowloon stacks of units each in its own cladding, windows lit or caged, AC units, washing; `column` the city's
great columns; `concrete` poured slabs with cracks, stains and patches; `stall` hawker stalls under tarps; `fluoro`
hanging tubes, `sodium` lamps, `caged_bulb`; `grate_drip` where the rain comes through; `drum_fire`, `chainlink`,
`scrap_heap`, `barrels`, `crates`, `mattress_corner`; `rooftop` a low block's crowded roof seen from above; `stair_up`,
`stair_down`; `room` an interior's shell, `pillar`, `long_table`; and for the Sump `canal` (`canal_water`, the teal glow
under it), `quay`, `bollard`, `sampan`). Always night: the scenes render `full` / `dim` light states (sodium orange and
fluorescent green below, teal on black water in the Sump), the toy finish from `scene_post.py`.

| World | Script | What |
|---|---|---|
| `arcadia-undercity` | `scenes/arcadia_undercity.py` | **The Warrens**, the hub (80 × 36): the stacks along the back with the doors in their ground storeys — the stair up to the Backs, Mama Roo's noodle counter, the Hawker Hall's gate, The Drip, Sallow's Chrome, the Foundry, the Pit, Gage's scrap counter, Tally's betting window, the Core Express lobby (B20) — a market lane of stalls, a low Kowloon block down the middle (the grill, the curry stall, a pawn shop, the mahjong den, the capsule hostel, the meds counter, a hawker stall, a noodle stall, the KTV; its roofs crowded with tanks, lofts, sheds and greens) with alleys through it, the south lane's tables and drum fire, the drain canal along the front with the stairwell down to the Sump, and east behind chain-link the Rust Saints' turf and the gate to the scrapyards |
| `arcadia-hawker` | `arcadia_hawker.py` | **The Hawker Hall** (40 × 24): fourteen stalls round the walls and back to back down the middle, long tables, pillars, lanterns, the kitchen god |
| `arcadia-dive` | `arcadia_dive.py` | **The Drip** (20 × 15): the bar under a wall of bottles (Nell), booths, a pool table, the jukebox, the bucket under the leak |
| `arcadia-chopshop` | `arcadia_chopshop.py` | **Sallow's Chrome** (16 × 13): the chair under the ring lamp (street chrome, js/implants.js), the wall of parts, eyes in jars, the bench (med-kits and stims) |
| `arcadia-foundry` | `arcadia_foundry.py` | **The Foundry** (32 × 22): an industrial club in a smelting hall — the furnace lit again, Static on the decks, a steel-grating floor lit red from under, the ladle hung over it, cages, the conveyor bar |
| `arcadia-pit` | `arcadia_pit.py` | **The Pit** (36 × 24): the cage of sand in the middle (world JSON `undercity.ring`), bleachers, the bar, the bell, Tally's book |
| `arcadia-scrapyard` | `arcadia_scrapyard.py` | **The Scrapyards** (64 × 36): heaps, stacked crushed cars, the magnet crane, the compactor, the Saints' shacks, Saint Ruin's throne |
| `arcadia-sump` | `arcadia_sump.py` | **The Sump** (84 × 40): the drowned original street — four quays between canals with footbridges, the old shopfronts with their tide line, the towers' feet; Old Teo's noodle boat, the drowned shrine and its gate standing in the water, the freight lift (B60), the old Central station's entrance |
| `arcadia-sump-rave` | `arcadia_sump_rave.py` | **Central**, the rave (34 × 22): a drowned station platform under its tiled vault, the flooded track bed, a half-sunk carriage made the bar, Lux on the decks, lasers |

- **Joined**: the Backs ⇄ the Warrens (until the Backs' rebuild lands, the way down is its manhole, added by
  `BridgeUndercity.decorate`; after, a stairwell goes into `arcadia_alley.py`), every door both ways, the Warrens ⇄ the
  Sump by the stairwell on the canal and by the Core Express (`express_lift`, shaft `core`, lobbies `undercity` and `sump`
  in `assets/arcadia-lifts.json`, with `places` entries for the Map's cutaway). Until js/transit.js lands, `decorate`
  turns `express_lift` into this module's own chooser of the shaft's stops.
- **The `undercity` block** (world JSON, written by the scene scripts): `level` (`undercity` / `sump`), `drips` ([x, row,
  height]: rain falling in a stream from that height onto the tile), `tubes` (fluorescents that stutter), `turf` (`rect`:
  where the fighters stand), `safe` (rects nobody spawns in), `boss` (the boss's tile), `ring` (the Pit's cage), `comeRound`.
  The scene's `steam` is drawn too. The arcadia scene hooks here draw the drips, the steam, the failing tubes, the dark
  of the underside overhead, and the fights (BridgeCombat's ground / items / over, exported for this).
- **Food and drink below** (items.js; sold by label, `SHOPS`): Mama Roo's Broth, Char Skewers, Steam Dumplings, Kopi,
  Teo's Laksa, Drip Lager, Foundry Tonic, Pit Rotgut, Glow Shot. **Scrap** (Gage buys it, `act: 'scrap'`): Scrap Metal,
  Circuit Board, Chrome Part, Sump Pearl, Eel Fillet (Teo buys eel).
- **Fighting, tougher the lower you go** (js/combat.js CREATURES): the Undercity — **Rust Saints** (`saint` with a pipe,
  `shiv` with a knife: people, drawn from their own painted sheets with the weapon over them, leaning into the wind-up),
  **scrap rats**; the Sump — **scav drones** (fly, a red bolt down a line), **sump eels**, **husks** (chromed-over drifters
  that blink away). `ROSTER` places them in each place's `turf` from a hash of the place and the day, so two pilots see
  the same ones. Once a day a boss: **Saint Ruin** on his throne in the scrapyards (a chrome fist; every other blow a
  slam; calls two Saints at two thirds and a third), **the Dredge** in the Sump's south basin (900 health, its scoop
  slams a 3 × 3, calls drones). Their first kill drops a trophy Doc Sallow fits (`ruin_knuckles`, `dredge_heart`).
  Blacking out below brings you round at Mama Roo's counter (the Undercity) or on the shrine's steps (the Sump):
  `onFaint`.
- **Things to do** (`under` interactions, by `act`): `scrap` (Gage), `search` (a scrap pile, once a day each — scrap, a
  board, chrome, or a rat out at you), `ring` (the Pit's cage: 20 to get in, three rounds against the house — two
  Saints, the knife sisters, a husk from the Sump — 30, 60 and 120 coins, 50 more for the night's first win), `bets`
  (Tally's book: the house bout of the twenty-minute slot, the same fighters and winner for everyone), `shrine` (a coin
  and a bow: health and energy full once a day), `boat` (Teo's laksa; he buys eel), `dance` (the Foundry's floor and the
  rave's platform: +40 energy once a day each), `lift`.
- **People** (people.json, characters.py, portraits): Mama Roo (`npc-roo`), Brick on the Foundry's door
  (`npc-bouncer`), Tally the bookie (`npc-bookie`), Gage (`npc-gage`), Doc Sallow (`npc-sallow`), Nell (`npc-nell`),
  the hawkers (`npc-hawker-a/b`), Static (`npc-static`), Old Teo (`npc-teo`), Sister Wen (`npc-wen`), Lux (`npc-lux`);
  the Rust Saints (`npc-saint-a/b`, `npc-saint-boss`) and the husks (`npc-husk`) are the fighters' sheets. Crowds:
  `assets/crowds.json` (the `undercity` and `sump` wardrobes in js/crowd.js).
- **Small happenings** (js/events.js): **the Saints come collecting** down the Warrens' market lane (stand up to them
  for a broth and twenty coins, once a happening), **a downpour through the grates** (rain in sheets over the Warrens),
  **something moving in the canal** in the Sump (rings going out over the south basin; people back off the edge).
- Test: `tools/tests/world/undercity.js` (every place, the doors both ways, nothing walled off, the rosters the same on
  two pages and tougher below, a fight and coming round, the shops, Gage, the book, a pile, the shrine, Teo, the lift,
  the cage's three rounds, implants).

### Implants (js/implants.js, BridgeImplants)
Chrome you have put in, in six slots — **eyes** (criticals ×1.75), **arm** (damage), **skin** (each blow on you lighter by
a few points), **legs** (speed), **core** (health), **lungs** (energy) — one in each. Three tiers (Beck: "back-alley
chrome below (cheap, now and then it glitches), clinic-grade on the District, designer up top"): **street** at Sallow's
Chrome in the Warrens (cheap; now and then it glitches — the arm seizes for a swing, the legs lock, the plate drops out,
the eyes fill with static, the core spikes and takes energy — about once in four minutes a piece walking about, once a
minute in a fight); **clinic** at the Chrome Clinic's IMPLANT SUITE (the holo-display; about twice the numbers, never
glitches; it also **tunes** a street piece steady for 60); **designer** up top (Workstream D's boutique: the best
numbers, a finish). And **trophies** fitted at Sallow's from what the bosses leave. Saved in localStorage
`bridge_implants`.
- Where it feeds the game: `BridgeGear.bonus()` adds `hp` and `energy` (max health and energy follow), `BridgeGear.speed()`
  adds `speed`, `BridgeCombat.damageFor` multiplies by `BridgeImplants.damage()` and rolls `crit()`, interactions.js takes
  a creature's blow through `absorb(dmg)`.
- **API** (for D's boutique): `BridgeImplants.open({ shop: 'designer' })` opens a shop's chooser (`SHOPS`: `street`,
  `clinic`, `designer`; `shop(id, { title, who, tiers, tune, line })` registers another), `register(id, { slot, tier,
  name, price, perk: { damage, armour, speed, hp, energy, crit }, blurb, finish, item })` adds an implant,
  `install(id, { free })` → Promise `{ ok, reason }` (pays through BridgeProgression.wagerCoins), `remove(slot)`,
  `installed()`, `has(id)`, `tier(slot)`, `bonus()`, `perkLine(perk)`. An interaction `{ type: 'implants', shop: '…' }`
  opens a shop.

### Homes and furniture
`js/home.js` (BridgeHome). Places of your own: **the White House** on Sapphire Port's square (`lumar-home`, `scenes/lumar_home.py`: hearth, two windows on the sea, bare boards; 1500 coins) and a **capsule apartment** over the District's Capsule Hotel (`arcadia-home`, `scenes/arcadia_home.py`: a strip window onto the live skyline and rain — the `'*'` behind hook draws them in `scene.sky` — the sleeping capsule, a kitchenette; 600 coins). **The farmhouse** on Verdance (`verdance-home`, `scenes/verdance_home.py`: Edda's kitchen in daylight — whitewash over green wainscot, the black range, a dresser of blue-and-white plates, two windows on the field, her kitchen table with the ledger) comes with the farm's deed (`HOMES[id].farm`: `owns` asks `BridgeHomestead.owned()`; the FARMHOUSE door goes in once it's yours, and Edda's rocking chair and bed are waiting the first time). Their doors are `home_door` interactions set on the street as worlds load (`HOMES[id].door`: the WHITE HOUSE door at 38,13 on Sapphire; the CAPSULE HOTEL on the street): the first time they offer the place for sale (`wagerCoins`, a menu option `home_buy`), then let you in. Each comes with two pieces to start. Aboard, **your quarters and the bridge** are yours already. The rooms are rendered empty; everything else is what you put in them. The legacy homes (*Legacy* below) are rendered furnished with their architecture and fixed pieces — the Penthouse's inside and roof deck, Ashcombe House's five rooms, the Starling — with open floor left round them for yours: a home can be several worlds (`part: '<home id>'` in HOMES: owned with that home, furnished on its own, no toast of its own), and a world can be a hotel you own (`resort`: owned once the holm's Starling is built).
- **Furniture**: 36 pieces, each built with the scene kits' own builders by `tools/bridge-art/furniture_sprites.py` into one floorless sheet (`assets/scenes/furniture`, a 3 × 4-tile cell each, footprint at the bottom of its cell) plus `assets/furniture.json` (id, name, price, footprint `w` × `d`, `walk` for rugs, the footprint's corner on the sheet). The engine cuts each sprite at its cell's opaque bounds and draws it depth-tested against the room (`drawOccluded` at its front row, tinted by the room's light but softened, since the sheet is lit already); rugs sort under everyone, and a seat you're sitting on draws before you. Non-rug pieces block their tiles. A module can add pieces it draws itself, live (`BridgeHome.add({id, name, price, w, d, spr: {img, sx, sy, sw, sh, ax, ay}, live(entry, now, theme), glow?, use?, after?})`: `live` hands back this frame's picture, drawn through the same `drawOccluded`; `glow` a light laid over it with `lighter`, untinted; `use` its interaction; `after` the piece it's listed beside) — the fish tanks are.
- **Pieces that do something** (`USES`): beds sleep, seats sit (one seat a tile: benches and sofas take two), the wardrobe opens the wardrobe, the arcade cabinet plays Asteroids, the CRT Breakout, the chess table chess, the telescope looks at the stars, the desk opens the journal, the clock tells the real time, the cat may allow a stroke, the lucky cat leaves 5 coins once a day, the bookshelf has a book for you. New piece: an entry in `PIECES` (furniture_sprites.py), re-render the sheet, and optionally a `USES` entry.
- **Furnishing** (H, or the Furnish pill, in a place of yours): the tray docks at the bottom (world-themed: parchment, neon, holo) and the camera frames the room above it (`BridgeWorld.reserveBottom`, `setFocus`). YOURS lists what you own, spare first; SHOP is the whole catalogue (a second tap buys, and the piece comes straight to your hands). Arrows/WASD or the mouse move the cursor (on touch: tap a tile, or the stick), E/click puts the piece down or picks one up, X puts one away, Z/C choose, Tab switches tabs, Esc or H is done. A piece can't go off the floor, on a door or the tiles round it, a spawn, a person, or anything you use, and never where it would cut off part of the floor or something you use (checked by flood fill from where you stand); rugs can go under furniture, not on other rugs.
- Saved in localStorage `bridge_homes`: `owned`, `have` (pieces bought), `layout` (per home: `{id, x, y}`, the footprint's top-left tile; a chest's `items`, a tank's `fish`), `seen`. A piece placed in one home isn't spare for another. `BridgeHome.reload()` (a partner's change coming in) re-applies the room, so its pieces' interactions hold the fresh entries; `BridgeHome.at(id, x, y)` is the entry as saved now.
- **Fish tanks** (`js/aquarium.js`, BridgeAquarium; Beck: "it would be cool to have a fish tank somewhere… and you can put the fish that you catch"): the **Fish Tank** (2 × 1, five fish, 250) and the **Grand Aquarium** (3 × 1, a dozen, 800), in every home's furnishing shop (H, beside the nebula tank) and the Ship's Chandler's *Fish tanks*, put down anywhere of yours, aboard too. Drawn live at native scale (not on the furniture sheet): a glass tank on a two-door cabinet in the room's style (`STYLE` by the home's theme: walnut and brass for parchment, black lacquer with a pink strip in Arcadia, gunmetal with a cyan strip aboard), a hood with the lamp in it, the water seen from above, dithered water lit from the top, waterweed, a red stem plant and a fern swaying (`TANKS[id].plants`), stones and driftwood, gravel with light moving on it, bubbles off an airstone, a reflection on the glass; and the fish — each the real fishart (`BridgeFishArt.canvas`) at a length from the sizes it comes in (`BridgeFishing.size`, a log scale, capped by the tank: `maxL`), swimming back and forth at its own speed and depth, easing off toward the glass and turning round there (squeezed through its own width), the ones further back smaller in the dark water; crabs pick their way along the gravel and shrimp potter near it (`EXTRA`, drawn by hand small and to size up close). Rare and legendary fish glow a little (the `glow` pass: their spec's glow or the tier's colour), and the lamp lights the water through the room's dark. Where a fish is comes from `Date.now()` and its seed (`swim(fish, t)`), so the room, the close-up and a partner's screen agree. **E** at a tank: *Look into the tank* (the room leans in, `fp-lean`, and `#tank-view` fills the screen: whole native pixels scaled by a whole number, the fish 3–4.6× their room size with their names under them in their rarity's colour as crisp text, nudged clear of each other; point at or tap one for its rarity and description; tap the water, Esc or *Step back* to go), *Put a fish in* (any fish with art, crabs and shrimp, from your pack — or from the fish hold of a ship of yours lying at this island, on Lumar: `BridgeFleet.here`), *Take one out* (into your pack; not when your pack's full). A tank keeps its fish on its layout entry (`fish: [{id, s}]`, `s` the seed), saved with `bridge_homes` — so it syncs, it moves with the tank when you pick it up in the furnishing tray, and it has to be emptied before it's put away. A **partner visiting** sees your tanks and their fish (js/partner.js routes them your `bridge_homes`) and can look in and slip a fish in as a present (it goes to your save, live or to the cloud), but not take one out.

### Sit, sleep, look out of the window
`js/actions.js` (BridgeActions) turns interactions into things you do, from `assets/actions.json` (by world id: tag interactions by label — or one at x, y — or `add` new ones where the art has a seat or a window). **sit**: the pilot sits on the `seat` (tile units, the floor point under the seat; `facing`) with the seated sheet (`pilot-sit-<suit>.png`, `characters.py pilot-sit`), energy comes back, moving gets you up; a seat with a `view` opens it too. **sleep** takes a little time (Beck: "you have to get a little bit of health and energy back for every certain amount of time that you sleep… shouldn't ever have to sleep for too long"): the room dims (not black), the bed's caption, bars of health and energy filling as you sleep — from nothing, energy in `FULL_EN` (45) seconds and health in `FULL_HP` (60) in an ordinary bed — and any key or a tap wakes you with what you've got (*You wake after 6s: +10 health, +13 energy*); sleep till both are full and you wake by yourself, rested. A bed's `rest` in actions.json scales the pace (the clinic's recovery bed 2, Okafor's hammock 0.8); the lodge's feather bed (js/lodge.js) is 1.2 and warms you through as you sleep, the farm's stove chair (js/homestead.js) 0.9. `BridgeActions.rest({ text, rate, rested, onTick, woke })` for any other bed; `deny` says why not instead. Test: `world/sleep.js`. **view**: first person — the room leans in toward the window (`#bridge-canvas.fp-lean`), then the view swivels in from that side to fill the screen (`#view-panel`, its canvas shaped to the screen; the window's frame and mullions round it indoors, open air outside, the eyepiece at a telescope; the caption along the bottom), and turns back when you look away. It shows, live, `sea` (the spore sea in the island's palette, its moon road and spores; `roofs` for a window over a town, `beam` for a lighthouse sweeping), `skyline` (Arcadia's layers framed for a window), `giant` (Enigma's ringed giant), `orbit` (the planet the Wayfarer is over), `void`, `stars` (a telescope). The interaction's text becomes the caption; quest clues still count. New seats and windows: add an entry, no re-render needed.

### The arcade games
The cabinets (Asteroids, Breakout, Helicopter, Missile Command, Space Invaders) are built on one shared kit, `js/shared/arcade-kit.js` (`BridgeArcade.run({...})`): a native low-res buffer scaled by a whole number to fit, a fixed 60 Hz step, key-repeat-filtered input and touch buttons, title / pause / game-over screens in a 5×7 pixel font, the palettes (`BridgeArcade.PAL`, with `UI` colours per palette for the panels), small synth sounds, and one score-and-coin path — best, plays and last run per game in localStorage `bridge_scores` (the Arcade's HIGH SCORES boards and the game room's score board read it), coins paid as you go (`api.award`) plus a score bonus at the end, at most 150 a game a day, `first_light` and `played_<id>` trophies. Every game of the Bridge's own (cabinets and apps — chess, Cipher, Signal Relay, the Canvas, Royale Link; not The Aether Seas) opens in the cabinet over the world (`js/cabinet-modal.js`), framed for where you are (Arcadia neon, Enigma terminal, the ship's brass, the Brink, the casino's gold on velvet): a game posts `bridge-run-state` so Esc pauses a run before it leaves, and `bridge-run-ended` so its score comes back as a toast. Played with someone else (a score duel, co-op, Helicopter's tandem), see *Playing games together* under Multiplayer. Each cabinet is its own place, by Beck's call (variety, not all Arcadia): **Breakout** is the candy cabinet (a Lite-Brite peg board, candy-cane walls, jelly bricks, a peppermint ball; ten walls from three short rows to a gingerbread castle, then looping tougher; a sugar rush bends the ball toward the last few bricks), **Spore Invaders** (`space-invaders`) is Lumar's harbour at night (spore creatures in the islands' sea colours, a brass cannon boat, sea stacks for shields, the ghost galleon), **Asteroids** is salvage in Enigma's ring belt on the tug's scanner (the ringed giant, ice, amber-cored rock and striped hull debris, a security drone), **Missile Command** holds the Brink (the Singularity in its accretion disk, crystal spires on the platform's rune-lit rim), and **Helicopter** stays in Arcadia's neon. The Arcade (`scenes/arcadia.py`) lays the classics along the back wall with open floor in front of every cabinet, the two old RUNOUTS tournament cabinets on a lit stage under the sign — Royale Link terminals now (`js/casino.js` relabels them as the Arcade loads; see *Royale Link*).

### The game room
Aboard the Wayfarer, through the bridge's east door (`gameroom`, `scenes/gameroom.py`). Every mini-game you play out in the worlds — the Arcade's cabinets, the Neon Royale's tables and Royale Link terminals (one game, `runouts`, its slot labelled ROYALE LINK), the chess table, Enigma's cipher terminal and signal relay, the canvas at the Brink, NULL's Splice rig, Lucky 7's pachinko — is marked found (`js/games.js`, `bridge_games`) and its slot in the game room swaps a dust sheet for the real thing (scene layers `g_<game>`: `on` / `off`); play it from there (`game_slot` interactions). The score board (`game_scores`) lists what you've found, where, Splice's best times and how many are still out there. A new game: an entry in `GAMES` (games.js) keyed by its app path, and a `g_<key>` variant pair in the scene. The inn has its guest floor upstairs (`stairs_up` / `stairwell_down` in `assets_lumar`), the Market Square the fisher's cottage and the cartographer's house, Enigma's relay deck the hydroponics bay through its east door (`enigma-hydro`, where Okafor lives; `station_room(..., east=(r0, r1))` opens the wall); more back rooms, cellars and rooftops follow (see `WORLDS.md`).

### The Wayfarer grown longer: rooms aft, the galley, crew life aboard
Beck: "the Wayfarer is generally a good idea" (the denser-worlds plan's workstream H): the ship grows with your crew.
- **The passage** (`ship-passage`, `scenes/ship_passage.py`, the kit `tools/bridge-art/assets_wayfarer.py`): through the
  bridge's new door in its south wall (tiles 13–14, east of the quarters'), a long panelled companionway under wall
  lanterns, a runner down it, round brass ports onto the planet between three **module bays**. Each bay is a scene layer
  `bay_<module>`: `sealed` (a riveted iron blank, a stencilled number, a builder's tag, a coil of cable) or `fitted` (a
  panelled door with a light in it, its lamp and nameboard) — js/wayfarer.js sets them as fitted. Also the **watch bill**
  by the bridge door (a HOLDOUT `scene.bill`: js/worlds/wayfarer.js pins each of your crew's faces to it with a lamp —
  green at their post, amber off duty, dark below), the **scuttlebutt** (a dipper once a watch, +10 energy, and whatever
  the crew are saying), fire buckets, lockers, oilskins, and the **hatch** down to the crew's berths, open and lamplit.
- **Modules** (`js/wayfarer.js`, BridgeWayfarer; localStorage `bridge_wayfarer` `{ mods: { id: at } }`): the galley and
  mess (1,800, one crew aboard), the observatory (3,200, two), the greenhouse deck (2,600, two) — bought from the Ship's
  Chandler (*Ship's modules*) or at the bay itself, the chooser showing her as she'd look fitted (`shipArt`: her sprite).
  The yard won't fit a room there's nobody aboard to use (`crew`). `key(mods)` names her sprite as fitted.
- **She's longer wherever she's drawn**: `assets_ship.mothership(…, mods=)` lets a 2.6-tile section in amidships for each
  room, just abaft the mainmast (`MOD_AT`, `MOD_LEN`; `module_section`): gold seams where she was let out; the galley's
  big warm mess windows and its copper stove funnel; the observatory's glass dome with the telescope out of its slot;
  the greenhouse's glasshouse along the deck glowing green. `cine_sprites.py` renders every set (`mothership-galley`,
  `…-galley-observatory-greenhouse`, eight in all). The jump (`BridgeCine.jump`: `SHIP`, and the wing galleon as hers is,
  `opts.wing` a sprite name), the title page (`BridgeCine.ship()`) and the other galleons in every window
  (`BridgeParty.drawShips`: presence `shipk`) draw her as fitted; a fitted set not rendered falls back on her as built.
- **The ship's clock** (`BridgeWayfarer.clock()`): the ship keeps no sun but keeps watches — her day is four real hours
  from ET midnight, read on a 24-hour face of six watches (middle, morning, forenoon, afternoon, dog, first).
- **The galley and mess** (`ship-galley`, `scenes/ship_galley.py`; `js/galley.js`, BridgeGalley): the cook's black range
  in a brick alcove under a copper hood (the stockpot and kettle steam: `scene.steam`), copper pans over the butcher's
  block, the sink under the water tank, sacks and apples, a cat on the warm hearth; the pass down the room; two long
  mess tables, the menu on a blackboard, windows onto the planet, the dresser, the card table under a low lamp (its
  cards are the `cards` layer, out while anyone's playing). **Dorrie Kettle** the cook (`npc-dorrie`, portrait) keeps the
  pass 05:00–21:00 ship's time. **Meals** by the ship's clock — breakfast 06:00 (+25 most energy), dinner 12:00 (+25 most
  health), supper 18:00 (+15 each), one of each a ship's day — leave you **fed** for 90 real minutes (BridgeGear.bonus /
  speed carry it, so the bars show it; a chip under them says what and how long). Give her a fish from your pack and
  it's the next meal's special (+6% speed while it lasts); greens from the greenhouse make a meal last half as long
  again. Broth from the stockpot once a watch (+15 energy), a bowl of stew to take once a ship's day, bread and cheese
  under a cloth at night.
- **Cards in the mess** (`js/eights.js`, BridgeEights): after supper three of the crew are round the card table (by
  turns; Dorrie sits in when there are fewer). Sit in for a hand of **Eights** — match the suit or the number, eights wild
  (call the suit), draw one if nothing goes, first out takes the pot (5 coins each, the house puts in the crew's; or for
  matches). Each plays to their own habits (`STYLE`) and says so. The panel is green baize in an oak rim, the cards drawn
  a pixel at a time (`card`); ←/→ choose, Enter lay, D draw, Esc leave.
- **Crew life** (`js/aboard.js`, BridgeAboard): hired hands keep a day by the ship's clock instead of standing at their
  posts (`day(id)`): asleep below through the middle watch (one keeps the night watch on the bridge by turns), breakfast,
  dinner and supper at the mess tables (below, with no galley), at their post (`crewPosts`), a forenoon errand (the
  watch bill, the cask, the lockers), one off duty in the afternoon by turns (the greenhouse, the observatory), the cards
  or the telescope after supper, below at 23:00. Between stops they walk — out by one room's door, along the passage, in
  by the next, down the hatch — at a steady pace, worked out from the clock and who's aboard alone, so a party guest
  sees them in the same places. Seated at the tables and the cards they use seated sheets (`characters.py`: the crew's
  `-sit` / `-stand` pairs); their talk point goes with them, and people.js knows them aboard. js/crew.js hands the ship's
  rooms to it (`BridgeCrew.decorate`).
- **The observatory** (`ship-observatory`, `scenes/ship_observatory.py`; `js/observatory.js`): the back wall all glass
  under a dome's brass ribs, the great telescope on its round dais raised to it, a star chart under a red-shaded lamp,
  a celestial globe, the orrery, an armillary, almanacs, two chaises, the round rug of the twelve houses. The telescope
  opens the stars (js/actions.js `stars`) with the ship's day's **sight** (twelve: a comet, the Singularity's ring, an
  aether whale…): log each the first time for 40 coins, all twelve for 500; the desk and the globe keep the log; the star
  chart reads every world's weather; the orrery every world's time.
- **The greenhouse deck** (`ship-greenhouse`, `scenes/ship_greenhouse.py`; `js/greenhouse.js`): glazed in green-painted
  iron onto the planet, a brick floor, raised beds under grow-lamps (herbs, salad, tomatoes, strawberries — forage spots
  from the scene's `beds`) and two lemon trees; the potting bench says what's ready; water the beds once a ship's day for
  one more of everything you pick. Picking here gives Dorrie greens for the next meal. Items: `ship_herbs`,
  `salad_greens`, `ship_tomato`, `ship_lemon` (js/items.js) and `strawberry`.
- **Aboard someone else's** (js/party.js): their modules come with `pship` (`wayfarer`), so you see their bays, rooms and
  crew; you can eat at their galley and sit in at their cards; you can't fit their bays.
- Tests: `world/wayfarer.js` — the door aft, the bays sealed, no fitting without crew, the galley fitted from the
  Chandler's list, Dorrie's meals (on the bars, the chip), broth, a fish for the special, the galley dark at night, the
  crew at the cards and a hand of Eights played out for the pot, someone walking up from below to breakfast, the
  observatory (a sight logged, the chart, the orrery, the log) and the greenhouse (watered, picked, greens for the galley).

### Royale Link (the casino games)
The Neon Royale's games are one app, **Royale Link** (`apps/runouts`, served at `/runouts`; its own `CLAUDE.md` has the inside): blackjack, roulette, craps, hold'em, the slots, the derby, crash, and the sixteen Runouts games (back a player to win, finish safe or come last). `js/casino.js` (BridgeCasino) is the Bridge's side of it.
- **Interactions** (type `casino`, registered with `BridgeInteractions.register('casino', …, 'Play')`): `{ "type": "casino", "game": "blackjack", "table": "bj-2", "label": "BLACKJACK", "prompt": "PRESS E TO PLAY" }` — games `blackjack` `roulette` `craps` `poker` `slots` `derby` `crash` `runouts` (Runouts adds `"mode": "<id>"`, one of holdem, plo, rng, wheel, dice, high-card, coin-flips, black-marble, slots, horse-race, rocket, space-invaders, bomb, plinko, battle-royale, stock-market) and `"stakes": "high"` for the high-roller room's tables; `{ "type": "casino", "game": "lobby" }` is a Royale Link terminal. Table ids are free text (`bj-1`, `poker-3`); the app numbers the table and picks its felt, dealer and regulars from it, so two ids are two different tables (a poker table comes with its regulars seated). Using one opens the cabinet in the **casino frame** (gold on velvet, *NEON ROYALE* over the table's name) on `/runouts/?game=<game>&table=<id>[&mode=<id>][&stakes=high]`, a terminal on `/runouts/?link=1` (the lobby; `/runouts/?game=runouts` is the Runouts section). Any cabinet or app opened on a `/runouts` URL gets the casino frame. The Arcade's old RUNOUTS cabinets, the game room's slot, the nav chart's app list and `js/games.js` all point at Royale Link now; playing any table or terminal finds it (BridgeGames `runouts`). Badges and map markers treat `casino` as a game.
- **Coins**: in the cabinet the chips are the pilot's coins. The app asks and BridgeCasino stakes and pays through BridgeProgression (`wagerCoins` / `awardCoins`, reason `casino:<game>`), one message at a time in the order sent, moving the cabinet bar's coins as it goes (`BridgeCabinetModal.coins`): `bridge-casino-hello` → `-wallet { ok, balance, pilot, high }`; `-stake { id, amount, game, hold }` → `-staked { id, ok, balance }`; `-pay { id, amount, game, key, hold }` → `-paid` (once per key); `-hold { hold, game }`. `hold` is what the table owes the pilot if they walk away right now — bets not yet in play come back, a hand in play stands where it is and plays out, a spin or race already under way counts as it will land, a poker stack is the stack. **Closing the cabinet pays the hold** (`frameClosed`, then a toast: up or down for the visit); the app's first Esc in a round only warns. A page that closes with something held leaves it in localStorage `casino_open` (an entry per tab with the pilot's name, refreshed every 10 s; never cloud-synced, so two devices can't both pay it) and the next load pays it back once the entry is 30 s quiet (*Your chips came back*). Nothing is ever paid twice: payouts are keyed, the hold is cleared as it's paid.
- **Figures and the high-roller room**: lifetime staked and won per game in `bridge_casino` (cloud-saved). Poker's net (cash-outs less buy-ins) is `BridgeCasino.pokerWon()`; at `BridgeCasino.HIGH_ROLLER` (1000) the Royale notices you (a toast, once: *The Royale has noticed you — the high-roller room is open*) and `BridgeCasino.unlocked()` turns true — the hello then says `high`, and the app's lobby offers the high-roller room (higher limits: blackjack 100–10,000, hold'em 25/50 with a 1,000–10,000 buy-in, and so on). A `stakes: "high"` table opens at those limits whatever your winnings — put those behind the casino's own door.
- **Playing together** (js/together.js, `royale-<game>`): *Play a game…* lists the tables where you are (nearest of each), or *Royale Link* (a terminal, the game room's slot, or anywhere you've both played there) with its games inside. Blackjack, roulette, craps, the derby, crash and Runouts are *At one table* (*At one counter*): the two of you at the same table against the house — one shoe, one wheel, one shooter (whoever asked runs the table; the other sends their moves and is sent the table), the other's chips on the felt beside yours. Slots is *Side by side*: two machines, the other's spins on the glass beside yours. Hold'em is *Heads up* or *With the regulars* (two NPCs as well): your chips against each other's. There's no stake step — every bet is your own coins through your own cabinet, as when playing alone — and the verdict compares how each of you came out (*You beat the house*, *Alyssa came out ahead*; hold'em counts in your record). If one of you leaves, the other plays on (taking the table over if it was the host who went; at hold'em two regulars sit in). Talk across the table without leaving it: the 💬 pill in the cabinet's bar (js/tablechat.js, under Multiplayer).

### Time: each planet's clock, seasons, day and night, opening hours
Beck: "each place should have its own clock and timing cycle… tied to real hours of the day", with seasons, weather with real stakes and shops that keep hours. The plan is `_claude_docs/spec-living-worlds.md`.
- **The clock** (`js/clock.js`, BridgeClock): one clock for everyone, pinned to US Eastern time (`America/New_York`, so two pilots see the same sky). Each planet's day is a whole number of real hours that divides 24, starting at ET midnight — Sylvara 24 h, Hoarfell 12, Lumar 8 (days start at midnight, 8am and 4pm ET), Verdance 6; Arcadia is always night (busiest 8pm–1am ET, `bustle()`); Enigma, the Singularity and the ship keep no sun. A planet's clock reads hours and minutes into its own day (`time(planet).text`, Lumar `0:00`–`8:00`). **Seasons** on the real calendar, turning on Mondays: Verdance a week each (js/seasons.js now counts days by this clock), Lumar two weeks each, Hoarfell three weeks of winter and one of each of the others, Sylvara the real moon (`moon()`: its full moon is the real full moon; the week round the new moon is *the Dimming*). **Daylight** (`daylight(planet)`, the share of the day the sun is up) moves smoothly through the year: Lumar and Verdance 65% at midsummer, 50% at the equinoxes, 35% at midwinter; Hoarfell's midnight sun (it never sets — "night" is a golden glow) and polar night (~12%); Sylvara a steady 55%, its dusk and dawn spreading over most of the day in the Dimming. `sky(worldId)` gives `sun` (0 night … 1 day: smoothstep of the sun's elevation `cos(2π(t/len − ½)) − cos(πD)`), `dusk` (the warm light at the horizon), the moon, the season, sunrise and sunset and a `part` (dawn, morning, midday, afternoon, dusk, night, midnight sun); dawn and dusk each last about a twelfth of the day. The Midnight Isle never sees the sun. **Previews:** `?at=2026-10-16T21:30` (an ET wall time) and `&timewarp=60`, or `BridgeClock._at(ms | 'ET string', warp)`.
- **Day and night in the art:** a scene rendered for more than one time of day has `cycle` in its scene.json (`{day, dusk, night}` → light state names) and js/scene.js draws it as a blend: night first (with its firelight flicker), dusk over it (weight `dusk × 0.9`), day over that (`sun × (1 − dusk weight)`), only the ones that show; characters' light (`tintAt`) blends the same way; `BridgeScene.sky(id)` / `shownState(id)` for anything drawn from one state (layer images, the map). The sea follows the scene it's round (js/sea.js `DAY` palettes and derived `@dusk` ones, sun sparkle by day, spores only in the dark) — never ahead of the island's art. **Rendering it** (`tools/bridge-art/scenekit.py` `render(…, cycle=…)` or `SCENE_CYCLE=night|day` on any scene script): a scene lit for night (Lumar's moonlight and lanterns) gets a `day` state (a sun, `_day`) and a `dusk` one (a low warm sun, `_dusk`), its own states staying the night ones; a scene lit by day (Verdance, Hoarfell) gets `dusk` and `night` (a moon over the whole place, `_moon`, and the lamps up). The kit tells the sky from the lamps by itself — suns and area lights eight tiles or more across are the sky (`_sky`), everything else a lamp (`_art`) — and a state's `_emit` scales how much glowing parts glow and cast (lamps out by day), `_world` its ambient sky. `scene_post.py` scales the emission per state and writes `cycle` into scene.json. The old `full` / `dim` names stay, so anything that loads `full.png` (the sloop, the chart, treasure maps) still gets the art it always had.
- **Opening hours** (`js/hours.js`, BridgeHours): `HOURS[world][label] = [from, to]` in anchors that move with the sky — `midnight`, `small` (just past it), `dawn`, `sunrise`, `morning`, `noon`, `afternoon`, `sunset`, `evening`, `late` — or fractions of the day (`to` before `from` runs past midnight). The working day is civil: shops keep at least the middle 40% of it, and keep their hours under the midnight sun. js/interactions.js `executeInteraction` asks `BridgeHours.gate(inter)` first: a shut counter says so — the hours on the door in that planet's time and how long till it opens — instead of opening. Lumar's bakery opens at dawn, its smithy and stalls in daylight, its taverns from noon till just past midnight; Verdance's shops likewise on its 6-hour day; Hoarfell's lodge never shuts.
- **The sky by the place's name**: a sun, a moon or half of each (dawn, dusk) beside the location badge, its season on hover (`.world-sky`); the time itself is for a timepiece. On Lumar's open ground the chapels ring the hours (`bell`, a toast that stays out of the journal).
- **The ship's chronometer**: the wireless on the bridge reads every planet's time, part of the day and season (`BridgeHours.chronometer()`).
- **Timepieces** (js/gear.js `TIMES`, sold like suits and packs): carry one that keeps a planet's clock and its time shows beside the sky icon there (`BridgeGear.keepsTime(planet)`; Arcadia's, and anywhere with no sun, is Eastern time). The Fell Pocket Watch (Hoarfell, the ski hire's outerwear rail), the Farmer's Watch (Verdance, Percy's *A pocket watch?*), the Moonglass (Sylvara, Mirelle's *A moonglass?*), the **World Watch** (every world: Cyber Gear on Arcadia's street, Rare Imports on Sapphire). None on Lumar: the chapels ring the hours.
- **The world watch** (`BridgeHours.watch()`): with any timepiece the chip by the place's name can be tapped — a panel under it with each world you keep the time of (all of them with the World Watch): its time, part of the day, season and weather now and next, and Eastern time. It ticks while open; a click anywhere else closes it.
- Test: `node tools/tests/run.js world` (`world/clock.js`).

### The trees through the year
`js/foliage.js` (BridgeFoliage; Beck: "all of the trees need more detail and more leaves… fall colors, and have them change… evergreens in some places"). Every outdoor scene on a planet with seasons (Lumar, Verdance) has the render's leaf pass, and as it loads (world.js calls `apply(worldId)`) its leaves are recoloured for that planet's season (`BridgeClock.season`; Verdance's from js/seasons.js) — a grade (`BridgeScene.setGrade`), worked out once, never per frame: **autumn** turns each tree its own way (gold, orange, red, russet, yellow — `AUTUMN`, by the tree's id), more of them as the season goes on (`frac`); **winter** on Lumar strips the leafy trees to twigs with a few dead leaves hanging on, on Verdance lays snow on their lit clumps over the twigs and on the firs' tier tops; **spring** freshens the green and puts blossom (pink, pale pink, white) on the lit clumps of some trees; **summer** is the render. Evergreens keep their green all year. Thresholds come from each scene's own leaves (`pct` of their lightness), so the lit clumps take the snow and the blossom whatever the light. A grade decides on the day image and carries each state's own light over it (`ref`, `BridgeScene.relight`: the new day colour times that state's light over the day's, channel by channel), so dusk and night turn with the day — Verdance's meadow grade does the same. Evergreens were mixed in: firs among the oaks round the farm and Brambleford, pines and yews in Crownhaven's gardens and on its cliff path. **The ground through the year** (Beck: "apply the seasons to all of the places in those worlds"): where the render marks its grass (the leaf pass, R 64), `ground()` regrades it on every outdoor place on Lumar and Verdance — spring freshens it and sprinkles wildflowers (daisy, buttercup, campion, violet); autumn turns it toward straw (Verdance's meadow gold) and drops each turned tree's leaves round its foot in that tree's own colour (`fallMap`: the turned crowns by 8-px cells, spread downward from the crown to its foot); winter leaves Lumar's grass dormant, olive and tan with brown litter under the leafy trees, and lays snow on Verdance's (and a cold light on everything else there). A render without grass marks on Verdance falls back to js/seasons.js's `meadow` (everything green turns). `BridgeScene.relight` carries a decided colour into another state by brightness and a damped share of each channel (a pure per-channel ratio blew snow to saturated blue under the moon). Test: `world/foliage.js` (each season, screenshots). `BridgeFoliage._force(season)` previews one.

### The outdoors alive
`js/nature.js` (BridgeNature; Beck: "cool little animations… especially the nature… very nice and pretty"). Read from the render's leaf pass as composited with the decor (`sc.leaves`, js/scene.js; worked out again whenever the images change, `sc.rev`), drawn through the `'*'` scene hooks in outdoor worlds: **wind** — each crown (the run of pixels with one tree code) leans its upper half a pixel downwind, two in a gale, as gusts run east across the map, never over anything nearer the eye (depth); gusts ripple over the grass as a wavy band leaning its lit blade tips a pixel and catching the light; the grass flicks up round your feet as you walk through it; strength from the weather (`WIND`: a breeze, a gale, a storm). **Water** — sun or moon glints on ponds, streams and fountains, rings where a fish rises (many in the rain). **Life** on Lumar and Verdance, by season, time of day and weather: butterflies over the flowers on fine days in spring and summer, dragonflies over water in summer, fireflies at dusk and night in summer, leaves falling from the turned trees in autumn and petals from the blossom in spring (the tree's own colours, as graded), a flock now and then with its shadow by day — gulls on Lumar, swallows on Verdance. The farm's hand-placed pond glints and butterflies and Verdance's screen-wide drift of leaves step aside where it covers them (`covers(sceneId)`). Costs a couple of milliseconds a frame (`_stats(sceneId).ms`). Test: `world/nature.js` (frames that move, each season, a flock, a summer night, nothing indoors).

### Small happenings (js/events.js)
`js/events.js` (BridgeEvents; Beck: small events on the hour, "a lot of fun"; the plan is `_claude_docs/spec-denser-worlds.md`). Between the festivals, small things happen in a place for a few minutes, and everyone online gets the same one at the same moment.
- **The schedule**: each place (a *stream*: a world, or a few sharing a sky — `arcadia-sky` is every Arcadia world with the skyline, `hoarfell-slopes` Fellhaven, the pistes, the Park and the summit) has a slot every twenty minutes (`SLOT`). Most slots one thing happens (`CHANCE`, 0.7 unless the stream says otherwise — quieter on Enigma and in the sky), picked by a hash of the stream and the slot from what fits then (`when(ms, E)`, asked at the slot's middle: the hour by the planet's clock through `skyAt` — the same sun as `BridgeClock.sky`, but for any moment — and the weather through `BridgeWeather.at`), weighted by `w`, at a minute in the slot the same hash gives. So the schedule is just the slots read ahead (`_schedule(worldId, hours)`), the same on every page. Nothing happens in a place while a festival's on there (`BridgeFestivals.on`).
- **Noticing one**: a sound as it starts (`sfx`, if you're in earshot — `hear`), then now and then while you're near (`loop`); a word overheard in a bubble over whoever said it (`say`: `[s, cast index or 'at', text]`), and as it starts — or as you walk in on it — a line about it (`hint`). Bubbles are DOM (`#ev-layer`, `.ev-bubble`; `.hint` is the dark italic one), placed from `BridgeWorld.getScreenView()` each frame, stacked so none sits on another, and held at the screen's edge with an arrow when the thing's off it. People gather when crowds are about (`gather` → `BridgeCrowd.gather(worldId, { x, y, r, n, until, face, id })`, and `react` → `BridgeCrowd.react`; both skipped while there's no BridgeCrowd). The journal logs each once, quietly (`BridgeQuests.note('Happening', …)`).
- **What happens** (`EV`):
  - *Lumar.* **A trader in** on Crownhaven quay: a sea trader's stall (blue-and-white awning, a chest, cloth, a lamp) and the trader beside it; three rare things a happening (`TRADER_GOODS`, a little over their worth — charms, a star pearl, moonglass, a telescope plan…), one of each to a customer. **A wedding** on St Brendan's steps (by day): the bride and groom (`npc-bride`, `npc-groom`) with Mother Coralie, petals falling, the bells pealing; wish them well for a slice of `wedding_cake`. **Toy boats** in the fountain: three children (`npc-kid-a`, `npc-kid-b`) and three boats round the basin; back one for 5 coins at the FOUNTAIN, triple if it wins (the same winner for everyone). **A fiddler** by the Market Square's fountain (not in the rain): a reel every few seconds, notes rising; tip him 5 for +20 energy. **A fight out of the tavern** (after dark): a sailor and a dockhand shoving, dust and the odd star; break it up (an ale for your trouble) or put 10 on one of them (20 back if he wins). **The catch auctioned** on every fishing harbour's quay as the boats come in at dusk (*Harbours that work*): a lot a minute chalked on the board; bid and the lot's yours at its price — unless the smokehouse man goes higher (by your own hash, so the odds fall with the fish's worth).
  - *Arcadia.* **A star on the Royale's carpet**: they walk up from the limo with an escort, flashbulbs going off in the crowd behind the ropes; ask for an `autograph`. **A drone loses its parcel**: it limps in from the east, drops a parcel on the lane (falling, then a thud) and wobbles off; the parcel's loot, once a happening for each pilot (`PARCELS`). **A brownout** rolls down the street block by block, each going dark with a whine and stuttering back. **A chase in the sky lanes** (every skyline world): a magenta car and two police cruisers, light bars flashing, across the sky three times (the `behind` hook, so they pass behind the buildings), sirens, red and blue washing over the street. **NULL takes every screen** in the city for a minute (`screens()` → `'null'`, read by js/skyline.js for the towers' screens and the street's own), a green scan over everything; the journal keeps what it said.
  - *Verdance.* **A hen loose** in Brambleford (by day, dry): she dashes and pecks round the square and the green (a searched way between `HEN_WAY`'s points, so never through a wall), a child and a farmer after her; get close and catch her for two eggs and 10 coins. **The mail coach**: in over the footbridge, a stop on the green, off to the east, the post horn as it comes and goes; ask for post — a letter (`LETTERS`, into the journal) and now and then a parcel. **A hot-air balloon** drifting over (fine days), its shadow on the ground. **Kites** over the green on a breezy day. **A rainbow** after the rain.
  - *Hoarfell.* **Avalanche blasting** of a morning: three booms, the view kicked, puffs of snow on the slopes at the top of the place. **The ski patrol** bringing someone down on the rescue sled to the lodge. **A sleigh** through Fellhaven of an evening, bells and a lamp. **A snowball fight** on the green: join in and for forty seconds the children turn on you — a ring closes on the snow where one's coming, so step off it — and E throws back at whoever's nearest; hit more than you took for a hot cocoa. **The aurora**: everyone out in the street looking up on a clear night, and Tove pouring free cocoa.
  - *Sylvara.* **The lantern procession** at dusk: five elves (`npc-elf-folk-a…d`) down from the Great Tree with leaf lanterns, to stand round the Moonwell; walk with them for a `leaf_lantern`. **The spirit stag** crossing the glade by night or in the spirit mist: pale and glowing, motes trailing; it stops for you if you come near, and lowering its head fills your health and energy. **Thaelin plays**, elves sitting to listen; listen and the verse goes in the journal (+25 energy).
  - *Enigma* (empty but for you): **the lights going down the ring** one after another, **a pressure door** that hisses open a hand's width by itself and shuts again (its own words while it's on), **a voice on the comms** at the antenna array (what it says, `VOICES`, goes in the journal).
- **An event** is data plus a few hooks — `stream`/`worlds`, `len` (minutes), `w`, `when`, `at`, `hear`, `cast` (people from their sheets: standing `{ sheet, x, y, f }`, walking a `path` at `speed` tiles a second from `from` s, a `shove`, carrying a `lantern`, pulling a `sled`), `props` (`ART` sprites with `solid` tiles, solid only while it's on and never on top of you), `talk` (interactions of type `event` at a tile, or following someone with `at(L)`), `take` (an existing interaction's label, taken over while it's on and given back after), `gather`, `react`, `sfx`, `loop`, `say`, `hint`, `note`, and `begin` / `tick` / `end` / `behind` / `ground` / `items` / `over` of its own, each called with the live happening L (`L.t` ms into it, `L.key` the happening's id). What a pilot got from one is kept once per happening (`bridge_events.got`). The art is drawn on the 16-unit grid as rects (`sprite`, mirrored for facing left), stood in the scene through `BridgeScene.drawOccluded` and lit by the room (`glow` things aren't: the stag, lanterns), people from their own sheets with the walk frames. Sounds are synthesised in js/audio.js (`fiddle`, `harp`, `peal`, `chime`, `gavel`, `flash`, `drone`, `thud`, `powerdown`, `glitch`, `siren`, `cluck`, `horn`, `jingle`, `blast`, `whistle`, `splat`, `buzz`, `crackle`). New sheets for them in `characters.py`: `npc-kid-a`, `npc-kid-b` (human children, `young`), `npc-bride` (a white gown and veil, a crown of white flowers), `npc-groom`, and Elderglade's folk `npc-elf-folk-a` … `d`.
- Test hooks: `BridgeEvents._force(id, s)` starts one now (`s` seconds into it) wherever it belongs, `_clear()`, `_schedule(worldId, hours)`, `_running()`, `_check()` (every cast spot, walk, talk point and prop on open ground; the hen's, the coach's and the stag's ways clear). Test: `world/events.js`.

### Legacy: the long goals
`js/legacy.js` (BridgeLegacy; spec §8): what a fortune is for, listed in the ledger's **Legacy** section with where to go for each — ten businesses, an enterprise, **a statue in Crownhaven** (250,000: commissioned at Captain Mira's statue, `handle()` asked by executeInteraction; you're cast in verdigris bronze as you looked that day, on a plinth beside hers, drawn through the `'*'` items hook), **the Sovereign** (300,000: a legendary ship at the Royal Yard, js/fleet.js — the galleon's lines, quicker and handier, a 400 hold, six guns), and the Neon Royale's high-roller room (2,000,000, a business). **The Penthouse** and **Ashcombe House** are homes in js/home.js like the others — furnished with H, visited by partners — and the **Starling** is your hotel; Beck: "make the Ashcombe House feel super bougie and gorgeous… the penthouse has like an outdoor deck area… that's where the pool is too… more space… make sure it's all pretty customizable."
- **The Penthouse** (250,000; `scenes/arcadia_penthouse.py` + `assets_penthouse.py`, 36 × 19): the whole top floor of Skyport 7's tower, its brass door a kiosk on the roof pad (arcadia_skypad.py), the lift in its front wall. A **backdrop world** (`scene.backdrop` + a `skyline` block): js/skyline.js draws the city behind everything — through the glass and past the balustrade — and its rain falls on the deck but not indoors (`skyline.rain.dry`: rects in world tiles left dry, js/skyline.js `rain`).
