Inside, on black marble veined in gold, glass to the ceiling along the back (holdout): the platform bed facing the city with a stone egg of a bath beside it, a bathroom glimpsed behind its vanity wall (twin basins, round mirrors lit from behind, a rain shower), a velvet sectional facing the glass, a white grand piano, the bar against the core (a back bar of lit bottles under HALCYON in neon, its stools), the kitchen (black units, a waterfall island under glass pendants), a marble dining table with velvet tub chairs. Through a wall of sliding glass the **roof deck** (teak): the plunge pool sunk into it glowing cyan, its infinity edge toward the towers; three loungers; a drinks counter; a fire pit in a ring of cushions; planters of grasses, clipped box and an olive tree; festoon lights strung corner to corner; a glass balustrade (the back panes are holdouts, the front ones drawn as glass over the drop by js/estate.js). The deck is furnishable like the rest; loungers and the fire pit are seats, the bath and the pool rest you (`action: 'sleep'` with a `rest` pace).
- **Ashcombe House** (400,000; `scenes/lumar_manor*.py` + `assets_estate.py` + `scenes/_manor.py`; its door the tall slate house beside the Crown & Anchor, (45, 29) on lumar-crown): **the great hall** (`lumar-manor`, 26 × 16 — cabochon marble in a black border, sage damask over cream panelling, the grand staircase with its runner, brass rods and balusters climbing to the gallery, a marble chimneypiece with its garniture under a glass and the last admiral's portrait, tall sash windows in swagged drapes, pier tables with flowers under portraits, a grandfather clock, marble statuary and busts, torchères, a Persian rug, the great chandelier over a drum table of flowers) and off it, **places within places**: west, **the drawing room** (`lumar-manor-drawing`: chevron parquet, old-gold silk, settees round a tea table laid for two, a grand piano and a harp, a vitrine of porcelain, a chaise under the window); east, **the library** (`lumar-manor-library`: bookcases floor to cornice with the ladder on its rail, a window seat and a telescope, a library table of atlases under green lamps, leather wing chairs and a chesterfield, the globe, the first admiral's ship in her case); through the double doors at the back, **the dining room** (`lumar-manor-dining`: a long table laid for twelve with candelabra and epergnes of roses, carvers at the ends, two chandeliers, a sideboard of silver under a marine painting, china cabinets, a cellarette, a drinks trolley); up the stairs, **the master bedroom** (`lumar-manor-bedroom`: a four-poster with an open tester and curtains at the posts, a dressing table with a triple glass, a small fire with a wing chair, a roll-top bath on claw feet behind a painted screen, a chaise under the window). Each room is a home of its own in js/home.js (`part: 'lumar-manor'`: owned with the house, furnished separately). The rooms render a **day** light state as well as night (`_manor.render`: `full` / `dim` lamplight and moonlight, `day` sun through the windows with the lamps out; it writes `cycle` into render.json), and their window glass is a hole: js/estate.js paints Lumar's sky now behind it (`scene.windows`) — blue with cloud by day, gold at dusk, stars and the moon at night, over the harbour's horizon and masts — with dust turning in the light by day.
- **The Starling** (150,000): built from the holm's ledger (`resort`, once the dock's rebuilt), it replaces the old boathouse (lumar_holm.py's `resort` variant: old | built) and its door (js/holm.js, reached along the strip of sand in front, which holm.js makes walkable once it's built) leads **inside** (`lumar-holm-resort`, `scenes/lumar_holm_resort.py`, 24 × 15): whitewashed boards, the front desk (bell, guest book, keys in their pigeonholes) — the business's own interaction, `holm-resort` in businesses.json is in this world now — a racetrack cocktail bar in rattan round a tower of lit bottles, rattan sofa and chairs and a peacock chair, palms, ceiling fans, and through a colonnade of white voile the terrace over the sea (limestone, a white balustrade with hurricane lamps, café tables, bougainvillea, a telescope). It's a **backdrop world** on the water's edge: a lawn and hedges round the building, rocks at the shore, and past the terrace the tileset's sea where the render is empty (`scene.backdrop`, `view` reaching past the walls); `scene.sea` marks the water for js/estate.js, which flies gulls over it by day. Its staff stand at their posts as you hire them (scene actors with a `role`: `desk`, `bar`, `floor` — the first, second and third person you take on) and guests sit about the place while it's running (`guest`): js/holm.js filters them as the world loads. It's yours to furnish too (`resort` in js/home.js HOMES). It runs as a venue (`built: 'holm'`, granted by `BridgeBusiness.grant`).
Tests: `world/legacy.js`, `world/homes.js` (buys each, walks every room and the deck, furnishes each with the tray, the doors both ways, the Starling's staff, screenshots by night and day).

### Weather: shared, forecast, with stakes
`js/weather.js` (BridgeWeather; spec §4). Each place's weather comes in spells of real hours on the ET clock (Lumar and Verdance 3 h, Hoarfell and Arcadia 4, Sylvara 6), each drawn by a hash of the place and the spell, weighted by its season (`REGIONS`), sometimes carrying on from the last (`keep`) — the same for every pilot, so the forecast is just the next spells. A spell eases in over its first 25 minutes and out over its last. `at(region, ms)`, `here(worldId)`, `forecast(region, n)`, `outlook(region)`; `_pin(region, kind)` holds one for tests and previews.
- **Lumar** fair · breezy · rain · fog · storm · gale (calm summers, autumn storms, winter fog and gales). At sea (`sea()`, read by js/sloop.js): squalls and fog banks come thicker or thinner; a storm turns every sea into the Crimson's (squalls that knock you flat if you carry sail, lightning); a gale blows harder and gustier; fog closes in round the boat and hides the islands, with the Sapphire light's beam sweeping through it (`drawSea`). Pirates keep to port in a storm (`pirates()`, js/seafight.js); the rare fish bite in rain and storm (`fishLuck()`, js/fishing.js).
- **Verdance** fair · cloudy · rain · storm · heat wave · snow (winter). Rain and snow water the field; a storm flattens half the crops grown past 40%, an hour into it (js/farm.js asks `stormsBetween`): they stop growing until staked up (E, 1 energy; Tam does it on his round); a heat wave dries a watering out twice as fast.
- **Hoarfell** clear · fair · snow · blizzard (an aurora over a clear night). A blizzard's cold bites through a Thermal Suit (at 0.6) and twice as hard without (js/worlds/hoarfell.js asks `cold()`), and drains a climber 50% faster (`climb()`); the lodge and huts are shelter.
- **Sylvara** clear · spirit mist · starfall · rain (more mist and stars in the Dimming). A starfall night drops up to six fallen stars by you (loot, `fallen_star`, 150; Mirelle buys them).
- **Arcadia** clear · rain · smog · blackout. A blackout leaves only a torch's worth of light round you; the Neon Royale runs on generators.
- **Business** (`trade()`, js/business.js): open-air trade falls to 45% in a storm, blizzard or blackout, less in rain or fog; indoor places gain up to 12% as people come in out of it.
- **Seen and heard**: outdoors (`BridgeClock.outdoor`, which now includes Arcadia's streets) through the `'*'` scene hook: rain as Look Book 2 drew it (`rain`: every drop somewhere of its own at a speed of its own, slanting a little, a pixel wide and six long, one to 700 px of screen, anchored to the world; a three-pixel crown where one lands and then a small ring spreading flat, none on water — the water rings; the light greyed and dimmed under it by a multiply, as the Look Book's grade — a scene in live light greys its own, and the drops take its light's colour; before, the drops sat on a lattice at five speeds and fell in lines), a storm with lightning and thunder, snow, a whiteout, fog round you, mist and wisps, smog, a blackout, a heat haze, the aurora, shooting stars; the weather's beds join the place's ambience (`amb`, js/audio.js).
- **Forecast**: an icon by the place's name with the outlook on hover (`.world-wx`), a word on arriving in it (once a spell; toasts stay out of the journal), the weather board in every harbourmaster's book, and the ship's chronometer.
- Test: `node tools/tests/run.js world` (`world/weather.js`: the mix over a fortnight, the effects, a storm on the field, a screenshot of each kind).

### Sound
`js/audio.js` (BridgeAudio): everything is synthesised with Web Audio — no sound files. **Music** is generated as it plays from a theme (`THEMES`: tempo, root, mode, chord degrees per bar, and a band — pad, arpeggio, bass, an improvised lead built from chord tones in A A' B A phrases, percussion), through a shared reverb and echo; every eight bars the band thins or fills. `themeFor(worldId)` picks the theme and the **ambience** beds (`BEDS`: sea, wind, rain, city, hum, engine, creak, fire, murmur, crowd, bleeps, beeps, drips, rumble, breeze, birds, snowwind, gale, chimes, slots, chips): Lumar outdoors folk over the sea, Lumar indoors warm by the fire, Arcadia streets synthwave in the rain, arcades chiptune, back rooms lo-fi, the Neon Royale smoky lounge jazz (`casino`: a walking bass — `bass.walk` — brushes and the ride, keys comping, a synth sax) over the crowd and the slot floor's chimes and payouts (`slots`), the Card Room over chips clacking (`chips`), Enigma drones and sonar, the Singularity deep and glassy, Verdance a folk tune over birdsong, Hoarfell bells over the snow wind, the Wayfarer a music box over the engine, caves and towers their own. Themes and beds cross-fade on every world change (`setWorld`, called from world.js). **Effects**: `BridgeAudio.sfx(name)` (doors, dialogs, clues, quests, coins, pickups, footsteps, sitting, sleeping, fishing, Splice, pachinko; fighting: a whoosh and a blow per weapon class, `swing_<class>` / `impact_<class>`, made from `whoosh()` — noise through a band sliding from one pitch to another — plus `roll`, `dodge`, `charge`, `charged`). The ♪ pill (or M) cycles sound on → effects only → off, saved in `bridge_audio`. Audio starts on the first tap or key.

### Lighting
A world opts in with a `lighting` block in its JSON (see the header of `js/lighting.js` for every field): an `ambient` colour, `fromGlow` (reuse the world's `tileGlow` tiles as lights, with `skip` for things like glowing sea), `tileLights` (a light on every tile of a type — lanterns, fireplaces, lava), hand-placed `lights`, a faint `pilot` light and `bloom`. Keep lights where the art shows a source, warm for fire and lamps, coloured for magic and neon. Lighting is subtle on the ship and stations and moody at night in Lumar.

### Ground under objects
Lumar object tiles (NPCs, lanterns, crates, stalls) paint the world's ground under themselves via `drawGround()`. A world chooses it with `"groundTile"` in its JSON (e.g. `28` cobblestone in the Market Square). New object drawers should call `drawGround`, not a specific floor.

## Multiplayer (playing together)

Tests: `node tools/tests/run.js multi together` (two pilots on a stand-in for Supabase; see tools/tests/README.md).
Beck and Alyssa play the same game at the same time: their own progress, the same worlds. Built on the Supabase project the game already uses — `docs/supabase/2026-10-16-multiplayer.sql` (run once) adds the tables; seeing each other needs none (Realtime). Everything degrades to single-player in the browser when the tables or the connection aren't there. Ownership is **separate but open to each other**: each keeps their own homes, farm, island and fleet; partners can visit and help in each other's anytime.

- **Cloud save** (`js/cloud.js`, BridgeCloud; table `pilot_saves`): every `bridge_*` localStorage key (all but `SKIP`: who's signed in, sound, screen state, the sync's bookkeeping; the hotbar has its own `pilot_inventory` row) as one jsonb document per pilot with a save counter `rev`. On sign-in (it wraps `BridgeState.setPilot`) the row is fetched: newer than this device's last sync → written into localStorage and the page reloads once (guarded by `sessionStorage.bridge_cloud_reload`); otherwise this device pushes. Pushes every 10 s when something changed and when the tab hides. The browser remembers whose game it holds (`bridge_owner`): signing in as someone else on the same browser saves the last pilot's changes to their row, clears the game (hotbar and world position too) and brings yours. A device's first sync with progress of its own keeps whichever game is bigger; anything replaced is kept locally in `bridge_cloud_backup`. `BridgeCloud.peek(id)` / `.write(id, key, value)` read and write another pilot's save (visiting a partner's places). Local testing: `tools/tests/fakesupa.py` / `fakesupa.js` stand in for Supabase (a tiny table store and a Realtime relay) — the real project isn't reachable from the build container.
- **The wire** (`js/net.js`, BridgeNet): one Realtime channel, `bridge-lobby`, joined when a pilot signs in (it wraps `BridgeState.setPilot` too). Presence (keyed by pilot id) is where everyone is and what they're doing (`set({ key, world, inst, place, act, sea, visiting })`, sent at most once a second, only when it changes); everything else is one broadcast event `m` carrying `{ e, d, from, to }` — `send(e, d[, to])`, `on(e, fn(d, from))`, plus `'peers'` / `'join'` / `'leave'` / `'up'`. Nothing at all happens without a client or a joined channel. **Keeping it honest** (after Beck and Alyssa saw each other go "offline", or stuck somewhere they'd left, and then vanish): every meta carries `t` (when it last changed) and `away` (the tab is hidden); with two tabs open the one in front wins; every pilot also broadcasts a heartbeat `_hb` with their meta every 8 s, so anyone heard from in the last 20 s is online even if presence lost them, and the freshest meta (presence or heartbeat) says where they are; a presence update that fails or times out is sent again, and presence is re-sent every 25 s regardless; a channel that isn't joined, or a tab back after 15 s asleep (a phone in a pocket), is torn down and rebuilt. js/multi.js keeps an avatar while its position messages (tagged with this place) keep coming, dropping it only 3 s after presence says they've gone elsewhere, and a reconnect within 90 s doesn't toast *Online* again. **A message budget** (after Beck kept "going invisible"): Supabase counts every message against the project and a burst over its limit drops messages or the channel; so each game sends at most ~12 a second — frequent state (`LOW`: `pos`, `sea`, `helm`, `fs`, `sf`, live `g-score`s) waits its turn when over and only the latest of each goes (`later`, flushed every 150 ms), anything else (a hit, a trade, a final score) always goes; positions go out at ~7 a second, fight and sea-fight snapshots at ~7. **Away**: someone whose tab is in the background sends no steps but stays where they last stood, their tag marked *· away* (presence `away`), and only goes when presence says they've gone elsewhere or offline. `BridgeNet.debug()` shows the channel's state, who's heard, and `stats` (sent, deferred, rebuilds, the channel's last statuses and system messages) — ask for it from the console when someone vanishes. `tools/tests/fakesupa.js` can fail tracks (`__fakeSupa.faults.failTracks`), drop presence (`dropPresence()`) and kill the channel (`breakChannel()`) to test all this.
- **Seeing each other** (`js/multi.js`, BridgeMulti): other pilots in the same place are drawn into the scene like you (a `'*'` scene hook: their suit sheet, walking or idle or seated, depth-tested and lit by the room), with a name over their head and speech / emote bubbles (DOM in `#mp-layer`, placed from `BridgeWorld.getScreenView()`). Positions go out as `pos` at up to 10 Hz while you move, every 2 s standing still, and only while someone else is in the same place (`hi` on arriving makes them answer at once); the receiver eases each avatar toward the latest position (snapping past 4 tiles). Each avatar is also a `player` interaction (added to `world.interactions` as they move: *E — Meet Alyssa*) opening a menu — wave, send a heart, say something (a bubble over your head; from somewhere else it arrives as a note) — that other modules extend with `BridgeMulti.addMenu(fn(id, peer))`. The **players pill** (`#mp-pill`, under Chart, while anyone else is online) shows who and where; its panel lists everyone with **Go to** (asks them `where`, loads their world and steps you onto a free tile beside them; not at sea, not while you're sailing). Personal places (`PERSONAL`: the Wayfarer's rooms, your homes, your island) are one copy per owner — `inst(worldId)` is the world plus whose it is — so two pilots each in their own cottage don't see each other until one visits the other's. Someone skiing or climbing is drawn in that pose, not walking (`pos` `p`, `posed`; see *Racing*).
- **Out sailing** you've left the island: `myInst()` is nothing at sea, so nobody ashore sees you standing on the pier you cast off from (they see *at sea, bound for …* on the pill), and the pill, its panel, letters, gifts and notes come up over the passage (`html.at-sea` raises them above the sloop's canvas).
- **Giving, trading, letters** (`js/share.js`, BridgeShare). *Give…* (walk up to them): pick from your pack (or coins), how many; it leaves your pack at once, held for them (`bridge_gift_pending`), and they're asked *Take it / No thanks*; declined, no room (`BridgeInventory.freeSlots`), offline, or still held when the page closed → it comes back. *Trade…*: once they say yes both get `#trade-panel` (your pack, what you put in, what they put in, coins); any change unreadies both; both ready → the asker holds their side and sends `trade-do`, the other checks both sides still match and theirs is all there, swaps and answers `trade-done`, then the asker takes theirs in (`trade-fail` puts it all back). *Write a letter* (their menu, or the players pill): to anyone you've played alongside (`bridge_known_pilots`), online or not, with something tucked inside — a `pilot_mail` row; delivered as a card the next time they're in a world (on joining, on a `mail` ping, every five minutes), the gift into their pack, the letter into their journal's log. Pop-ups from someone else wait until you're not mid-conversation (`whenFree`).
- **Partners and each other's places** (`js/partner.js`, BridgePartner). *Be partners* from someone's menu; they say yes and each keeps the other in `bridge_partners`. A partner's places — their Starling Holm and its cottage, their farm on Verdance, their homes (`PLACES`; the Wayfarer is open only to your party: see *Playing together as a party*) — are listed on their card (*Alyssa's places*, from the players pill, online or not), and *Go to* takes you into theirs when they're in one. A visit routes the localStorage keys of the modules that run those places (`KEYS`: `bridge_homes`, `bridge_holm`, `bridge_farm`, `bridge_homestead`, `bridge_animals` — it patches `Storage.prototype.getItem/setItem` for just those) to a copy of the owner's (fetched from them with `pget` if they're online, else their cloud save) and each module re-reads (`reload()` on home, holm, farm, homestead, animals). Your writes go back to the owner: live (`pset`; they apply and their own cloud sync saves it) or, when they're offline, straight into their `pilot_saves` row (`BridgeCloud.write`). Their changes while you're there come to you the same way. The visit ends when you leave that place's worlds (not while their world is still loading in: `arriving`); your own state comes back. While visiting, `inst()` is their copy of a personal world, so you see each other there. On Verdance (a public world) *Tend Alyssa's farm* / *Back to your own farm* switches whose farm you're working. Theirs stays theirs: their island's ledger and strongbox are closed to you; what you harvest or pick up lands in your pack; what you plant, water and build is theirs; you can look into their fish tanks and slip a fish in, not take one out (js/aquarium.js). `js/cloud.js` snapshots raw storage (`window.__lsGet`) so a visit never leaks into your own save.
- **Sailing together** (`js/sloop.js`). On an island you can sail from, someone's menu has *Sail together*: `sail-ask` → they *Come aboard* (`sail-yes`) → you cast off with them as crew (`start(w, null, { crewWith })`), and once the harbour's laid out `sail-go` (your island, any charted course, the wind's `from` and `seed`, your hull and suit) starts their passage as crew (`start(from, to, { crew })`). Both of you sail the same water — the chart at the same scale from the same island, what's out there seeded by position, the weather by `wind.seed`, position and the clock — and a course goes to the crew as `course` (their gold mark), so they get your sea, islands, rocks, flotsam and squalls; `helm` (your boat's state and phase, 10 Hz) drives their boat (`crewUpdate`: no physics, no fights, no damage, no steering, no moor/landing of their own), `haul` marks flotsam taken, and `sail-end` (your `stop()`) brings them ashore where you land. Each sees the other aboard in their own suit (`drawPilot`/`drawHelmsman` take a suit; the mate sits forward of the helm); the crew can fish over the side (their catch to their pack), chat and emote, open the chart, or row back ashore; their status reads *Crewing — Beck has the helm*. If the captain goes quiet or offline, the crew brings her in. **Other boats at sea:** anyone sailing while someone else is sends `sea` (their chart position — `chartPos()` — and chart heading, 4 Hz); the receiver maps it onto their own water through the same chart frame (`fromChart`, inverse of `chartPos`) and draws the boat (their hull, their suit at the helm) with their name over it.
- **Fighting at sea together** (`js/seafight.js`): sailing together, the fight is the captain's and both of you are in it. The captain's game runs it and sends the crew its state ten times a second (`sf`: each foe's id, kind, place, heading, speed, health and state; her hull; the shots fired since), what's said (`sf-say`), the hail (`sf-hail`: *Black sails… Beck answers them*), each hit on her (`sf-shake`) and the prize (`sf-prize`). The crew's game draws it (`apply`, then its own frames in `follow`: foes eased to where the captain has them, shots played out), shows her hull, and gives the crew **the swivel**: F (a FIRE button on touch, `#sf-fire`) sends `sf-fire` and the captain's game fires it at that ship (`SWIVEL`: 5 a ball, 7 tiles, every 1.6 s; the HUD says FIRE / OUT OF RANGE / RELOADING). With a mate aboard: *Fight* is open even in a boat with no guns, your guns reload a quarter quicker, you're two fighters stronger when boarded, and a ship sunk pays the crew too (their share, the same coins; it counts toward the Crown's *Crimson Seam*).
- **Found together** (`js/multi.js`): a quest clue you find with someone within six tiles is sent to them (`clue`) and counts in their journal too (*Found together*).
- **Fighting together** (`js/fight.js`, BridgeFight). With someone else in the same place the creatures are shared: one game runs them — the **host**, whoever's been there longest (presence `since`, stamped as you arrive) and has the game in front of them (`away` hands it over) — and the others follow. The host's game moves them and sends who's where (`fs`: every creature's id, tile, health, maximum, kind, flags — engaged, frozen, burning — and facing; ten a second while anything changes, and on request `fs?` from someone arriving), each wind-up as it starts (`fw`: kind, target tiles, how long) and one broken off (`fc`). interactions.js `updateHostiles` picks each creature's target as the nearest of everyone on their feet (`BridgeFight.targets`), never pauses for one player's dialog, and on a follower's game only plays out strikes it was sent; whether a strike lands is always checked against your own tile (`updateHostileAttack` uses BridgeCharacter, not the target), so dodging is the same for both. Creatures have ids (`fid`, kind and first tile, from `BridgeCombat.spawn`, the same in everyone's copy). A hit on a creature someone else runs shows at once (items.js `strike`: the flash, the number, your drinking blade's heal) and goes to the host to settle (`fh`, the weapon's traits and where you stood — `onHit` knocks back and blinks away from you, `w.remote`). **Seeing each other fight** (js/arms.js, js/multi.js): every swing, charge and roll you start goes to the others in the same place as one message, `atk` (`{ k: 's' | 'c' | 'r' | 'x', c: class, n: which swing of the chain (-1 charged), f: facing, w: weapon, s: its speed }` — a one-off, not a LOW message, so it's never held back by the budget), and their game plays it out on your pilot from its own clock (`BridgeArms.remote` / `remoteTick`: the attack frames, the weapon in hand, the arc of the swing on its landing frame); `pos` carries `h`, the weapon you're carrying, so they see it in your hand as you walk. The blows themselves still go the usual way (`strike` → `fh`; the knock back's `push` and the class ride along). Whoever lands the killing blow gets the coins and drops; the host announces it (`fd`) and anyone within nine tiles gets their own share (`killHostile(…, { quiet })` takes it away for anyone further off); the Bell Warden's first-time staff and daily reset are each player's own. The Warden rings louder for company: half again its health for each extra fighter (`BridgeCombat.engage`). **Down, not out**: at no health with someone fighting beside you, you're down for 30 s instead of blacking out — slumped where you fell (the seated sheet), the creatures leaving you be, a banner counting down; their prompt on you becomes *Help up* (`BridgeMulti.promptFor` / `direct`, a red DOWN on your name tag) and `up` puts you back on your feet with a third of your health. Nobody's there to help, or everyone's down, and you black out as ever. A partner who's hurt shows a health bar under their name (presence `hp`, a percentage; orange and pulsing under a third).
- **Down the mine together** (`js/mine.js`): a floor is one of three layouts, so its instance is the layout plus the dig (`BridgeMine.party()`: floor and seed). Climbing down (or taking the lift) while someone's on the floor with you asks them *Follow them down* (`delve`, with your seed): following uses the same seed, so the rocks, the ladder and the creatures are the same. A swing at a rock is sent (`rk`) and breaks it for both (the drops are the breaker's; the great songstone sings for everyone); arriving late, you ask what's been broken (`rk?` → `rks`). *Go to* someone in the mine follows their dig too.
- **Playing together as a party** (`js/party.js`, BridgeParty; Beck: "send somebody an invite to play together… so then every time you go to travel somewhere, you need to wait for that person to come to the boats or your ship or shuttle… and then that would solve the issue of coming to each other's Wayfarers"). *Invite to play together* from someone's menu (or their card on the players pill); they say yes (`party-ask` → `party-yes`) and you're a party, kept in `bridge_party` (it lasts a reload and a night away). More than two is fine: as someone joins, the one who asked tells everyone who's in it (`party-roster`); joining another party leaves your old one (`party-leave`). The players pill's dot goes gold while anyone in your party is on (`#mp-pill.party`); its panel has *Your party* — who, where, online or not, *play a game* with them (BridgeTogether.pick) — and *Leave the party*; offline party members stay on the pill like partners (`BridgeMulti.friends`).
  - **Travel waits for each other.** Whoever sets off asks the others first — `BridgeParty.depart(spec, go)` (true while it's waiting; false and you just go, with nobody in your party on): the jump from the nav chart (js/starmap.js `jump`, `kind: 'jump'`), the shuttle down from the hangar and *Return to ship* (js/ship.js `launch` / `returnToShip`, `down` / `up`), casting off (js/sloop.js `board`, `sail`, after the ship's chosen), the mine's ladder and lift (js/mine.js `goFloor`, `mine`, with the dig's seed). Each is called (`pcall`); the departure waits on a ready check (`#party-check`, under the toasts: *Waiting for Alyssa…*, each one asking / on the way / ready / not this time, *Go now* or *Go without them*, *Call it off* or Esc; the leader's controls held meanwhile). Thirty seconds without an answer counts as no (`ASK_MS`), someone on their way who isn't ready in forty-five is left behind (`ARRIVE_MS`); once nobody's still to answer or arrive it goes (`pgo` to the ready, `poff` to anyone left) and `go(readyIds)` runs.
  - **Called** (`#party-call`, with a bar for the time left; E/Enter come, Esc not this time): it waits out a conversation, a trade, a game in the cabinet, a flight (`free`); not at sea or mid-jump (answered *can't come*, with why). Come, and when it goes (`pgo`, after `clearHands`: a dialog, a mode, the nav chart): **a jump** takes your own Wayfarer too — you get the same cutscene with the other galleon keeping station off your bow quarter, further off (js/cinematic.js `wing`: `farShip`, the sprite shrunk once with a hard edge and a little haze, in the spool-up, the leap a beat ahead, the drop-out, the title and the shuttle shot), then down to the pad a tile from them (js/ship.js `descend(world, { beside: true })`), or, if they stay aboard, onto their bridge; **the shuttle down** lands you beside them from wherever you are (`BridgeShip.rideDown`); **going up** takes you aboard their Wayfarer, in their hangar; **casting off** brings you to their pier first (you're *ready* once you're there, `pready`) and you answer `sail-yes`, so js/sloop.js casts off with you as crew (the sloop takes one mate); **the mine** takes you to their floor and dig (`BridgeMine.goFloor(f, seed)`; the old *Follow them down* is skipped for your party). Your ship always comes with you (`BridgeOrbit.setLocation`). *Stay behind* on the line that says you're setting off with them (`#party-wait`) drops out.
  - **Aboard each other's Wayfarer** — open to your party, not to partners as such: js/partner.js has the ship's rooms as one place (`SHIP`: bridge, quarters, game room, hangar, office) whose furniture, chests and tanks go through its lens (`bridge_homes`) like a home's, and lets party members in (`allowed`, `trusted`). What you see aboard is theirs: their decor (`BridgeProgression.getDecor`), their crew at their posts (js/crew.js `decorate(…, over)`), their game room (js/games.js `layers(over)`) — asked of them as you come aboard (`pship?` → `pship`, the view kept while that visit lasts). Their office's books stay theirs (js/worlds/office.js asks `BridgeParty.guestOf()`). *Go to* someone aboard their ship works for your party.
  - **In orbit together**: presence carries where your galleon's parked (`ship`), and every window aboard shows the others' galleons over the same world — and, aboard theirs, your own (`BridgeParty.drawShips`, called from js/orbit.js `draw`: the cutscene sprite shrunk to the window with a hard edge, drifting, its engines and a masthead lamp).
  - Test: `multi/mp_party.js` (the invite; up to the ship together and aboard his; a jump together, landing a tile apart; *not this time*; casting off together from the square; down the mine to the same dig; leaving).
- **Playing games together** (`js/together.js`, BridgeTogether). Every game in the worlds can be played with someone. Walk up to them (or open their card on the players pill) and pick *Play a game…*: the list is the games in the place you're in, nearest first (a station marked *right here* when you're at it), then any game you've both found (`found`, carried in presence, so the game room's games work anywhere you're together). Then how to play it and a stake: *Just for fun* or 10 / 25 / 50 / 100 coins each. They're asked (`g-ask`, through `whenFree`, so it waits while they're in a conversation or a game); *Play* stakes theirs (`g-yes`), then yours goes in and the seed is drawn (`g-go`) and you're both taken into it. Asked from the players pill while they're somewhere else, they *Go and play*: `BridgeMulti.travel` brings them to your world and tile first. Asks lapse after 45 s; one game at a time.
  - **Stakes and pay.** Both stakes go in before anything starts (`BridgeProgression.wagerCoins`). Against each other the pot (two stakes; three at a dice table with a regular) goes to the winner and a tie gives each their stake back; co-op you stake together against the house, which pays each of you double if you make the target. Each client pays itself (`awardCoins`) when both results are in: trust-based, it's two people who like each other. If the other one vanishes mid-game (presence gone for 6 s, or nothing heard for 45 s) your stake comes back, unless they'd already finished, in which case it settles with their score when you finish. A stake still on the table when the page closed comes back the next time you load (`bridge_together.stake`); your own final score clears it, so leaving after you've finished can't undo a loss. `bridge_together.rec` keeps your record against each partner (won, lost, drawn, co-op made of tried), shown on the invite and the verdict.
  - **Modes** (`MODES`): *Score duel* (`duel`: the same game at the same time, the best score takes it), *Co-op* (`team`: your scores added up against a target, the game's `base` or 0.6 of your two bests added together if that's more), *Race* (`race`: the same board, first to finish), and the games' own: *Tandem*, *A game of chess*, *Four hands*, *Decode together*, *At the table*, *At one table* / *Side by side* / *Heads up* / *With the regulars* (the Neon Royale's), *Dance-off* / *In step*, *Race the tray* / *Beat the house*, *Signal duel* / *Two operators*. A game reports with `BridgeTogether.score(n, label)` as it goes and `.done(n, label)` at the end, or `.result('win' | 'lose' | 'draw' | 'house')` when it decides for itself; it talks to the other copy with `.send(k, d)` / `.on(k, fn)` (`g-data`).
  - **The cabinets** (`kind: 'cabinet'`): the game opens in the cabinet (`js/cabinet-modal.js` with `together: true`) at its URL plus `?together=<json>` (the session: seed, mode, names, side, stake, target), which `js/shared/together-frame.js` (BridgeTogetherFrame) reads inside the game and carries messages for (`bridge-together-ready / score / result / data` out, `start / peer / data / verdict / gone` in). The cabinet's bar shows the two of you live (`setVersus`: *You 4,210 · Alyssa 2,950 · pot 50*, or *Together 13,400 / 12,000*), then the verdict. Closing the cabinet mid-game counts what you'd scored (a game of chess is given up). **Arcade cabinets** (the kit, `js/shared/arcade-kit.js`): the title waits for the other pilot, counts 3-2-1 and starts both runs at once with `Math.random` seeded the same, so the same rocks, walls and waves; the score goes out as it changes, the game-over panel waits for theirs and shows the verdict; one run counts, then the cabinet plays on as normal. **Helicopter** keeps its cave on a random stream of its own (`cr`), so both pilots fly the same cave whatever the sparks do, and the two craft swap where they are twelve times a second (`heli`): in a duel your rival flies beside you as a ghost; *Tandem* is one cave for two (the cyan copter is your partner, chips taken by either are gone for both) — crash and you relaunch beside them a couple of seconds later while they're still flying, and the run ends when you're both down; tandem never pauses. **Chess** (`apps/chess`, mode `net` in WizardChess3D): whoever asked plays Ivory, each board shows its own side at the bottom, moves go over as they're made (`mv`, played on the other board by `applyRemoteMove`, queued behind any animation), no undo, no AI rewards; mate or a draw is seen by both; Esc twice gives the game up. **Cipher** (`apps/cipher-room`): an intercept of the session's own (a date picked by the seed, never today's), the clock starting for both; a race, the other's progress under your grid; or decode together, one grid where every letter either of you types lands on the other's (`cell`) and their cursor shows amber on yours (`cur`). **Signal Relay** (`apps/typist`): the same messages in the same order and one 60-second window after a 3-2-1, the other's WPM beside yours. **Royale Link** (`royale-<game>`, the casino frame): both cabinets open the same table (`opts`: table, stakes, Runouts mode) — see *Royale Link* above; no stakes here, each pilot's bets are their own coins, and the score each reports is their net at the table (*UP 120*, a round in play counted as it stood before it).
  - **The dice** (`js/lodge.js`, Fell Dice at the lodge and Bones at the Crown & Anchor): real pilots take the seats — heads up, or with a regular in the third (`rivalFor`: Sigrun, Cutter Joss). Whoever asked runs the table: seat 0 is theirs, 1 the other pilot's; the host rolls, plays the regular and judges every call, and after each change sends the table (`dice`: their own cup only until the cups come up); the other pilot sends bids and calls back (`dact`). Each sees the others' cup counts and bids live, every cup on a call, the log in their own words (*You bid…* / *Beck bids…*); the next round starts by itself after a reveal (or on *Next round*). Out of dice, you watch it play out; walk away and your cup's out (the host's table runs on without its panel until it's done). The last cup standing takes the pot; a regular taking it, the house keeps it.
  - **The dance** (`js/dance.js`): each dances on their own device, side by side on the floor (whoever asked in the middle, the other a tile to the right, each seeing the other's pilot turn to their steps), the day's chart at the level chosen when asking, your panel on the left and theirs on the right, fed by their judged notes as they make them (`dance`: when their steps start, then each hit or miss) and drawn on their own clock. A dance-off goes to the higher score; *In step* adds them up against `BridgeDance.target` (1.1 × a perfect run). No coins or bests from it, like the keyboard dance-off.
  - **Pachinko** (`js/pachinko.js`): the same machine for both, a match tray of 50 balls from the house each (your box isn't touched); what the board pays goes into a match cup instead of back into the tray, and when your tray's fired and the board has settled the cup's your score. The other pilot's cup shows under yours. *Race the tray*: the most balls won; *Beat the house*: win back more than the hundred you fired between you.
  - **Splice** (`js/splice.js`): the board from the session's seed. *Race*: 6×6, each on your own copy, first spliced takes it (the score's the time left on the trace), the other's progress under the board. *Four hands*: 7×7, one board for two — every quarter turn goes to the other copy (`splice`; turns add up the same in any order, so the copies agree) and their cursor shows pink on yours.
  - **Races** (`js/race.js`, kind `race`: `race-ski`, `race-climb`, `race-sail`): offered where you stand — the pistes, the face, any Lumar pier — the run, route or course picked before the stake (`BridgeRace.choose`), both of you at the start, the gun when both are in it (`rdy`), the quicker time takes it. See *Racing* under Lumar's sailing above.
  - **Watching.** Presence carries `play` (the game, the mode, who with, both scores, the verdict), so anyone else in the same place sees a badge over the players' heads (one between them when they're playing each other: *▶ Breakout Beck 812 – 640 Alyssa*); their menu has *Watch Beck play Breakout*, a small live card under the players pill. *Play a game…* is greyed while someone's busy, and *Stop playing* in your partner's menu ends a game you're in.
  - **Talking at the table** (`js/tablechat.js`, BridgeTableChat; Beck: "send them messages while you're at that table still"). While a game covers the world — the cabinet (Royale Link's tables, the arcade, chess…), the dice, the dance, pachinko, Splice (`gameOpen`) — and there's someone to talk to (`recipients`: the pilot in your play-together session while it isn't settled, else everyone in the same place), a 💬 pill shows: in the cabinet's bar (`.cm-right`, clear of the game's own buttons; on a phone with the score showing the bar's title makes room), else bottom-left (`.tc-pill`, z 320, above the cabinet's 300). Open it for the last lines (15 min, 30 at most), quick lines (*GG*, *Nice hand!*, *One more?*, 😂…) and a line to type (90 characters); the box and what they say pop beside the pill, below it up top, above it down below (`place`, kept on screen). Messages are multi.js's own `say` (`{ t, i, tc: 1 }`), sent only to those people; multi.js's `say` handler asks `BridgeTableChat.heardHere(from, t)` first, and while the chat shows (and it's from one of them) the words go into the box instead of a bubble you couldn't see — a pop for 6 s and an unread count on the pill. Keys typed in the box never reach the game (a window-capture handler, registered before the cabinet's and ahead of the in-page games' document-capture ones, stops keydown, keyup and keypress from inside `#tchat`); Enter sends, Esc closes the box only (and hands the cabinet's iframe its focus back). `/` opens it over a game drawn in this page; in the cabinet the iframe has the keys, so it's the pill. Leave the game and it goes; words go back to bubbles. Tested with two pilots at a Royale blackjack table (desktop and a 390-wide phone) and over Fell Dice.
  - Testing: the `tools/tests/fakesupa` stand-ins carry the Bridge's own channel; Royale Link has no server of its own: two pilots' tables talk through the Bridge's channel, so the same stand-ins carry them. Chess's modules come from CDNs that the build container can't reach; tests serve the npm registry's copies of chess.js, three and cm-chessboard instead.

## Don't

- Don't ship a new place in the old look (`SCENE_STYLE=dither`), as a flat board, or without live light if it has a day.
- Don't put a pond in a field as a circle: water sits in the land, with banks.
- Don't make new AI-painted PNG art for the bridge (the PNG rules are legacy).
- Don't ask the image model for "isometric," "diagonal rotation," "realistic perspective," "side view," or "pure top-down roof-only view." High-oblique elevated front-facing only.
- Don't generate PNGs at 64×64 native — image models render small badly. Generate 700-1200px and let chunkify handle resolution.
- Don't paint window interiors in PNGs. Yellow rectangle only — we overlay glow.
- Don't use sub-tile detail in procedural drawers. Whole `u` units only for foreground subjects.
- Don't load or stretch full-size PNGs at runtime — convert them to native sprites with the tool.
- Don't count frames for timing — use elapsed time.
- Don't draw text into the world buffer; text belongs in the screen-space layer.
- Don't introduce a new color palette without adding it to this doc first.
- Don't mix view angles in the same world — every building must use the same high-oblique camera.
- Don't generate art with these prompts for any other Becknology app (aether-seas, cipher-room, etc.). These rules apply to the **bridge app only**.

## Reference: what good looks like

- **The reference dioramas** in `tools/bridge-art/dioramas/` (Millpond Farm, Lantern Quay, the Hoarfell tarn, Elderglade Brook, Rainline Street) — the bar for a place: shaped land, layers of vegetation, the richer house kit, small life.
- **Look Book 2** (`_claude_docs/lookbook2/`, published as an artifact) — the toy finish with soft light, live light through the day and the seasons, the water, the faces: what Beck picked.
- **The toy finish** as `scene_post.py` writes it, and live as `js/litscene.js` composes it.
- Older references (still right for what they cover): the **procedural lantern** `drawLantern` in `js/worlds/lumar.js` (limited palette, hard edges, a 1-px outline) and the **animated sea bands** `drawSeaShared`.

If a future asset doesn't match these, fix the asset.
